[What a port number is not](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) exists because the construct produced two misreadings of the same family within a day --- a reusable-interface-with-fan-out reading, then a port-number-as-part-identity reading, both of which propagated into assessments before being caught. **The design was stable before its durable statement was.** A future reader who takes locked-frame clause 2 without that section is likely to make the third.

### What is not locked, and must not ride in on this

**The lock is on shape. Everything below is calibration inside the shape, and every one of them is genuinely open.**

| Not locked | Why it stays out |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-12's second bet, part (b)** --- Room/Area/Feature as **levels in the part-of ladder**: rooms nesting in rooms, Area as a bigger Object, Feature as part-of-Room | ***The* exclusion locked-frame clause 3 exists alongside, and the one most likely to be read as included.** Added 2026-08-09 with the clause. AB-12's Room/Feature bet is two separable claims: **(a)** hosts and nodes unify --- a host is a node in its own graph --- and **(b)** Room/Area/Feature join the composition ladder. **Locked-frame clause 3 locks (a) and only (a).** (b) is where the **provenance seam** lives: the room boundary is an authored asset-stack merge, not a play-time graph mutation, and [the concepts entry's warning](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) about claims *earned for object interiors and aspirational above the room* is a warning about (b) --- so it survives the lock **intact and still needed**. **A uniform graph interior is not a uniform containment ladder**, and AB-12's own instruction (*do not let the second ride in on the first*) applies to this boundary too. **Status of (b) as of 2026-08-10, since "open" is now too coarse:** *Feature-as-part-of-Room* is in active play at **AB-30**; *Area-as-bigger-Object* is untouched (and note `Area -> Room` containment already exists, authored); *rooms-nesting-in-rooms* is **parked** --- schema-representable, modeller- and UI-unsupported, with improvisation **redirected** to mint a Feature rather than prohibited. See AB-12 for the gate and its unpark trigger |
| **Which things have a `positionGraph` at all** | Locked-frame clause 3 is **conditional** --- *whatever kind of host it belongs to* --- so it constrains graphs that exist and mints none. **AB-30**'s wall-as-edge candidate stays live, and `EphemeraMembershipHostId`'s `OBJECT# / AREA# later` roadmap stays a roadmap. **A reader concluding from locked-frame clause 3 that Features are hosts has read an inventory claim into a structure claim** |
| **What relation joins a root to its members** | Locked-frame clause 3 makes the root **available** as a relation endpoint and says nothing about what attaches to it. `HostRelationalEdgeKind` is `'On' \| 'Under' \| 'Against' \| 'Custom'` --- there is **no containment kind at all** --- so the vocabulary the clause needs does not exist yet. That is **AB-48**, which the lock makes urgent rather than answers, and it is the closest thing locked-frame clause 3 has to a precondition |
| **[PQ-4](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)** --- port identity and reuse | AB-21's identity obligation one layer down; unchosen between monotonic allocation and keying by what is bound |
| **PQ-9** --- numbered vs named, declared vs derived, **and the separator** | The separator is the one with teeth: per PQ-10, `:` is silently swallowed by every id predicate in the system while a second `#` throws loudly. A shape lock says nothing about which |
| **PQ-13** --- does a plain object carry a presence port | The uniformity argument is good and the ceremony objection survives; deciding it by "well, ports are locked now" would be exactly the momentum this plan warned against. **Re-weighted 2026-08-07 by [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine): still open, but no longer calibration.** If a lantern is present by membership node and a rope by port, the level at which an object switches mechanisms *is* the whole/part line C11 dissolved --- so this is the **base case locked clause 1 rests on**, and it should be decided before anything downstream. **Axis reading, 2026-08-07:** positioning and apprehension are indifferent, mutation charges a small cost, and **reasoning wants one mechanism rather than two** --- which leans yes on a stronger basis than the ceremony objection opposes it. ~~A lean, not a decision~~ **Superseded 2026-08-09: forced yes by entailment, not by the balance of argument** --- see [the whole/part dissolution](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). This row named its own decider (*the level at which an object switches mechanisms **is** the whole/part line C11 dissolved*) and then went on weighing uniformity against ceremony; there is no category *plain object* to switch from |
| **PQ-8** --- where sibling links' licensing labels live | Ports do not help here and may make discovery worse |
| **PQ-12** --- must relocation follow adjacency | Widens AB-26 onto the write side; belongs to that row |
| **PQ-1's record split** --- ***the least locked part of ports*** | Marked settled **provisionally, after one reversal on the day it was made**, and its answer rests on the ingress list living in `positionCache` --- which is **AB-35**, unresolved. If the cache's derivation story comes out differently this reverses again. See [The port record](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06) |
| **PQ-10** --- the matcher fix | An implementation obligation the lock creates, not a design question it answers. Must land *in* the change that introduces ports, never after |

**The single-use property ([PQ-1's basis](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06)) and the scale-boundary reading ([each side stores its own kind](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06)) sit between the two lists.** They are not shape claims, but nothing currently open depends on their being wrong, and both deflated questions rather than adding them. **Treat them as locked-with-a-lower-bar:** re-openable on a case, not merely on a preference. **The bar has now been met once, 2026-08-10: [C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c22-the-ships-wheel-and-the-lighthouse) re-opens single-use as PQ-14.** **Re-opened is not withdrawn** --- the arguments for single-use stand and nothing has replaced it --- but the property is no longer *settled with a low bar*, it is an open question with a case attached. **The scale-boundary reading is untouched** by this and keeps its previous standing. **Worth noting how the bar behaved:** it was met by a **scalability** objection reachable under ordinary fiction, not by a representability failure --- which is a class this list did not anticipate when it wrote *"nothing currently open depends on their being wrong."* Something now does.

### Consequences accepted by locking, recorded so they are owned rather than rediscovered

**AB-6's option space changes rather than narrowing.** [PQ-2](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) found that if a whole's ports enumerate its presence, presence is stored **in the whole** --- a carrier neither of AB-6's two framings (a stored member set, or a predicate derived over graph edges) contemplated. Locking puts a *third* option on that row's table, and it is the one that puts presence and member sets in the same carrier. The row still needs splitting; the split now has three candidates on the presence half, not two.

**AB-25 stays deflated, permanently rather than provisionally.** Sibling links living inside the whole's graph gave AB-26 a home *below* the rooms, which removed most of what Area was being summoned to do above them. That deflation is now a property of the frame. **PQ-8 is the acknowledged loose end in it** --- and it becomes a loose end in something fixed rather than in something still moving, which raises what it costs to be wrong about. Note AB-25 does **not** close: it remains H1's only live falsification trigger, and its character-as-interior-node widening is untouched by anything here.

**The thumb on the clause-1b scale must stay legible.** Ports were designed and priced under "assume multi-host for the moment," and [the motivation section](#the-motivation-stated-plainly-2026-08-06) states plainly that the assumption is not neutral --- ports answered C7's where-along objection without single-hosting, which is one of the four pressures on 1b. **Locking does not add a fifth pressure and must not be read as adding one.** The risk is not that the argument is wrong; it is that a locked-in frame stops looking like an argument at all. Whoever decides 1b should re-read that section knowing ports are fixed, and weigh the pressure at exactly the weight it had before the lock --- no more.

### What the lock delivered for scale-invariance, and where it stops (assessed 2026-08-06)

**The question asked:** [Clean fractal, dirty implementation](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) recorded that the corpus does not fully deliver scale-invariance, and the first pass read it as **more conceptual than practical**. Re-assessed after the lock. **It is now practical below the room boundary and still conceptual above it**, and the two halves should not be reported as one verdict.

**What became practical:**

| Gain | Why it is practical rather than conceptual |
| --- | --- |
| **The recursion is in the type** | Before, a room's graph was a *shard* and an object was a *node* --- different kinds of thing, so a level-crossing walk changed representation as it went. Locked clause 1 makes a whole a graph **and** a node in another graph. `EphemeraPositionGraph` is the single recursive type; "the same relation at every level" stops being a claim about the model and becomes a property of the data |
| **The recursion is composable, not merely repeated** | A fractal with no re-entry construct cannot be traversed. [PQ-5](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) answers yes to chaining and yes to a port being addressable as a part, so a rope in a box in a room resolves by the same hop taken twice --- with `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has` as the cycle-guard precedent |
| **Invariance across *time*, not only across levels** | ***The largest gain, and it was not what the lock was bought for.*** A thing can change scale --- decompose, reabsorb --- without any external reference knowing what scale it was. AB-17 and AB-31 were failing precisely because external code was bound to a decomposition state. Level-uniform *reading* is the weaker property; this is the stronger one |
| **The clean/dirty discipline gained teeth** | It was a rule with no mechanism behind it, so nothing stopped a shortcut becoming a model change. Coarsening supplies the mechanism --- and in doing so exposed that the discipline was missing a category, now added as **scale-relative truth** |

**Where it stops. Three residues, none of them softened by the lock:**

1. **The fractal is invariant below the room and something else above it.** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is `Room | Character`. The lock says *objects* are ported graphs; it says nothing about rooms, and **AB-25**'s Room/Area containment is a structure of different provenance --- authored asset-stack merge rather than play-time graph mutation. A walk upward from a battery still changes representational regime at the room boundary. **AB-25's character-as-interior-node widening sharpens this rather than softening it:** characters are hosts, an LCA of a room and a character is needed, and nothing says a character is a ported graph. **This is the next boundary that would have to be locked for the fractal claim to be general.** **Partially softened the same day by [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system):** a moonbase computer system ladders *above* human scale while remaining an `OBJECT#`, which suggests the fractal extends upward without Room/Area needing to be ported at all. **Read this residue with that case beside it** --- the room/area *provenance* problem is untouched, but "the fractal stops at the room" is stated more absolutely here than the corpus now supports.
2. **AB-13's DAG is untouched**, exactly as the clean/dirty table already classifies it. Chaining plus multiple parents makes traversal an unambiguous DAG walk rather than a tree recursion --- workable, and a permanent qualifier on the word *fractal*.
3. **Write-side scale change has no vocabulary at all.** One identity changing scale is now clean; one identity **becoming two** is undefined, and `divide`/`merge` are [handed to the ladder](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) and blocked on **AB-31**.

**And the base case is not decided.** [PQ-13](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) is deliberately outside the lock, and it *is* the practical invariance test: does a lantern run the same code with zero hops? The uniformity argument there is the one that rescued [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) --- *a loop that sometimes iterates zero times is one mechanism* --- so it is likely to land, but until it does the bottom of the ladder is as open as the top.

**The risk the lock introduces, recorded so it is watched rather than discovered.** Encapsulation means external code cannot see what scale a thing is at --- which is exactly what invariance requires, and also exactly what would let a real level asymmetry go unremarked. **The room/area seam is now less visible, not less real.** The failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained. Residue 1 is that reader's warning, and it should survive into `AGENT.concepts.md` whenever this graduates. **Discharged 2026-08-07** --- it travels with the [concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) as a required warning rather than waiting for Phase 6, since the entry is exactly what would otherwise be read as a uniformity claim.

### What would re-open a locked clause

Not a preference, not a cheaper-looking alternative, and not a rival proposal that happens to also work. **A case in the corpus that either clause cannot represent** --- the standard [C7 met](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) --- or a demonstration that locked-frame clause 2's encapsulation claim fails where it was bought, which means an interior repartitioning that still breaks external references. Either goes in [Discussion record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#discussion-record) with the case attached, and the lock is lifted by name rather than eroded by exception.

### Locks need a dependent set too (2026-08-09)

**An existing discipline pointed at a construct it was never aimed at.** [The tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) exists so a hypothesis's dependent rows are **known rather than rediscovered** --- `H1-dependent`, `H3-dependent`, `P4-i1-dependent`. **Locks have no equivalent, and locks are what have actually been invalidating rows.**

**The evidence, measured 2026-08-09 rather than asserted.** A single session's work across roughly eight rows found **six already-decided-but-unrecognized**: AB-42's *room-or-whole* fork, AB-32's non-discriminating decider, AB-4's record option, AB-4's endpoint-address horn, PQ-13's *lean not a decision*, and AB-33's entire framing. **Three or four of those were invalidated by a locked clause on the day it landed** and sat live for days afterward. That is not a tail of stragglers; it is most of what was examined.

**Why the asymmetry existed, and why it should not survive.** Tagging was built to make **rollback** cheap, and a lock is not expected to roll back --- so the machinery was never pointed here. But the dependent set answers the opposite question just as well: not *what do I revert if this falls* but ***what do I re-check now that this stands***. Same enumeration, opposite use, and the second use is the one that has been costing time.

**The mechanic, and it differs from hypothesis tagging in one deliberate way.** A hypothesis tags **the rows**, because when it dies you need to find them. **A lock should enumerate at the lock** --- one list, written where and when the knowledge exists, rather than N edits to rows the author is not currently reading. Rows move; a locked clause does not. **The resolved rows already point back**, so the pointer only needs to exist in one direction.

**Do it at lock time, because the [tagging test's own warning](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) applies here verbatim:** *"tagging retroactively does not work --- the reasoning that shows which one it is exists while the answer is being written and is gone afterwards."* That warning explains why locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 have no recoverable dependent set today and why today's sweep had to be done by reading every row that used a word.

**Locked-frame clause 3's dependent set, enumerated at lock time --- the first application.**

| Row | What locked-frame clause 3 does to it | State |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-33** | Resolves it --- host-as-node, uniform across host kinds | Closed same day |
| **AB-12** | Splits its second bet: (a) locked, **(b) excluded and still open** | Updated same day |
| **AB-48** | Creates it --- the root needs a containment kind to be an endpoint of | Opened and closed same day |
| **AB-2** | Node and host stop being alternatives; *projection* was already AB-9's | **Closed 2026-08-09** --- and AB-30's *addressable but not a member* found surviving as a fourth answer the row never offered |
| **AB-3** | Discharges the root-node trigger --- five independent arrivals in the end | **Closed 2026-08-09** --- `CarryClosureFragment` turned out to be the precedent rather than the casualty |
| **AB-30** | Gives a wall's relation-target requirement a root to attach to | **Re-checked 2026-08-10** --- re-framed toward wall-as-`PartOf`; the edge candidate turned out unable to satisfy C5, which the row had never tested it against. Still open, gated on H3 clause 6 |
| **AB-42** | *Room or the whole* collapses to one slot | Corrected same day |
| **AB-13** | DAG multi-parenthood is across graphs, not multiple roots within one --- confirms single-root | Consistent, no change |

**Locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 get a sweep rather than a set**, since their reasoning is gone: the honest recovery is to re-read rows against them once, record what falls out, and not pretend a retroactive list is the same artifact. **That work has a name and a home: the *parts-and-ports back-sweep*, scheduled as a [Phase 3](#recommended-order) sub-step.** **Named on the lock's section rather than on its clause numbers deliberately** --- `clause 1` denotes at least three unrelated things in this plan (locked frame, H3, P2), and *[Locked frame: parts and ports](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06)* is a unique heading, so the name survives a cold read where *"clauses 1 and 2"* would not. ***Back*-sweep, not sweep**, to distinguish retroactive recovery from the forward dependent-set discipline this section otherwise describes.

**Two failure modes this does not catch, named so the mechanic is not oversold.** (i) **A proposal that retracts its own option mid-write-up** --- AB-4 cited P2's endpoint-address horn, which [P2 withdrew in its next section](#where-p2-clause-3-risks-recreating-the-very-defect-it-fixes); rows cite proposals by summary, so the summary outlives the retraction. (ii) **A row malformed at birth** --- AB-33 was never invalidated by anything, it simply had no requirements twin and argued entirely on cost-of-change. **Neither is a staleness problem, and a dependent set would not have found either.**

## The whole/part dissolution and its unswept consequences (2026-08-09)

**Not a new decision --- an entailment of one already locked, recorded because a sweep found it had not propagated.** [H3 clause 2](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) establishes that whole and part are **roles relative to a level, not kinds**, on [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s four-level chain; the [graduated concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) carries it durably with the consequence attached --- *any rule that gives parts and wholes different behaviour is not a rule at all, since it assigns two behaviours to one object*; and [AB-0](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s lock cites the same finding as the name's payload, *nothing distinctive at any level to type*. **Three independent statements of it, and it still did not reach four rows.**

### The entailment, stated once so rows can point at it rather than re-derive it

**"Whole" is not a term of art and denotes no category.** It is a contextual position --- *this is the whole comprising these parts* --- and the concepts entry's own definition (*a thing with a `positionGraph` of its own and a root node in it*) is, term for term, **a host that has a root node**. Three consequences, each of which a row below was still treating as open:

1. **"Whole" adds nothing to "host."** Locked clause 1 is therefore not a claim about a construct at all; it is the claim that **hosting is recursive**. [P2](#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge) already wrote the equation in passing --- *"the rope hosts its parts exactly as the box hosts its contents --- same mechanism"* --- and treated it as a load-bearing reading rather than an aside, which is what stopped its P2 clause 3 recreating the phantom-edge defect one scale down.
2. **A fork of the form "owned by the room, or by the whole?" is not a fork.** A room is a whole of its contents; a whole is a host of its parts; there is one slot, asked from two directions.
3. **A question whose answer turns on whether something *is* a whole has no purchase**, because everything is one relative to whatever it contains --- including things that contain nothing, which is exactly **PQ-13**.

**What this does not settle, and the distinction matters more than the entailment does.** The claims are **earned below the room and aspirational above it**, per the lock's [own recorded risk](#consequences-accepted-by-locking-recorded-so-they-are-owned-rather-than-rediscovered) and the warning that travels with the concepts entry. `EphemeraMembershipHostId = Room | Character` is a **closed enumeration**, and the room boundary is an authored asset-stack merge seam rather than a play-time graph mutation. **The residual privilege lives in that union, not in the vocabulary** --- which is **AB-12**'s deliberately separable second bet, and it stays open. **Dissolving *whole* does not dissolve *Room*, and this section must not be cited as though it had.**

### The four rows still written against it

| Row | The framing that presumes the dissolved distinction | What the entailment does to it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **PQ-13** --- does a plain object carry a presence port | Recorded as *"a lean, not a decision,"* weighing uniformity against ceremony --- while its own text already says the level at which an object switches mechanisms *"**is** the whole/part line C11 dissolved"* | **Forced yes by entailment, not decided on the balance of argument.** There is no category *plain object* to switch **from** --- only a thing nobody has decomposed yet. The row identified its own decider and then went on weighing. **This is the base case locked clause 1 rests on**, so it unblocks what it was flagged as blocking |
| **AB-4** --- what is the level-crossing relation | The record option is *"a whole need not be a graph node, needs no host"* --- and the row calls this the **highest-leverage choice in the initiative** | **That horn is dead**, and was dead on 2026-08-06: locked clause 1 gives every whole a graph and a root node. The row's stated deciding sub-question (*can a whole itself be a part of a bigger whole?*) is trivially yes under the contextual reading **and** was already answered yes by AB-32. **The fork has one live horn; the row is not blocked on AB-32** |
| **AB-32** --- what must composition express | *"The actual decider: must a relation ever attach to a whole rather than to one of its parts? Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes"* | **The decider cannot discriminate.** Wholes are already nodes by locked clause 1, whichever way the requirement goes. **The row half-noticed** --- it appends that the decider *"assumed endpoints are node references"* --- and did not follow through. Its two answered requirements (wholes nest; one reference mechanism) are untouched |
| **AB-42** --- are negatives nodes or a collection | Option (a) is *"a negative collection owned by the room **or** the whole"* --- consequence 2 above, verbatim | **Not two candidates on that axis.** The room *is* the whole. What survives is AB-42's real question, node-versus-collection, which P2's phantom-edge argument already answers one level up and which this section does not touch |

**Its source case states the fork and flags it as a test, which is the closest anything came to catching this.** [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about): *"The coarse envelope could belong to the **room** or to a **whole** inside it … Under the fractal's no-privileged-level claim these should be one mechanism, and whether they are is a real test of it: H3's ports are the boundary mechanism for a **whole**, and a room carrying negative records has no port to hang them on."* **The last clause is the presumption doing the damage** --- it infers *a room has no ports* from *a room is not a whole*, which is the dissolved distinction supplying a premise. The case was right that this tests no-privileged-level and wrong that the test was unrun.

**Counter-evidence from inside the same cluster, worth recording because it shows the usage was already split.** [AB-45](#open-decisions-design--plan-only) treats *"what a tavern needs"* as a whole carrying an intensional description. **A tavern is room-scale.** So one row in the negatives cluster was using whole=host in practice on the same day another posed them as alternatives.

### Vocabulary residue: three rows that reach the right answer under a name that will re-teach the error

**Separated from the table above deliberately** --- nothing here is a wrong conclusion, and treating naming residue as equivalent to a stale decider would inflate what the sweep found:

- **AB-37, *"Whole-level reasoning summaries."*** Implies a summary tier. The row's own finding --- *"sufficiency is question-relative, so there is no single summary"* --- says there is none; summaries are at whatever level is read.
- **AB-25** --- *"any scheme that hosts a whole 'above' its parts."* H1-era height framing; a whole hosts its parts **in itself**. The row is already deflated and this is residue on a narrowed question.
- **AB-31** --- *"mint a second whole."* The identity question it owns (one new whole, or two and retire the original?) is real and untouched; the surrounding multi-step-write description is H1-era, which the row already says.

**Sixteen further rows use *whole* contextually and correctly** (AB-6, AB-11, AB-13, AB-15, AB-16, AB-17, AB-19, AB-26, AB-34, AB-36, AB-39, AB-41, AB-45, AB-46, PL-1, PQ-2/6/7). **Recorded as a count rather than a list of nothing**, because it is the measurement that makes the four above a specific failure rather than a general vocabulary problem.

### The process finding: the distinction grew back after it was locked

**The four rows split cleanly by date, and the split is the finding.** AB-4, AB-32 and AB-25 were written **before** C11 (2026-08-07) and carry deciders that predate it --- ordinary staleness, and exactly what this plan's supersession convention exists to absorb. **AB-42 and C17 were written on 2026-08-09, two days *after* the lock**, and reintroduced room-versus-whole as a live fork. **That is not stale text. That is the distinction growing back in new work.**

**Why it is worth a note rather than a fix.** This plan already names the failure mode --- *a caveat nothing acts on becomes an assumption* ([AB-0](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)), and the lock's own risk entry warns that *"the room/area seam is now less visible, not less real."* **This is the mirror image of both**: not a caveat that decayed into an assumption, but a **locked claim that failed to reach a cluster opened after it**, in a plan whose locked claims are otherwise its most-cited text. The mechanism that failed is the one thing locking was supposed to guarantee.

**The likely cause, stated so it can be checked rather than assumed:** the negatives cluster (AB-42--AB-45, C17, C18) came in through **P5 and improvisation licence**, not through the positional lane, and every statement of the whole/part dissolution lives in positional-lane text --- H3's clause table, the locked frame, the concepts entry. **A lock is only reached by whoever reads the section it lives in.** Worth watching whether the next cluster opened from a non-positional direction repeats it; a second instance makes this structural rather than incidental, and would argue for the dissolution being restated at the head of the Open decisions table rather than only in the frame that produced it.

## The parts-and-ports back-sweep (2026-08-10)

**The retroactive half of [the dependent-set discipline](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09), executed.** [Locked-frame clauses 1 and 2](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) --- *a whole has its own graph with a root node*, and *boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns* --- could not enumerate their dependents at lock time, so this is the re-read that stands in for the set that was never captured.

**It is a re-read, not a reconstruction, and nothing below claims to be that set.** A row that this sweep clears is a row nobody found a problem in today; it is not a row certified independent of the clauses.

**Scope: every open row of [Open decisions](#open-decisions-design--plan-only) and of [the PQ table](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused).** `P4-N`, `PL-N` and the corpus are out unless a finding reaches them. **Tranche A is recorded below; tranches B and C are pending.**

### The triage, and the one thing it does not mean

**The lock's text entered the file in commit `a3e20a95b`.** A row byte-identical between that commit and now has **not been edited since the clauses existed** --- a fact about the file rather than a guess about attention, which is what makes it a better ordering signal than the dates rows carry in their own prose.

| Tranche | Rows | |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A --- not edited since the lock landed** | 27 (16 AB, 11 PQ) | Swept below |
| **B --- edited since, but not against these clauses** | 25 | Pending |
| **C --- opened after the lock** | 13 | Pending. **Lowest yield, not zero:** AB-33's precedent is a row *malformed at birth*, which no staleness signal catches |

**One confound measured rather than assumed:** the 2026-08-10 clause-reference correction pass (the convention at the head of this file) edited many rows for hygiene alone. Normalizing construct prefixes away before comparing moves exactly **one** row (PQ-7) across the tranche boundary, so the confound is real and small.

**And the limit worth stating, because the tranche name overstates itself.** `a3e20a95b` was a batch commit carrying P3-era content alongside the lock, so **a tranche-A row may already be fully port-aware** --- AB-26, AB-31, AB-34, AB-36 and most of the PQ table are. *Not edited since the lock landed* is not *written before ports existed*. The tranche orders the reading; it predicts nothing about what is found.

### Tranche A: eleven rows clear, sixteen implicated

**Clear: AB-10, AB-14, AB-20, AB-22, AB-23, AB-24, PQ-2, PQ-3, PQ-6, PQ-10, PQ-11.** Recorded as a count with names rather than silently, on the same reasoning the whole/part sweep gave: it is what makes the rest specific.

**Two findings are structural and account for most of the rest; the remainder are per-row.**

#### Finding 1: "parts on different hosts" is a pre-lock premise, and one row is built entirely on it

**Under locked-frame clause 1 a whole's parts are nodes in *its* graph.** They do not sit on different hosts; the *whole* has ports into different hosts. **AB-26 and AB-27 are both framed on the opposite premise** --- AB-26's title asks *how do parts on different hosts connect*, AB-27 divides the world into parts-sharing-a-host and parts-on-different-hosts.

**AB-26 survives the correction; AB-27 largely does not.** AB-26 already found its answer (*part-connections become sibling links inside the whole's own graph*) and keeps a real open question in [PQ-8](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) --- only its framing is stale. **AB-27's central claim is the framing.** Its "genuinely new edge kind is required exactly and only where the current model already refuses to go" locates a joint at the host boundary, and locked-frame clause 1 moves every part off that boundary: `applyStepSequenceCore`'s throw-on-host-mismatch never fires for parts of one whole, because they share the whole's graph. Its closing question --- *when a co-located assembly is pulled apart across hosts, does a same-host relational edge become an adjacency-mediated connection* --- describes an event that does not occur; [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) already established there is no whole-migration under ports, only a port whose egress changes. **What survives is one narrower question:** whether the sibling links inside a whole reuse the shipped intra-host relational vocabulary or need their own kind.

**PQ-12 carries the same residue at operation scale.** *Must relocation follow adjacency* is posed as a constraint on **a part relocating to a host**; the operation C9 actually describes is **a port's egress being rewritten**. That is not only vocabulary: an egress is a boundary fact the interior owns, so the constraint is **locally enforceable inside the whole** --- materially stronger than this row's *rule versus the caller's business* fork, which assumed the enforcement point was outside.

#### Finding 2: port identity is a boundary fact, and three rows treat it as an interior one

**[What a port number is not](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) forecloses reading a port as a name for the node behind it.** Three tranche-A rows still do, in three different registers:

| Row | The residue |
| --- | --- |
| **PQ-4** | Its second candidate --- *key ports by what they bind rather than by ordinal* --- makes port identity a function of the interior node, which is the foreclosed reading as an allocation scheme. **And the argument against it already exists**: [PQ-9](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) reached exactly this conclusion on 2026-08-09 about *naming* (`thread:skein` re-creates the misreading) and it was never carried the two rows across to PQ-4, where it bears on a live candidate rather than a lean |
| **PQ-5** | Answers *yes* to **"is a port itself addressable as a part"** --- correct in substance (an exterior edge may terminate on a port, and chaining is the fractal working), stated in the one phrase the negative statement exists to prevent. A port is addressable **as an endpoint**; it is not a part |
| **AB-21** | *"Worth resolving with this row rather than separately, since the obligation is identical and only the scale differs."* **The obligation is not identical.** The north wall needs a *nominal* identity so two descriptions reach one thing (`stableKey`'s problem). A port needs an *allocation* discipline so one slot is not silently reused. They rhyme; their fixes do not, and merging them is what makes PQ-4's key-by-binding candidate look natural |

#### Per-row findings

| Row | What locked-frame clause 1 or 2 does to it |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-13** | **Two problems, opposite in kind.** *Already answered:* the row asks whether multiple parents are permitted; [H3 clause 6](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) states membership **is** a DAG as a hard requirement forced by [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer). *Written against a withdrawn model:* its one taken decision --- *[part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger)'s parent index should be plural from the start* --- names H1 machinery. **AB-13 was not among the six `H1-dependent` rows [rolled back](#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07)**, so the decision was never re-homed. **The insight survives the loss of its carrier and should move rather than go:** widening a singular field late is the expensive order, and under locked-frame clause 2 the field it now applies to is the **port list**, which is plural by construction --- so the decision is *discharged* by the design rather than merely relocated |
| **AB-19** | **Half answered, and the half is the structural one.** The row asks what a recognized abstraction must carry so an LLM step over it is *bounded*. Locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 answer the **extent**: the packet is one level of interior graph, and its boundary is the port set --- which is [H3 clause 4](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s grounding invariance read from the reasoning side. **What stays open is the content** (what annotation, in what vocabulary), which is the row's live half and is untouched |
| **AB-31** | Its concrete write sketch --- *mint a second whole, repartition the member set, rewrite the parent pointers of the moved parts* --- is scoped **"under H1"** in its own words and was never restated after the withdrawal, and AB-31 was not in the rollback set either. Under locked-frame clause 1 fission is *split one graph into two, each with a root, and re-bind ports*; there are no parent pointers. **The row's corrected port findings sit on top of an uncorrected mechanism sketch**, which is the more misleading of the two states |
| **AB-34** | **Status cell stale on three counts, and it is the row the whole PQ table hangs from.** (i) *"Provisionally answered by P3"* --- P3's two shape claims are **locked**, and only its calibration is provisional. (ii) *"PQ-6/PQ-7 blocking"* --- **PQ-6** deflated on 2026-08-06 (its own cell reads *no longer the blocking item*) and **PQ-7** was restated to not-open the same day. (iii) *"nine sub-questions"* --- there are fourteen |
| **AB-35** | ***The one finding here that is new rather than stale, and it is the sharpest.*** `positionCache` is specified as *a denormalized read structure holding expanded graphs across multiple host nodes*. **An expansion that crosses port boundaries is precisely the thing locked-frame clause 2 buys encapsulation to prevent, arriving as a performance measure rather than as a decision** --- and the lock's own recorded risk is that *"external code cannot see what scale a thing is at ... the failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained."* **A flattening cache is how that happens silently.** None of this row's open sub-questions asks it. **The question to add: does expansion stop at a port, and if it does not, what preserves the caller's knowledge that it crossed one?** |
| **PQ-1** | **Its two halves have different standing and the row states one verdict for both.** *Which side is authoritative* is **locked** --- locked-frame clause 2 says the binding is the one **the interior owns**, and the row's *interior egress list authoritative* is that clause, not an independent settlement. *Where the ingress denormalization lives* is genuinely provisional and rests on **AB-35**. The [exclusion list](#what-is-not-locked-and-must-not-ride-in-on-this) calls *PQ-1's record split* **the least locked part of ports** without drawing the line |
| **PQ-7** | **Points at a dead construct.** Its residue is *"logged against H1 clause 4"*, and H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07. Worse, its reasoning is conditional on that hypothesis: the exposure exists *"under single-host at an Area + ports"* and **never under multi-host presence + ports**, which is the assumption ports were designed under. **So the question may be moot rather than homeless --- but nobody has said which**, and the underlying question is first-order for locked-frame clause 2: *does interior-owned encapsulation cost the shipped `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` guarantee?* It should be answered or retired explicitly, not left restated-and-parked on a withdrawn clause |

### The finding that is about the lock rather than a row: locked-frame clause 2 does not state its direction

**Locked-frame clause 2 reads:** *"Boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns --- not by direct addressing of interior nodes from outside."* **The headline is direction-neutral (*boundary crossings*); the gloss names only the inward case.** Nothing says which is the clause.

**[PQ-8](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) sits exactly in the gap, and it is not a hypothetical.** A sibling link **inside** the rope carries a label naming an Exit **outside** it. The row reasons that this is *"arguably fine (an Exit is an edge, not a graph node, so it is not a port case)"* --- which is a defence against the **gloss**. Against the **headline** it is an unmediated boundary crossing and locked-frame clause 2 forbids it.

**Four rows depend on which reading holds** --- PQ-8, AB-26's licensing labels, AB-28's floor-hole repair, AB-36's second surviving consumer --- and they form one cluster: **reverse discovery from an exterior relation to an interior dependent.** Under the strict reading, that discovery is illegal and needs a mediating construct nobody has proposed. Under the loose reading it is merely *harder*, which is what AB-26 and PQ-8 already record.

**This does not meet [the re-open bar](#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause) and is not offered as meeting it.** No corpus case is unrepresentable and locked-frame clause 2's encapsulation payoff is untouched. **It is a request to state the direction**, which is cheaper now than after something is built against the reading nobody chose. **Note which way the evidence leans:** the payoff [argued for clause 2](#the-strongest-argument-for-ports-is-not-the-traversal-one) is that *interior repartitioning stops being externally breaking*, which is entirely about inward addressing --- so the gloss is probably the clause and the headline is probably over-broad. That is a reading, not a decision.

### The process finding: a row that corrects another row records the correction on itself

**Three instances in one tranche, all the same shape.** PQ-9 settled an argument in 2026-08-09 that bears on **PQ-4**'s live candidate, and PQ-4 does not know. AB-26 observed that locked-frame clause 2 buries AB-28's dependent link a level deeper, and **AB-28** does not know. AB-36 recorded that the port list demotes two of its four consumers, and **AB-34** still names the demoted pair as blocking.

**This is the [dependent-set discipline](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09)'s gap, one tier down.** That section fixed the failure for **locks** --- enumerate dependents at the lock, where the knowledge exists. **Ordinary rows correct each other constantly and have no equivalent**, so a correction is recorded where it was *discovered* and the corrected row goes on being read as written. Every instance above was cheap to fix at the moment of discovery and expensive to find afterwards, which is the same economics that argued for enumeration at lock time.

**Not proposed as a new discipline here, because the cost profile differs.** A lock is a rare, deliberate event that can afford a ceremony; row-to-row correction is continuous, and requiring a back-reference on every one would tax the ordinary case to catch the occasional one. **Recorded as a measured rate --- three in twenty-seven rows --- so a second tranche can confirm or deflate it before anything is built on it.**

---

## Proposal P4: settling, dataflow, and narrative heat

**Recorded 2026-08-07 from conversation, and it is the largest piece of design in this initiative that until now existed only there.** ~~**Not promoted to a working hypothesis**, for the reason [candidate H2](#candidate-h2-not-adopted-ab-12s-privileged-level-reframe) gives and [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) and [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) already follow: H1 is still in flight, and two hypotheses make the dependent sets overlap.~~ **That reason no longer holds --- H1 died the same day this was written, and P3 was promoted into H3 for exactly the same reason.**

**Status: accepted 2026-08-07, as a Prototype, not a Proposal or a Working Hypothesis --- deliberately a third category.** See [What P4 being load-bearing here means](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) for why the name matters and not only the label. The heading above is left as **Proposal P4** rather than renamed, per this plan's supersession rule and to keep every existing anchor into this section valid --- read "Proposal" in the heading as historical, not current.

**Provenance, and it predates this initiative by a long way.** The scheme comes from the **Ludic Network** conception worked out when `Variable` and `Compute` elements were abandoned --- *a connected graph-of-graphs of story elements*. The conflict it was built to resolve is the one [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable) has now reached from the practical side. **Recorded as prior conception rather than as a new idea**, because a design arriving twice by different routes is evidence, and because this plan has twice mistaken a remembered claim for a verified one. **Checked 2026-08-07 for scalability precedent and found silent, not favorable:** `Variable`/`Compute` were abandoned for a *flexibility* reason --- a deterministic JS cascade couldn't address unexpected player input, and the JS itself was a black box no LLM step could sensibly read --- not a scaling one; by the author's own account it scaled fine. **This history neither supports nor undermines P4's scalability**, and should not be read as either.

### What P4 being load-bearing here means (2026-08-07)

**The pattern to watch for is named in H1's own write-up, and it is happening again.** [Trigger 2's conclusion](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) warned that "conditional adoption goes de facto permanent once the dependent set is large enough that rollback means redoing fifteen answers --- at which point [a hypothesis] survives on accumulated cost rather than merit." Three rows lean on P4: **AB-39** (opened first, "if it falls this question returns intact"), and, added the same day as this section, **AB-40(b)** and **AB-37**, both narrowed by [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork) toward P4's topology-change/settling answer for whole-summary upkeep.

**Accepted, and accepted as staged --- iteration 1 only, not the full scheme.** [Discussed and agreed 2026-08-07:] P4 is a natural fit for iterative implementation, because the concrete grounding later iterations need (real fan-out, real diamond frequency, real settle time) is discoverable only by running an early one, not by further design-time reasoning. Four iterations sketched, in order: **(1)** a naive everything-at-once cascade, single invocation, committed in **sequential batches rather than one atomic transaction** --- this is what keeps it distinct from the path [the problem table](#the-problem-both-obvious-answers-are-unbounded-and-one-of-them-is-impossible) already rules out as structurally impossible, since each batch still declares its own footprint even though the scheduling across batches is naive. Untenable at scale; workable for a prototype the size of CoyoteGame; buys the partial-completion resilience that makes committing in batches safe at all, which nothing later can skip. **(2)** split into separately scheduled invocations, naive ordering --- this, not iteration 1, is what actually unparks the unshipped `thinking` `claimed`-status worker noted in [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark). **(3)** heat-maps, used to influence scheduling. **(4)** decay and re-energize --- the largest *conceptual* jump (lossless eventual consistency becomes a lossy, attention-driven cascade) but, by that point, not a large *implementation* one.

**Only iteration 1 is designed now; only iteration 1 is what the three dependent rows may cite.** AB-40(b) and AB-37 need nothing beyond "a self-maintaining, eventually-consistent cascade exists, with a stated partial-completion guarantee" --- iteration 1's whole content, no heat or decay required. AB-39 is only partly cleared: iteration 1 gives it a concrete "against which snapshot" answer (the last completed batch), but its PS-1/PS-3 sub-questions are about the reachability mechanism, not the cascade's staging, and stay open regardless of how many iterations P4 eventually has. **Tag dependent rows `P4-i1-dependent`, not `P4-dependent`** --- a row citing the full scheme when it only needs iteration 1's guarantee is the same over-tagging error [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) already warns against, one proposal over.

**Why "Prototype" and not "Proposal," and it is not a labeling nicety.** H1's rollback, had it come, cost **design time only** --- reasoning re-done, rows reverted to Open, no code touched, because H1 was never built. **P4-iteration-1 is going to have code behind it before iterations 2--4 are designed.** If it fails, the rollback is not "revert some Status cells" --- it is deciding what happens to shipped code that was known, going in, to be non-scalable. That is a heavier and more dangerous failure mode than H1's, not a different-flavored version of the same one: sunk *engineering* cost pulls harder toward keeping something that isn't working than sunk *reasoning* cost ever did, which is exactly why this needed its own category rather than riding on the Working-hypothesis vocabulary built for a design-only bet.

**The gamble is accepted on the following basis, stated so it can be checked against later rather than re-litigated:** further design-time analysis has run out of leverage --- the one available piece, "this is the same family as spreadsheets and circuit simulation," is real supporting evidence that a scalable version is *possible in principle* (those systems scale via exactly iteration 2/3's bounded-batch-then-prioritized-recompute shape) but cannot say whether *our* graph, with C6's diamonds and C12's directed causal edges layered over an LLM-authored consequence shape, behaves like theirs. Only running iteration 1 answers that. **Iteration 1 must therefore be built to measure, not only to work:** it should report cascade fan-out, diamond frequency, and settle time under naive scheduling as a first-class output, not an afterthought, since that data is what iterations 2 and 3 are designed from and the only way to obtain it short of building them blind. **A fourth measurement added 2026-08-09: fault propagation** --- see [the feedback regime](#the-feedback-regime-is-the-thing-to-measure-and-it-decides-which-solutions-to-look-for-2026-08-09). Proxies rather than direct measurement: how often a later reasoning step contradicts an earlier established fact, how deep summarization chains actually run, and whether contradiction frequency **rises with world age**. **That last one is the damping-versus-amplifying question stated as something countable**, and iteration 1 is the first place it can be asked of real data rather than argued.

**Named rollback trigger, set now while it costs nothing to be honest about:** if iteration 1's measured fan-out does not visibly compress under a sketched heat-driven redesign --- i.e., prioritized recompute would still have to touch most of what naive scheduling touches --- P4 as a whole is the thing that failed, not iteration 3's design. **AB-39, AB-40(b), and AB-37 all revert to Open if that happens**, exactly as `P4-i1-dependent` tagging exists to make cheap to discover. **P4 also has known, named costs independent of this trigger** (diamond glitches on DAG membership, cross-noted at H3 clause 6, and oscillation on causal cycles) that iteration 1's instrumentation should surface evidence on, not merely inherit as accepted risk by default.

### The problem: both obvious answers are unbounded, and one of them is impossible

| Path | Cost | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Store all cascading updates immediately on change** | Unbounded write fan-out | **Not merely expensive --- structurally impossible on the shipped kernel.** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once**, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered, so a transaction must declare every host it touches *before* walking. **A cascade whose extent is discovered by walking cannot declare its footprint** |
| **Re-derive from first sources on each read** | Unbounded fetch --- and after [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine), unbounded in *depth* as well, since no level is privileged | Workable, and pays on the player-facing path |

**That asymmetry is what makes the third path structural rather than preferential.** It is not the compromise between two workable options; it is the only shape that fits a kernel with a once-only footprint.

### The scheme

**Impulses of causal change are persisted into the graph as data.** Repeated waves of processing continue a chain of consequence that earlier passes carried only partway. **The consistency guarantee is given neither at write time nor lazily at read time, but *eventually*, when the graph's energy settles.**

**What it actually buys, stated so nobody defends it as an efficiency:** total work does not go down --- waves re-process, so it likely goes up. What changes is **which cost is bounded**. One unbounded transaction becomes many bounded ones, each able to declare a footprint it can know. **Unbounded *synchronous* becomes bounded synchronous plus unbounded *asynchronous*** --- the right trade precisely because the synchronous path is the player-facing one.

### The substrate is half-built, and the missing half is exactly this half

Verified against [`thinking/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/thinking/AGENT.md) when this plan mined it on 2026-08-05, before any of this was in view:

- **Shipped:** durable `Meta::Schedule` rows on `TASK#${workItemId}`, the status vocabulary `scheduled | claimed | cancelled | completed`, and a retention policy that explicitly does **not** TTL in-flight rows, so *"opportunistically queued work is not TTL'd while waiting."*
- **Not shipped:** **nothing writes `claimed`.** No worker, no deferred dispatch, no queue anything pulls from.

**A persisted impulse is a durable worklist entry, and a durable worklist is what those rows are.** `Meta::Result`'s `nonCollidingPutItem` --- first write wins, duplicate finalize returns `alreadyFinalized` --- supplies the idempotence a wave system needs when a wave is processed twice.

**Note the pattern rather than the instance.** This is the sixth time this initiative has found infrastructure pre-registered for a need it had not yet articulated. **The rule that follows: look for the pre-registration before designing anything new here.**

### Retraction dissolves under a dataflow reading, and that reading is the ludic-native one

**The problem as first posed.** Turning power *on* propagates naturally; turning it *off* after a cut is a **retraction**, and a naive relaxation never un-powers the door because nothing gives the door a reason to re-examine itself. That is the classic truth-maintenance problem, and it is genuinely harder than the direction the scheme handles well.

**The reframe, and it is a different computational model rather than a restatement:** *turning off the power is not the retraction of a positive state --- it is its own change, propagating as any other change does.*

| | Truth-maintenance | **Dataflow** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What a node holds | Facts, with justifications | **A current value** |
| Removing support | Find dependents, unwind --- absence has no representation to propagate | **Does not arise.** A new value arrives and flows |
| What justifies a value | A tracked justification structure | **The graph itself** --- every dependent is visited by construction |

**Under dataflow there is no retraction, only a new value.** Spreadsheets, reactive systems and circuit simulation are the right family. **The graph holds *states*, not *beliefs*** --- and "retraction" was an artifact of modelling the world as a knowledge base rather than as a world. **Same instinct that produced *ludic* as a name, arriving at the algorithm instead of the vocabulary.**

**Where the cost went, because it did not vanish.** Dataflow is **eager** where truth-maintenance can be lazy: cutting the trunk visits every downstream device, including the ones nobody will look at. Total work becomes proportional to the **graph**, not to what anyone asked. **Here that is the right trade rather than merely a different one** --- eager full fan-out is exactly what waves are good at, since each frontier is bounded and declarable. It is also what [narrative heat](#narrative-heat-the-optimization-that-is-probably-not-only-an-optimization) exists to prune.

#### The one wrinkle that survives: topology change

**Value changes propagate. Topology changes do not, because the thing that would carry the impulse is the thing being removed.**

The generator going dark is a value change on a node --- push it downstream. **Cutting the cable is an edge deletion**, and in a push model there is no longer an edge to push along. Nothing tells the door it lost an input.

**The rule that follows: edge deletion emits its own impulse before it ceases to exist.** The cut knows both endpoints; it delivers *you lost an input* to the downstream one as part of the cut, then removes the edge.

**And the codebase already forbids silent edge disappearance.** `removeObject` **asserts and throws** when a relational edge still references a node, deliberately with no silent-strip variant (BD-33/35), and [`boundaryEdgeOutcomes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) classifies every severed edge **carry / dissolve / defer / error**. **Fifth arrival of the boundary-edge discipline in a case outside its brief** --- and the least expected, since it was written for carrying a chest through a doorway.

#### Two defects inherited with the model

1. **Glitches on diamonds.** A node with two inputs tracing to a common ancestor can be reached by the short path before the long one and briefly compute a value that was never true. **[AB-13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s DAG makes this real rather than theoretical** --- one part, two wholes, is the diamond. Fixes: topological ordering of the worklist, or accepting transient glitches.
2. **Oscillation on cycles.** Generator powers pump, pump cools generator, and that is ordinary fiction. **Composition is a DAG; the causal kind is not**, so termination needs a guard rather than a structural guarantee --- `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has` is the shipped precedent. The dataflow framing does not fix cycles but it makes the failure **legible**: oscillation you can detect rather than regress you cannot.

### Narrative heat: the optimization that is probably not only an optimization

**The waste, made concrete.** [Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) strung through thirty labyrinth rooms. A naive cascade --- impulses along every link, no decay, no differentiation --- eventually proposes *"the string goes briefly taut"* to all thirty. **In actual play most of those rooms are empty**, and that calculation is the noise of a tree falling in an unoccupied forest.

**The scheme, in two halves.**

1. **The network cascades a second quantity besides causality: *narrative heat*.** Places people are generate more heat than empty rooms; things people are interacting with are hotter than things they ignore. **Heat cascades as a heat map in the *reverse* direction of links.**
2. **Change-impulses carry an energy payload** that **decays over traverse**, **splits on splits**, and **reinforces as it encounters heat** --- so impulses stochastically **travel toward heat**.

**Prior art, named because it tells us where the failure modes are.** Closest is **importance sampling with Russian-roulette termination** from path tracing, which faces this exact shape --- unbounded transport, most contributions negligible --- and has a rigorous answer about when stochastic termination is *safe*: kill paths probabilistically, but scale survivors so the expected result stays exact. **Unbiased, not merely cheap.** Also adjacent: ant-colony pheromone gradients (reverse-propagated attractant plus stochastic traversal, nearly one-to-one) and attention weighting.

#### The distinction the scheme requires: narration versus state

**Two different things travel the same links, and decay is safe for only one of them.**

| Impulse kind | Example | Decay in a cold room |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Transient / narration** | *"the string goes briefly taut"* | **Free and correct.** Nobody hears it, nothing persists. This is the tree in the empty forest, and the argument for heat is exactly right |
| **Persistent / state** | *"the string is now cut"* | **Lossy.** Segment 27 stays wrong --- and stays wrong **forever**, surfacing an hour later when someone walks in and finds a taut string that should be slack |

**This is a property of the impulse, not of the edge.** The same `part-of` link carries both, so it belongs in its own column rather than folding into [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register).

#### Heat rising must pull unsettled state to settle

**The repair falls out of the same mechanism, which is the main reason to think the scheme is sound rather than merely clever.** A character entering a cold room **is** a heat spike --- and it is also **the read**, the exact moment the state must be correct.

**So heat works in both directions in time:** it guides impulses forward in space, and a heat *increase* reaches back and finishes the ones that stalled. That is lazy evaluation reintroduced precisely at the boundary where laziness is correct, and it is what keeps cold regions from diverging permanently.

**It locks together with the tri-state.** A room whose state is `unsettled` is a room whose pending impulses must resolve before it answers. **Fourth independent arrival at that requirement** --- after coarsening ([C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable)), reads-during-settling, and Coyote observability --- and the first where it is load-bearing rather than tidy.

#### Decay to *parked*, never to *dead* (PROVISIONAL: challenged on two counts)

**Stochastic termination has a failure mode that reads terribly in a game:** *the trap door did not fire because we rolled badly.* Non-reproducible, undebuggable, and --- in the prototype setting --- indistinguishable from a dropped wave, which is itself indistinguishable from comic timing.

**So split the stochasticity:** **stochastic in scheduling priority** (which impulses process first --- where the optimization actually lives), **deterministic in termination** (an impulse below threshold is **parked**, never discarded). A parked impulse is a durable low-priority worklist entry that a heat rise promotes.

**And the shipped retention policy already describes exactly this:** in-flight `Meta::Schedule` rows *"do not set `deleteAt`, so opportunistically queued work is not TTL'd while waiting."* **A parked impulse waiting for heat is opportunistically queued work.**

**Recorded as provisional 2026-08-07.** Two objections, and they are not objections to parking --- they are objections to *never dead*, which is a stronger claim than the argument above earns.

**Objection 1: the parked set is an unbounded history.** Nothing above bounds it. It grows with every below-threshold impulse the world has ever produced, and the cost is not primarily storage --- it is that **a heat rise promotes the whole accumulation at once.** A room that goes cold for a simulated year and then has a character walk into it faces a settle proportional to a year of events, at exactly the moment a read is waiting. The mechanism advertised as the cheap path has its worst case precisely where its correctness guarantee is invoked.

**Objection 2: it aspires to the wrong discipline.** *Never dead* is a promise that **every causal lead is eventually followed up, regardless of whether anyone ever cared.** That is not how fiction works and not how a world should be run. There has to be a way to say **"this happened, it might have mattered if anyone had cared, nobody did, and we are not going to keep pushing it"** --- and the scheme as written has no such statement, only indefinite deferral.

**What the original argument actually supported.** The trap-door complaint is about **silent, non-reproducible** loss. It is satisfied by a *recorded* termination --- *this impulse decayed below threshold, here, then* --- not by keeping the impulse **live**. Those are different properties, and the section conflated them:

| Property | What it buys | Was it argued for? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Auditable** --- termination is recorded | Kills the undebuggable-roll failure mode | **Yes.** This is the whole of the trap-door argument |
| **Revivable** --- a heat rise can still promote it | Cold regions converge on read | **No.** Asserted alongside, never separately justified --- and it is the one the objections hit |

**Three repair directions, none adopted.** Recorded so the next pass argues rather than re-derives:

1. **Collapse rather than queue.** [The dataflow reframe](#retraction-dissolves-under-a-dataflow-reading-and-that-reading-is-the-ludic-native-one) already says *the graph holds states, not beliefs* --- so parked work should be a **pending delta per node**, not a log of impulses. Fifty queued changes to segment 27 collapse to *what segment 27 should now be*. That bounds the parked set by **graph size × relation kind**, not by event count, and it kills objection 1 outright. **This is a consequence of the reframe that was already recorded and not drawn** --- which is reason to suspect it is right, and reason to check it is not merely tidy.
2. **Park only what is not rederivable.** Under dataflow most state is recomputable from current sources, and rederivation from a cold region is affordable *because it is rare and local* --- the trilemma's second path, readmitted at the one scale where it was never the expensive option. If a node can be rederived on heat rise, its parked impulses are redundant and may be discarded. **Parking is then mandatory only for facts with no surviving cause record** --- *the cable was cut* --- which is a much smaller set.
3. **Let absence of heat be terminal, not merely deferring.** The mechanism objection 2 asks for **already exists in the scheme** --- it is heat's absence. What is missing is *permission for it to end something*. The candidate rule: an impulse parked below threshold past some horizon **expires**, auditably, and the node it targeted is marked rederive-on-read rather than settle-on-read.

**And the narration/state split above probably already decides most of it.** Dropping a **transient/narration** impulse nobody was present for is not a defect --- it is correct, and permanently correct; the loss the table describes is entirely in the **persistent/state** column. If so, *never dead* was over-generalised from the one row that needed it. **Not asserted --- it is [PS-11](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused).**

#### Split versus replicate is a per-relation-kind fact

**Energy dividing at a branch is conservation --- right for a *pull*, where force genuinely divides between paths. Wrong for a power cut**, which is a broadcast: all thirty downstream devices go dark and none goes one-thirtieth dark. Attenuating across a fan-out would leave a large power system unable to notify its own edges.

**So split-versus-replicate tracks whether the underlying fiction conserves something**, and it is a register entry. **It is also the register's first *prediction* rather than a recorded observation**, which is what that section was opened hoping it would do.

#### Heat's own cost, and a tension to resolve before building it

Heat is itself a reverse cascade from every occupied room, and its source term is **character navigation --- the highest-frequency mutation in the system** (BD-36; it routes through the same `commitStepSequence` as take/drop). On the face of it, one unbounded cascade has become two.

**What saves it: heat has no correctness obligation.** A heat map stale by seconds is fine; a causal state stale by seconds is a bug. So heat may be batched, timer-decayed, approximated, and shed under load --- none of which causality may do. **The asymmetry is what makes the second cascade affordable, and it should be stated as a rule before someone tries to make heat exact.**

**But heat is probably not only an optimization**, and that is where the tension is. *What does anyone care about right now* is the signal at least four things have been reaching for: [C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)'s Coyote context, which stages a whole room relevant or not and has no relevance filter; **AB-22**'s affinity filter; **AB-24**'s ambient context tier; and the [reasoning axis](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to) generally, since what belongs in an LLM packet is the hot material. Apprehension wants it too --- what gets described in detail is the hot material.

**Those two claims are in direct tension.** A mechanism several correctness-adjacent consumers depend on cannot be as sloppy as the paragraph above says it is allowed to be. **Resolve that before heat is built, not after** --- most likely by separating a cheap approximate heat used for scheduling from whatever the context consumers actually need, and confirming they are not the same artifact.

### Why CoyoteGame is the prototype, and the seam in that choice

**The artifact this architecture produces is a delay:** a trap door opens, nothing happens for several seconds, and *then* the person standing on it falls. **In most settings that is a defect. In cartoon slapstick it is the genre's defining comic beat**, so the prototype domain was chosen partly because the architecture's characteristic failure is a genre-confirming win there.

**A second, independent reason points at the same prototype**, and it is the stronger one for the record: **[C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name) establishes Coyote is also the domain that most needs the structure** --- `CoyoteStagedObject` carries no edges at all, so a rope tied to a branch and the same objects in a heap produce **byte-identical** prompt context.

**Three seams in the choice, recorded because a comfortable prototype is where measurements do not get taken.**

1. **The genre that forgives the delay never measures it.** Nothing in Coyote will complain that settling took eight seconds rather than eight hundred milliseconds. **Instrument settle time from the first wave anyway** --- Coyote is the only place to measure it under real load without the pressure to fix it first.
2. **In Coyote the delay is load-bearing, so Coyote will push to *preserve* it** --- and then to make it *reliable*, because a gag needs the beat to land consistently rather than varying with worker scheduling. **A reliable beat is a *scheduled* delay, a completely different mechanism from an incidental one.** Keep them separate by construction: incidental latency is an artifact to minimise; a deliberate comic beat is a narration- or trope-level device that asks for itself. Otherwise genre content ends up tuned into the causal substrate --- the instance-taken-for-the-category error already twice in this plan's catalogue.
3. **A dropped impulse and a gag that has not landed yet look identical**, and so does non-termination. **This is the third argument for making settle state explicit** rather than inferring it from *nothing has happened yet*.

**And one consequence for sequencing.** Both motivating cases --- the cut cable and the trap door --- are **removals of support**. That is not coincidence: assertion is fictionally boring and technically easy, while removal is where the drama is and where the algorithm is hard. **Slapstick is almost entirely removal of support**, so the hard direction is what the prototype hits on day one rather than in a later phase. Plan the first slice accordingly.

### What P4 would do to rollback trigger 3

**Moot as originally posed, 2026-08-07: H1 is dead, and not by this trigger firing.** Rollback trigger 3 asked what happens if *the migration cascade proves unplannable* under H1's clause 4/5 whole-migration. [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) has no whole-migration to be unplannable --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) already showed coiling a rope rewrites port egress with no whole ever moving. There is no cascade here for P4 to rescue, because the thing that would have needed rescuing no longer exists.

**Left in place rather than deleted, because its second paragraph turned out to be the more important half and was vindicated independently.** *"Eventual consistency on presence is a different and less obviously acceptable proposition than eventual consistency on state, and it should be argued on its own rather than inherited"* --- written 2026-08-06, before C13 existed. **This is exactly the distinction AB-40 split on 2026-08-07** (see [Open decisions](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)), from a completely different direction: taking the fork (presence/position) must stay synchronous, while what `PlaceSetting`'s summary says about it (state/consequence) can settle. Two independent arguments, a day apart, landing on the same line --- worth noting as the kind of convergence this plan has already shown repeatedly (AB-28, AB-29, and C5's guard among them), not as a coincidence to skip past.

### Open questions (P4 sub-questions; ids stable, never reused)

**Parallel to P3's PQ series, and none of these is settled.**

| ID | Question |
| --- | --- |
| **PS-1** | **What is a wave's unit of work** --- one impulse, one frontier, one host, or a time budget? Determines what a wave's footprint declaration looks like |
| **PS-2** | **Who claims?** `Meta::Schedule`'s `claimed` status has no producer. Is the claimer a worker, an opportunistic rider on the next request touching that host, or both? |
| **PS-3** | **What is the settle-state vocabulary**, and is `unsettled` a value in the lattice or a flag beside it? Four consumers now want it; none has specified it |
| **PS-4** | **Is heat one artifact or two?** The scheduling heuristic may be cheap and approximate; the context-relevance signal AB-22/AB-24/C3 want may not be. **Answer before building either** |
| **PS-5** | **Glitch policy** --- topologically ordered worklist, or accept transient wrong values? Coyote will not force the answer, which is why it needs deciding rather than discovering |
| **PS-6** | **Cycle guard** --- iteration cap, damping, or a declared acyclicity constraint per relation kind? |
| **PS-7** | **Does narration ride the same wave as state, or a separate one?** They decay differently (transient vs persistent) and the narration must be delivered in an order a reader can follow, which is [relational narration](../../AGENT.relationalNarration.planning.md)'s standing problem |
| **PS-8** | **What does the unbiasedness result require of us?** Path tracing's Russian roulette is exact in expectation *because survivors are rescaled*. Whether an analogous correction is meaningful for discrete state --- or whether *parked, never dead* makes it moot --- is unexamined. **Amended 2026-08-07:** the *makes it moot* half now leans on a premise that is itself provisional (PS-9), so this row cannot be discharged by appeal to parking |
| **PS-9** | **What bounds the parked set?** Opened 2026-08-07 from the objection that *never dead* is an unbounded history whose worst case lands exactly on a read. **Leading candidate is collapse-not-queue** --- parked work as a **pending delta per node** rather than a log of impulses, bounding it by graph size rather than event count, which [the dataflow reframe](#retraction-dissolves-under-a-dataflow-reading-and-that-reading-is-the-ludic-native-one) arguably already implies. **What must be checked:** whether every relation kind's impulses actually *are* collapsible (order-independent and idempotent on the target), because a kind whose impulses do not commute cannot be collapsed and would keep its queue |
| **PS-10** | **How does a lead die on purpose?** Opened 2026-08-07. The world needs to be able to say *this happened, it might have mattered, nobody cared, stop pushing* --- and the scheme has only indefinite deferral. **Separate the two properties the original argument conflated:** termination must be **auditable** (that is all the trap-door complaint required); it need not be **revivable**. Candidates: expiry past a horizon into an auditable dead record; **rederive-on-read** for any node whose state has surviving sources, so parking is mandatory only for facts with no cause record; or letting sustained absence of heat be terminal rather than merely deferring. **Note this is a fiction requirement before it is a scalability one** --- a world that never drops a thread can never close one |
| **PS-11** | **Is *never dead* only a persistent-state rule?** Opened 2026-08-07. [The narration/state split](#the-distinction-the-scheme-requires-narration-versus-state) says decay is free for transient/narration impulses and lossy only for persistent state. If that is right, *never dead* was over-generalised from the one row that needed it, and PS-9/PS-10 shrink to the state column. **Cheap to settle and it gates the other two, so settle it first** |

### What this does for the name
