## Open decisions (design --- plan only)

Plan-only: decisions being made in order to design this initiative. When one ships, record it in the relevant durable doc ([`positions/AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) for normative rules, [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md) for graduated vocabulary) and remove the row here.

**IDs are stable and never reused**, following the ladder's BD-N convention.

**Requirement rows and representation rows are different, and most rows here are the second kind.** Noticed 2026-08-06 when AB-4 turned out to be conflating both. *What must the model be able to express?* is answerable from the fiction with confidence. *What is the representation?* is not, and depends on the first. A row asking both makes the answerable half unreachable --- so when a row resists, **check whether it is really two rows** before treating it as hard. AB-4 / AB-32 is the worked example; AB-1 is the main pre-existing requirements row. **Splitting a conflated row is not the same as adding scope:** the count rises, the surface does not.

**A second distinction, added 2026-08-10 and cutting across the first: *not every constraint in this design got here the same way*, and how one arrived decides what re-opening it costs.**

| Kind of constraint | How it got here | What re-opening costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Derived constraint** | A corpus case forces it --- [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s DAG, [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s *where along* | **Highest.** There is evidence to overturn, and a case to explain away |
| **Minimal primitive** | The simplest thing that worked; never compared against an alternative, because no alternative was ever on the table. Justified by **absence of demand** rather than by argument | **Lowest.** The reasoning has not been done, so re-opening *does* it rather than undoing it. Its justifications are typically **post-hoc** and cannot be cited as grounds |
| **Placeholder** | Known-inadequate, retained pending design --- `Custom -> defer`, which *"has never had a resolution path"* | **None to overturn**, but it owes a design; re-opening is scheduling work, not revisiting a choice |

**Why this earns a place beside the requirement/representation split: it is how *provisional, acted on as settled* actually happens.** A minimal primitive quietly accumulates post-hoc justifications until it **reads** like a derived constraint, and then defends itself with reasoning that was never the reason. **[Single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) is the worked example** --- see **PQ-14**, which characterizes it as a minimal primitive and records the tell: *both* of its justifications plausibly survive the relaxation they are cited against, which means they were not doing the work. **PQ-9's numbered-versus-named is a likely second instance**, and is still genuinely undecided rather than quietly settled. **The diagnostic:** ask what evidence produced a constraint, not what arguments now support it.

**Rows whose answer is *derived from* a working hypothesis carry an `H1-dependent` marker in their Status cell**, per the tagging test in [Working hypotheses](#working-hypotheses). Merely being *compatible* with H1 earns no marker. The set of markers is the rollback unit if H1 is withdrawn, and its size is a deliberate measurement --- do not apply it defensively.

| ID | Question | Constrained by | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-0** | **Is "Abstraction" the right name, and is it one name or several?** ~~Provisional working term only.~~ **Resolved 2026-08-07: the name is *Abstraction Fractal*, locked.** The Improvisation mechanism/trigger collision that this row also carried is now **AB-38** --- **split out rather than left inside a resolved row**, since a live sub-question buried in a closed row is the dangling-pointer anti-pattern [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md) names. **What settled it, and it is not "we achieved scale-invariance."** The name's central commitment --- *self-similar across scale, no privileged level* --- stopped being prose and became two corpus findings: [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer) gives **not a tree** (one part, two wholes) and [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) gives **no privileged level** (a four-level chain, every middle term both part and whole). Those are the two properties that make *fractal* operational rather than evocative, and neither was evidence when the term was proposed. C11 also strengthens the name's payload from the other side: if whole and part are **roles**, there is nothing distinctive at any level to type --- which is the "no `Abstraction` type" position the name exists to argue. **The second reason is about this plan's own failure mode.** The term is already load-bearing throughout these three files. *Marked provisional, acted on as settled* is exactly the shape of "hold even 1a loosely" --- a hedge that sat for days and was then treated as a theorem. **A caveat nothing acts on becomes an assumption**, so locking the name with its limits attached is strictly safer than leaving a provisional label on a term everything already leans on. **Locked with its negative statement**, on the port scheme's precedent: **there is no `AbstractionFractal` type, entity, or record.** The name is the organizing principle. This is not pedantry --- the name reads like a noun while asserting that there is nothing to type, so someone will mint the type, and minting it falsifies the claim. **Three known departures, recorded with the name so the metaphor cannot paper over them:** (1) **DAG, not a tree** (AB-13) --- permanent conceptual limit; (2) **finite depth with a base case** --- a real fractal recurses infinitely and this one bottoms out, with **PQ-13** deciding whether the bottom is uniform; (3) **aspirational above the room** --- unchanged by anything since 2026-08-06, since Room/Area containment is still a structure of different provenance. **Departure 3 carries a visibility hazard rather than only a cost:** the lock already logged that *the room/area seam is now less visible, not less real*, and **a name asserting uniformity makes it harder still to see.** Adopting the name raises that risk; stating it loudly at the point of adoption is the mitigation, not a reason to decline. **Departure 3 deepened and partly repaired on the same day, 2026-08-10, and both halves belong on the record.** *Deepened:* **AB-12(b)'s rooms-nesting-in-rooms is parked** --- representable, unsupported --- so the level above the room stays aspirational by decision rather than merely by not having been reached. *Repaired:* the park is a **redirection, not a prohibition** --- an improvised sub-space mints as a **Feature `PartOf` the room**, which is an ordinary part-whole relation. **That matters to this departure specifically:** a ban would have installed a real asymmetry (*objects may decompose, rooms may not*) directly contradicting the name; a redirection keeps rooms decomposing like everything else and moves the asymmetry onto **apprehension**, which is smaller, honest, and already has a reserved slot at H3 clause 8. **Per this departure's own stated mitigation, the park is required to announce itself** rather than degrade quietly: a nook must be reportable as existing even where its perceptual effect is not modelled. **What would un-name it: AB-1 resolving as three genuinely unlike things.** The name assumes one substrate. The dependency has moved **favourably** --- C11's roles-not-kinds pushes AB-1 toward *one substrate, distinct types above it* --- but AB-1 is formally open, so the name is **falsifiable by AB-1 rather than waiting on it.** **Discharged 2026-08-07: AB-1 resolved as one substrate**, which is the answer the name assumed --- so the name's largest outstanding risk is closed one day after it was locked. The remaining departures are unaffected. **Graduated to [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#abstraction-fractal)** with the negative statement and all three departures in full, not as a pointer: a durable doc that adopts a metaphor without its limits is how the metaphor becomes the argument. | AB-1, AB-12, AB-38 | **Locked 2026-08-07** --- earned below the room, aspirational above it, falsifiable by AB-1 |
| **AB-1** | **One concept or several?** Do composition, functional-state aggregate, and multi-host extent share a structure (one type with facets), share only a membership mechanism (a common substrate, distinct types above it), or share nothing but a motivation? **Root question --- every other row reads differently by its answer.** **Resolved 2026-08-07: option 2 --- one substrate, distinct relation kinds above it**, covering all three facets. **The evidence is of two different kinds and they should not be blurred.** *Structural, from the corpus:* [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s impromptu timer and [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s machine are functional aggregates that turned out to be **ordinary compositions** --- a whole with members, needing no separate mechanism --- in cases built for other purposes. *Requirements, from [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable):* every construct on the positional side has a **state twin** (roles not kinds, on-demand improvisation, coarsening, the summarization seam, scale-relative truth, no privileged level), so a coarse causal rule and its exceptions are the same graph-connectivity problem this initiative has been solving. **The requirements argument is the same class as AB-32's same-mechanism requirement**, which has been decisive here twice. **Emphatically not option 1.** Two disanalogies: *powers* is **directed** where part-of is containment, so traversal rules differ; and the door is **attached to** the power system without being **part of** it, so a member set is participants rather than parts. Both argue for the middle option, and the substrate already tolerates them --- AB-26's licensing rule is *any kind, any provenance*. **"Unify AB-1" without this clause is ambiguous in the direction that costs more later.** **One cost accepted, and it is the real blast radius.** Unification pulls **AB-6 toward *derived*** --- a causal answer is reachability through intact edges and cannot be cached, since a cut anywhere invalidates it --- while position pulls AB-6 toward **stored**, because `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once. Two good reasons pulling opposite ways. **Softening:** state removes the option of dodging a question rather than adding one, since C7's derived-presence walk is the same computation. Evaluation timing is now **AB-39**. **One bound, held by charter rather than by argument.** Reachability is binary; magnitudes that combine along a graph (pressure, temperature) would need arithmetic position never needs. [`diegeticLogic/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/diegeticLogic/AGENT.md) commits to structure for narrative copy and generation context *"without treating the world as a fully simulated physical space."* **If that commitment changes, re-open this row** --- it is the stated re-open trigger. **What this does not license: a simulator.** AB-19 rejected compiling fictional physics into deterministic structure. The system hands a reasoning step *"powered; one fault, on the airlock branch"* and lets it infer. That is the seam, not a solver. **Two debts this resolution created, both noted 2026-08-07 rather than left to be rediscovered.** (i) *Distinct relation kinds* names no kinds --- now [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register), seeded with four and explicitly unstable. (ii) **`positionGraph` will be lying**: causal edges sharing the substrate means the type, the lane name and the graduated concepts vocabulary all describe something narrower than what they hold. Minimum a Phase 6 obligation; whether it needs handling sooner is [open](#the-naming-consequence-positiongraph-will-be-lying). **Note (2026-08-05):** AB-12's candidate reframe would dissolve the *composition* axis into "Object minting at another level," leaving AB-1 to arbitrate only between extent and state. Still not settled, and **compatible with this resolution either way** --- it changes how many facets there are, not whether they share a substrate. | Phase 0 corpus, AB-12, P4 iteration 1 | **Resolved 2026-08-07 --- one substrate, distinct relation kinds; AB-6 tension and AB-39 named as the accepted costs.** **Same day, both given a resolution path rather than left as open-ended costs:** P4-iteration-1's acceptance dissolves the AB-6 tension (stored structural membership, settled consequence facts --- not a single stored-or-derived answer) and gives AB-39 its "against which snapshot" (the last completed batch), tagged `P4-i1-dependent` in both cases |
| **AB-2** | **Node, host, or projection?** An abstraction plausibly wants to be all three: referrable as a thing (node), containing members (host), and summarizing for description (projection). Graph roles (hook 4) is the precedent for splitting rather than conflating. **Resolved 2026-08-09 by [locked-frame clause 3](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06), and the trichotomy turns out to have been mis-drawn rather than hard.** **Node and host are not alternatives** --- locked-frame clause 3 makes every host a node in its own graph, so *referrable as a thing* and *containing members* are one construct seen from outside and from inside. **And *projection* was never a peer of the other two.** It is a **read-time** concern --- which level of existing structure to present --- which is **AB-9**, and it sits on top of the representation rather than beside it. The row's own framing invited this: *"an abstraction plausibly wants to be all three"* is true, and reads as three rival representations when two are one thing and the third is a different layer. **Nothing here decides AB-9**, which keeps the perspectival question and its guardrail collision intact. **A fourth answer the row never offered survives too** --- **AB-30**'s *addressable but not a member*, which is neither node-as-member, host, nor projection. | AB-1, AB-9, AB-30, AB-33 | ~~**Open**~~ **Resolved 2026-08-09 --- node and host collapse under locked-frame clause 3; *projection* relocated to AB-9 as a read-side layer, not a third representation.** Closed via [the clause-3 dependent set](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09), which is what it was enumerated for |
| **AB-3** | **Does `EphemeraPositionGraph` gain a root concept?** If yes, `CarryClosureFragment` collapses into it per its own pinned trigger (hook 1), and `rootId` must remain an input, never derived. If no, composition needs a different carrier and the fragment's note should be updated to say so. **The trigger fired 2026-08-06, from an unexpected direction --- [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation).** This row anticipated a root arriving from *composition*; the candidate that actually showed up is **the host itself as a node in its own graph** (AB-33). Same root, different motivation, and it would satisfy this row without composition having answered anything. **Worth noting because the pinned trigger was written for the wrong arrival.** **Third arrival the same day --- [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) *requires* the root**, since `part` edges must emanate from the whole. So the trigger has now fired from composition (where it was written), hosting (AB-33), and ports, independently. At three, `CarryClosureFragment = {rootId, members, edges}` is simply what a ported object's graph looks like, and the question is less whether the root arrives than which motivation shapes it. **Fourth arrival, 2026-08-07 --- [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up) executes the collapse this row only anticipated**, and answers "which motivation": the root arrives so carry closure and composition share one code path. See [Shipped code this initiative would retire](#shipped-code-this-initiative-would-retire) for the concrete consequence. **Discharged 2026-08-09 by [locked-frame clause 3](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06): yes, and uniformly.** Every `positionGraph` gains a root node, whatever kind of host it belongs to. **The trigger fired from five independent directions in the end** --- composition (where it was written), hosting (AB-33), ports (P3), carry closure ([C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up), which executed it), and finally the uniformity argument that locked it --- which is worth recording as this plan's clearest case of a pre-registered trigger being **over**-determined rather than ambiguous. **`rootId` remains an input, never derived**, per this row's original condition: locked-frame clause 3 requires the graph to *designate* which node is root, which is that condition restated as structure. **`CarryClosureFragment` collapses into it**, and it turned out to be the worked precedent rather than the casualty --- `{ rootId, members, edges }` is the target shape, and `hostId` re-points and renames into it (AB-33). **Phase 6 discharges the pinned note in both of its locations.** | AB-1, AB-2, AB-33, AB-34, AB-41, AB-48 | ~~**Open --- trigger fired from four independent directions, one of them now executed rather than anticipated**~~ **Resolved 2026-08-09 --- yes, uniformly across host kinds, via locked-frame clause 3.** Five independent arrivals; `CarryClosureFragment` is the precedent, not the casualty. Closed via [the clause-3 dependent set](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09) |
| **AB-4** | **What is the level-crossing relation?** Is "part of" a membership relationship with an Object host (activating the deferred `OBJECT#` host, hook 2), a new edge kind, or something that is not an edge at all? Note that today's membership is exclusive, and parts-of-a-whole may or may not want that. **The exclusivity is now known to be a live problem, not a design question --- [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread), 2026-08-05.** *Held* and *present in the room* are not mutually exclusive for a spanning thing: Ariadne's thread is held by Theseus **and** lying across the floor of the room he is standing in. For a coin in a pocket exclusivity is exactly right; for a thread it is wrong, and **nothing in the model can currently say both.** This is the gap underneath the whole bystander case, and it is why **both** framings need an explicit step --- so it is not an artefact of H1 and will not be resolved by rejecting H1. Decide whether the fix is a second, non-exclusive relation ("occupies", distinct from membership), or whether presence becomes derived (AB-6) and exclusivity survives untouched at the membership layer. **This row's third option --- "something that is not an edge at all" --- turned out on 2026-08-06 to be the highest-leverage choice in the initiative, and the row did not know it.** If composition lives in the **record** (a `Meta` row carrying the member set and the part-connection graph) rather than in position-graph edges, then a whole need not be a graph node, needs no host, and **[H1's clauses 1b, 4 and 5 all disappear together](#candidate-drop-h1-clause-1b-and-the-whole-chain-with-it)** --- taking AB-25 off H1's critical path and dissolving three queued test cases. If composition lives in **edges**, wholes must be graph nodes and the whole chain stands. **The deciding sub-question:** can a whole itself be a part of a bigger whole? If composition nests and is expressed as edges, the record option collapses. **Sequencing consequence: answer this row before running any H1 clause 4 or H1 clause 5 test**, since those target machinery that may not need to exist. **Scope corrected 2026-08-06: this row asks *only* the representation question, and was conflating it with a requirements question now split out as AB-32.** "Is part-of a membership relationship, a new edge kind, or not an edge at all" is entirely about the planned implementation; nowhere did it ask what must be **expressible**. That mattered practically --- the requirements half is answerable with confidence while the representation half is not, and one row asking both made the answerable part unreachable. **Answer AB-32 first.** **Two changes 2026-08-06 from [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation), and the first is a precondition this row cannot answer around.** (i) **There is no containment relation kind in the model at all** --- `HostRelationalEdgeKind` is `'On' \| 'Under' \| 'Against' \| 'Custom'`, with no `In`, `Contains` or `PartOf`. So "is part-of a new edge kind" is not choosing between rivals; whichever option wins, the containment vocabulary is being **created**, and today's phantom host link is the only thing currently doing that job (AB-33). (ii) **The membership/edge/record trichotomy is missing a fourth option: an edge *endpoint address*.** P2 clause 3 lets an edge attach to a part without that part being a node, which is neither membership nor a node-to-node edge nor a record --- and it is what dissolves the AB-9 level problem. **Do not close this row on the three options as written.** **Narrowed 2026-08-09 --- the record option is dead, and was dead on 2026-08-06 without the row noticing.** See [the whole/part dissolution](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). *"A whole need not be a graph node, needs no host"* is contradicted by the locked frame's clause 1, which gives every whole its own graph and a root node; and this row's stated deciding sub-question (*can a whole itself be a part of a bigger whole?*) is trivially yes under whole-as-role **and** was already answered yes by AB-32. **What this row called the highest-leverage choice in the initiative has one live horn.** **Resolved 2026-08-09, and the surviving fork turned out to be false as well.** **The answer is a new edge kind: `PartOf`**, root to part inside the whole's own graph, per **AB-48**. **P2's endpoint-address option was never a rival, and P2 itself said so** --- the option looked like one only in the phrasing *the ends are endpoint qualifiers, **not members***, which [the very next section](#where-p2-clause-3-risks-recreating-the-very-defect-it-fixes) retracts in favour of *"`rope: rope-end` is an **address into the rope's own graph**."* Under P2's own adopted reading the part **is** a node, in the whole's graph; *not a member* meant *not a member of the room's graph*. **This row carried a horn its source withdrew on the day it was proposed** --- ordinary staleness rather than the AB-42 regrowth pattern, since this row predates the retraction, but the same class of miss. **The general statement is [the register's third finding](#the-relation-kind-register): kind and endpoint granularity are independent axes**, so *edges versus endpoint-addressing* was a category error --- both are needed, for different jobs. **The held-and-present requirement is resolved too, and by the same finding rather than by a separate mechanism.** Containment kinds take qualified endpoints like any other kind, so `Theseus -Held-> thread:1` and `room -In-> thread:2` are both true, of different parts, in different graphs. **There was no contradiction to dissolve:** exclusivity was an artifact of collapsing the thread to one indivisible referent, which is C7's *where along* defect wearing a different face. **Two consequences filed elsewhere:** AB-8 gains a framing, and `Held`/`Worn` are new register kinds. | AB-1, AB-2, AB-3, AB-6, AB-32, AB-33, AB-34, AB-48 | ~~**Open --- representation only; highest-leverage fork, blocked on AB-32; option set widened by P2**~~ ~~**Open --- representation only, and no longer either a fork or blocked on AB-32**~~ **Resolved 2026-08-09 --- `PartOf`, a containment edge kind; the endpoint-address horn was retracted by P2 itself and should never have stood; held-and-present resolved via qualified containment endpoints.** Both of this row's forks were false, one dead since 2026-08-06 and one since P2 was written |
| **AB-5** | **Latent vs. materialized: who mints, and when?** The flashlight's battery does not exist until dismantling; the place setting does not exist until recognized. Is minting an event (improvisation precedent, hook 3), a continuous derivation, or a presentation-time inference that never persists? Is it player-visible? If an LLM can promote a subgraph to an abstraction, that is a **write**. **Partially resolved 2026-08-10, on both halves the row asks.** **(A) Minting is an event, and specifically an *instruction the planner emits*.** Not a continuous derivation and not a presentation-time inference --- the third option is rejected outright rather than left standing, because it is the option that makes description a write path. **The instruction sits at the compile layer, as an abstract op, not as a new kernel step kind.** Call sites already do not hand-build step lists: they emit a `PositionKernelMoveOp` and [`compilePositionKernelOp`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/compile/compilePositionKernelOp.ts) expands it into `MutationKernelStep[]` ([Step vocabulary](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/AGENT.implementation.md#step-vocabulary)). An establish-whole / dissolve-whole op is a second member of that family, which keeps `MutationKernelStep` **closed** and revives AB-11's cheap "no new step kind" answer **on grounds independent of the withdrawn H1**. **Name them for the graph operation, not the concept** --- `establishWhole` / `dissolveWhole`, matching the shipped `establishRelation` / `dissolveRelation` pair --- because **AB-0**'s lock states there is no `AbstractionFractal` type, entity or record, and *"someone will mint the type"* is exactly the pressure a step called `mintAbstraction` applies. **The zero-new-step-kinds claim carries verifications, not forks --- and the A-and-B framing below retired one of the two originally listed here.** *Retired for the ordinary case:* AB-40(a)'s interior addressing, since a basic mint sources from an ordinary room host; it is still required for **nested** wholes and for C13's fork-taking. *Still owed:* `establishRelation` must carry AB-4/AB-48's `PartOf` kind, because `transferMembership` moves members and does not create edges, so mint is transfer **plus** establish. **Both are [parked](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) rather than carried here**, per AB-40(a)'s own precedent --- scoped, known-needed, no rival answer being weighed. If either refuses, the op stays and one step kind is added: a cost, not a shape change. **The op's shape, stated 2026-08-10, and load-bearing enough that the rest of this row reads differently without it: it is a relation between two things that already exist.** *Mint an abstraction **between A and B*** --- A the current host (a Room), B a subset of its current members (the objects forming a snare trap) --- and *dissolve* the same. **Everything involved already exists**; the mint **interposes a new level between A and B** rather than bringing anything into being, and the only genuinely new entity is the whole's own identity. **That makes it an ordinary `transferMembership` shape, not an exotic one:** `entityIds` = B, `fromHostIds` = {A}, `toHostId` = the new whole's graph. **Warning, recorded because a plausible parallel has misled this plan before: do not reason about minting from improvisational object spawn.** Spawn is `transferMembership`'s **pure-add** shape --- nothing previously existed --- so its hard problems are existence/placement atomicity and orphan compensation. **Those are not this operation's hard problems**, and importing them aims the design at the coordination layer when the actual work is **at the planning and compilation layer**: identifying A and B, and expanding *interpose* and *release* into steps. **The parallel is sound only *below* the instruction-planning line** --- where a whole, needing a name to render, acquires its identity row the way any improvised object does --- and misleading everywhere above it. **Three things follow, and each is a simplification.** (i) **Establish and dissolve are exact inverses**, not merely "designed together": the same interposition with A and B swapped in role --- a stronger form of what AB-17's ratchet worry asks for than the symmetry originally claimed here. (ii) **[C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s per-whole rule --- *release my claim on these parts, do not delete them* --- becomes structural rather than a caution**, because dissolve is a **transfer** of members from W back to A, and transfers do not delete. (iii) **The recognition producer's job description becomes crisp: emit A and B** --- which is also where AB-18's scope question and AB-6's member-set question actually land. **The one hard mechanical constraint is cleared, and this is the non-obvious part.** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered, so any instruction must declare its full lock set at plan time. **Mint** does: the planner knows which parts it is gathering, hence their hosts. **Dissolve** does *because of ports* --- a whole's port list enumerates every host referencing it, computable at plan time, which is precisely the footprint payoff **AB-31** records (and the reverse of what that row first claimed). **Three payoffs, and the first is the reason to prefer this over any derivation reading.** (i) **AB-9's guardrail stops being a policy and becomes structural.** *Description must never create a level* is today a rule someone has to remember; if minting is reachable only as a planner-emitted mutation op, description **cannot** mint by construction, since the presentation kernel filters to `describe` / `narrate` and never enters the mutation walk. Same move as *"`captures` exists only on the success branch"* --- a type-level guarantee replacing a convention. (ii) **AB-18's convergence requirement gains a mechanism.** That row requires a snare reached via a blocking referent-resolution path and via a deferred background path to produce *the same component*, and has had no machinery to deliver it; **one instruction with two emitters** satisfies it by construction. (iii) **AB-17's ratchet worry dissolves** --- a symmetric establish/dissolve pair *is* "design them together," and [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer) fixes the dissolve operand's shape: it releases a whole's `PartOf` claims, it does **not** delete part nodes. **Leans AB-38 toward (a)** --- if the op is the mechanism and on-demand / recognition are *emitters*, the three corpus cases that fire **neither** trigger (C9 lifecycle; C10/C11 authored) become additional emitters with nothing renamed. **(B) Minting is player-visible in some cases, by design --- and the *shape* of that visibility is what keeps it compatible with (A).** In every case below the player sees minting because a **committed mutation** invalidated something and the re-read showed the new level. **No description pass created anything.** AB-9's guardrail forbids description *creating* a level, not description *showing* one that was just created --- so visibility is a **consequence of the write**, never a write performed by the read path. Three cases, all intended: (1) **Recognition of a snare being built** ([C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)) refreshes the room's live affordance and the RoomHeader goes from *Contents: Rope* to *Contents: Snare trap*, some undetermined time after the player's action --- real-time reflection of what the system has realized, and acceptable UI. (2) **Asking whether a well-stocked kitchen has a chef's knife** materializes the knife and refreshes the same way. (3) **Asking for detail on an object** may answer with new interior detail inside a perception event on that object. **The timing split corroborates AB-14 rather than merely fitting it:** (1) and (2) are asynchronous, (3) is immediate --- the criticality axis **predicting** the UI shape, which is stronger evidence than an axis that only accommodates it. **And (2) runs downward where (1) runs upward** --- materializing a part from a coarse whole versus recognizing a whole from parts, this row's own two seed examples --- so **one instruction covers both directions**, which is a point in its favour and not an accident of framing. **Case (3) is [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it), and it is now chosen rather than argued.** Under (A) it works **only** if *look carefully at the flashlight* routes through the planner as a **command that happens to read** --- AB-9's own first candidate resolution. That horn is adopted here on the strength of a stated product intent, not on the balance of the AB-9/AB-14 argument, which remains genuinely even. **AB-9's guardrail text as written forbids it and owes a carve-out.** **One concrete obligation this creates, found by tracing the shipped path rather than assumed.** Affordance invalidation reasons are a **closed union** --- `'roster' \| 'objects' \| 'topology'` ([`affordanceOrchestration/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/affordanceOrchestration/AGENT.md)) --- and `objects` is fed by `Objects Changed`, which is an **object-id existence fact** (`createdIds` / `destroyedIds`), *not* a `positionGraph` shape change. So case (1) fires today **only if minting the snare creates an `OBJECT#`**. If a minted whole is purely graph-shaped --- a root node plus `PartOf` edges, no record, the direction AB-0's no-type-no-record lock leans --- then **nothing invalidates and the RoomHeader says *Contents: Rope* indefinitely.** **Two exits, and this is an open fork rather than a defect:** give minted wholes an `OBJECT#` identity via the [improvisation two-step](#improvisation-is-the-objects-lanes-mechanism-generalized) (existence-then-placement, S1 compensation --- shipped, and where the displayable `shortName` already lives), or add a fourth invalidation reason fed by a positions-side fact. **Decided 2026-08-10: the first.** A minted whole takes an `OBJECT#` identity via the improvisation two-step, because case (1) needs a *name* to render and that row already carries one. **Three consequences that are gains:** the whole gets an `EMBEDDING#IMPROMPTU` vector, so a recognized snare trap becomes semantically findable --- which is [C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)'s actual complaint; it becomes a render-cache host once looked at, which is the mechanism case (3) needs; and **AB-41's ephemeral/durable line becomes crisp** --- ephemeral means *no `OBJECT#`, never written*, durable means it has one, so **promotion is "run the two-step"** as a known operation even while its *criterion* stays P4 iteration 3's. **The objection this invites, answered once so it is not re-litigated: no, this does not mint the type AB-0's lock forbids.** A whole takes an `OBJECT#` because it **is** an object --- [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s roles-not-kinds --- and a snare trap is an object that happens to have parts. Nothing typed as an abstraction exists. **That is a vindication of the lock, not a strain on it.** **Case (1) also couples this row to AB-9 in the affordance compose:** *Contents: Snare trap* rather than *Contents: Rope, Stick, Loop* is a **level-selection rule** --- show wholes, suppress their parts --- which is AB-9's territory arriving in a concrete consumer. Named here so it is not rediscovered as a bug. **(C) Recognition gets its own producer --- decided 2026-08-10, closing this row's other fork.** It is not P4's settling lane; it is a producer emitting the **same** ops into its own step sequence, with **P4's heat plausibly the *trigger* that wakes it** rather than a rival mechanism (and `thinking`'s [shipped ledger, unbuilt queue](#thinking-as-the-scheduling-substrate-shipped-ledger-unbuilt-queue) is the substrate such a producer would want). **What this buys, beyond fork-closure:** AB-18's convergence gain stops being contingent, since both paths now demonstrably emit one op through one expansion; and **AB-20's failure/authorization asymmetry lands entirely at the producer** --- blocking-and-compensated for on-demand, best-effort for recognition --- so **the kernel never learns which trigger fired** and nothing downstream branches on criticality. **What it costs: it names a component that does not exist**, and AB-18's hard half (scope, trigger, latency, and the authored room with six utensils that has *no transition to fire on*) now belongs to it. That is **localization of work AB-18 already owed**, not new work. **Two obligations, restated smaller than they were first drafted --- the A-and-B framing shrank both, and recording the original size would overstate them.** **(i) Failed-mint residue, not an orphan.** The identity row and the interposition are separate writes, so a failure between them leaves a **named whole with no members**. **This is not the objects lane's orphan** --- the members never moved, so the world is in its pre-mint state plus a stray name row, which is strictly more benign than existence-without-placement. Compensation is the existing S1 delete. **What is owed is small and specific:** an empty whole can still render in Contents, so either the sweep's litmus gains a clause or the ordering makes the state unreachable. **(ii) Dissolve's remaining asymmetry is one clause, not two.** *Release the parts* is structural under (A)(ii) and needs no rule; what dissolve additionally does is retire **its own** three rows plus its render-cache footprint via `persistDeleteImprovisationObject`. **State the two together at the call site** --- deletes itself, transfers its members --- because they sit adjacent and conflating them deletes the parts. **Cases (2) and (3) are one rule, not a precedent plus an exception --- AB-9's carve-out, committed 2026-08-10:** *a perception command actively seeking detail beyond what is currently represented, at levels above or below the coarseness now held, may mint the level it seeks.* Chef's knife and flashlight interior are the same act at different signs. **The carve-out is bidirectional deliberately** (a downward-only licence would install the decompose-but-not-compose asymmetry AB-0's departure 3 exists to prevent) and **naturally idempotent** (the licence is to mint what is *not yet* represented, so a second look selects rather than mints --- AB-18's convergence obligation met from a second direction). **It does not license *what may be found***, only what may be sought; that is AB-19 and the P5 licence surface, and AB-9 records why it does not simply inherit AB-20's *the-player-said-it-so-there-is-one* rule. **Row closes 2026-08-10.** **What is left is two verifications, both relocated to [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) rather than carried here**, on AB-40(a)'s explicit precedent that a scoped known-needed capability with no rival answer is not a fork for this table: `establishRelation` carrying `PartOf`, and **the footprint locking a host that does not exist at plan time** --- `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once from a snapshot and mint's `toHostId` is the whole being created, knowable since the planner mints the id, but not yet present in any graph. **Two things this row does not answer and never owned, flagged so the closure is not read as wider than it is:** recognition's **scope, trigger and latency** are AB-18's (this row settles only that a producer exists and what it emits --- A and B), and the **plausibility bound** on what a mint may produce is AB-19's. | AB-1, AB-9, AB-11, AB-14, AB-17, AB-18, AB-19, AB-20, AB-31, AB-38, AB-41 | ~~Open~~ ~~Partially resolved 2026-08-10~~ **Resolved 2026-08-10.** Minting is an **event**, emitted as a planner op **interposing a level between an existing host A and a subset B of its members** --- not a derivation, not a presentation-time inference. **Player-visible by design**, always as a consequence of a committed write. **Identity** via the improvisation two-step; **two producers** (on-demand, recognition) over one mechanism; **description still mints nothing** except under AB-9's committed active-seeking carve-out. Two verifications parked; scope/trigger routed to AB-18, plausibility to AB-19 |
| **AB-6** | **Membership derivation and decay.** Is an abstraction's member set **stored** (an explicit set) or **derived** (a predicate over graph edges)? Someone takes the fork --- does the place setting survive annotated, dissolve, or get recomputed? Trim-to-valid is the structural precedent (hook 5). Stored sets imply a drift-repair story; derived sets imply a cost and stability story. **Pushed toward *stored* 2026-08-05 by [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger), on a mechanical rather than a preferential argument:** evaluating an operation over a spanning whole needs every part **and its host** known *before* the walk begins, because `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered. A *derived* member set cannot supply that without already knowing where to look --- the derivation would need the answer it is computing. **What stays open:** the drift-repair story a stored set implies, and whether decay is trim-to-valid (hook 5) or something stronger. **This row is being asked its question twice, possibly with opposite answers --- noticed 2026-08-05 via [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread).** Part-mediated reference forces **member sets** toward *stored* (the footprint must be known before the walk). Ariadne's thread pulls **presence** --- which rooms a spanning whole occupies --- toward *derived*, since a flat container set cannot supply "the thread now also occupies the room the walker entered" without the same inference either way. Those are different things and the answers may legitimately differ, but the row as written reads as though it has **one** answer. **Split it explicitly in Phase 2** rather than resolving it as a single question and discovering the other half later. **A third answer to the presence half arrived 2026-08-06 from [P3](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06), in a carrier neither option contemplated: presence stored *in the whole*, as its port list.** Under single-use ports a port's exterior end is a graph plus an optional endpoint, so a presence port is simply one with no endpoint --- and the rope then enumerates the rooms it occupies from its own graph. **That is neither "a stored container set on the part" nor "a predicate derived over room edges"**, which is why the row's two-option framing did not reach it. **Consequence to weigh when splitting: it puts presence and member sets in the *same* carrier**, which is the outcome the double-question worry was written to avoid --- so the split may resolve more cheaply than expected, or may reveal the two answers were never really in tension. **The third carrier stopped being contingent 2026-08-06 when [the frame was locked](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06):** it arrived via P3 and was therefore only as available as P3 was, but the port list follows from locked clause 2 rather than from any open PQ. **And the presence half acquired a *qualifier* 2026-08-06 from [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system), which is a different thing from a fourth carrier.** Whatever carries presence, C9's rule for reading it --- at least one port egressing here --- **is falsified as stated**: a moonbase computer system and a rope have identical port topology and require opposite room-scale answers, so presence is **not derivable from the graph at all** and needs a declared property (working name: *apprehension scale*). That does not change where presence is stored; it changes what the stored thing has to say. **Split the row with this in view** --- the presence half is now two questions, carrier and derivability, and the second was invisible while the rule looked sound. **So this row must now be split against three presence candidates, not two** --- and the third is not a variant of either, which is why the original framing could not reach it. | AB-1, AB-4, AB-5, AB-34 | ~~Open --- member sets forced toward stored (`H1-dependent`); presence has a third carrier (port list), now standing rather than contingent; row still needs splitting~~ **Reverted 2026-08-07: H1 withdrawn.** The stored-leaning argument rested on `computeStepSequenceFootprint` needing every part and host known before a single up-front walk --- entailed by H1's clause 4/5 whole-migration machinery, which dissolved with H1. **What survives:** the port-list presence carrier is H3's mechanism, not H1's, and is unaffected by this reversion; AB-1's pull toward *derived* and P4's settling reading stand as before. **Same day, closed by P4-iteration-1's acceptance:** the "contested" note above asked whether AB-1's *derived* pull or the footprint's *stored* pull wins, and P4 was flagged as the candidate that dissolves the conflict rather than picking a side. It does, concretely, now that iteration 1 is decided rather than provisional --- **the two pulls were never actually about the same fact.** The **structural member set** a synchronous transfer step needs (AB-40(a)'s territory --- the fork's node must be findable before the footprint is fixed) stays **stored**, mechanically, independent of P4. The **consequence facts** AB-1's state facet was pulling toward *derived* (is the door powered, what does a whole's summary say) are **settled**, via P4-iteration-1, which is neither of the row's original two options. **Row is Open only on the drift-repair story for the stored half and the settle-state vocabulary for the derived half (PS-3) --- not on which of stored/derived wins, which no longer has a single answer to give.** |

Confirm first that no home for diegetic state exists today (hook 8) --- if the slot is genuinely vacant, this row is larger than it looks and may deserve its own initiative. **Re-weighted 2026-08-07 by AB-1's resolution and [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable).** The *is it the same object* half is answered: state shares the substrate, with a distinct relation kind above it. **What survives is the carrier question, and C12 makes it concrete rather than speculative** --- object-level state is structurally absent ([`requestIntake.ts:11`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/renderOrchestration/requestIntake.ts)), the one live carrier is `Meta::Room.state.marks`, and it mutates **only** by inbound API message, so **no play-time path exists** by which repairing a generator changes world state. **Drop "possibly out of scope":** it has two named consumers now (AB-23 via C4, AB-1's state facet via C12) and a worked case. | AB-1 | **Open --- narrowed to the carrier; no longer speculative, and no longer plausibly out of scope** |
| **AB-8** | **Multi-host extent.** The rope. **[Proposal P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) (2026-08-05) proposes resolving this by *removing* multi-host objects entirely** and modelling spanning things as parts with the whole hosted at the containing node --- which would settle this row as framing (c), vindicate the contract clause rather than rewriting it, and move the difficulty into AB-25. Recorded as a proposal, not adopted; the framings below remain live. Three candidate framings: (a) one node with presence in many hosts --- **less cheap than an earlier draft of this row claimed.** Adjacency is natively multi-host, but "nothing enforces single-room today" was wrong: the membership compile gate **terminally errors** on `containers.length > 1` before planning is reached (corrected 2026-08-05 from [C2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c2-the-rope-in-two-rooms-refused-at-the-gate); see [Multi-host presence](#multi-host-presence-what-is-actually-true-today)). Framing (a)'s real costs are therefore that gate, end-state planning collapsing it, a singular `toHostId` on the transfer step, intra-host-only relations, and per-room affordance compose rendering a whole rope in each room --- **five sites on four routes, each of which decided single-host independently.** That multiplicity is itself evidence about the framing: a design the codebase has re-derived against five times is being pushed uphill. **It strengthens P1 symmetrically** --- a single-host design makes the gate correct as written, exactly as it vindicates rather than rewrites the contract clause --- though P1 remains a proposal, not adopted; (b) the abstraction is itself a manipulation host above Room; (c) per-room segment nodes composed by a **composition** abstraction, which would **collapse AB-8 into AB-4**. Note (a) and (c) differ mainly in *where the seam is drawn*, not in capability --- weigh them on which one a player-facing description and a `pull` operation read more naturally from. **Strongest evidence for (c), 2026-08-05 --- [C7, Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread).** Framing (a) represents a spanning thing as **one node** with presence in many hosts, which means there is nowhere to record *where along it* an attachment sits: three attachments on three hosts, a container set that is unordered, and a route that cannot be recovered from exits because a labyrinth has multiple paths. For an object whose **purpose is the route**, (a) is not merely awkward --- it cannot express the object. **This is the clearest discriminator the corpus has produced against (a), and it is about node-versus-parts rather than about hosting**, so it argues for (c) without by itself settling single-host. **Earlier evidence for (c), same day:** a rope *tied to a rock* puts a relation on one end, not on the whole. Under (a) that edge is ambiguous about which host it lives on, and the kernel derives a relational step's host live and throws on mismatch; under (c) it is cleanly cliff-top-segment-to-rock, same host, no new machinery. ~~**Settled sub-point:** extents spanning hosts of *different kinds* (two adventurers holding opposite ends in different rooms) is **not** a concern --- confirmed 2026-08-05.~~ **Reopened the same day, and the reason is instructive: the words did not change but the question did.** That point was settled while framing (a) was live, where it is genuinely a non-issue --- a rope simply has presence in two hosts, of whatever kinds. **Under H1 it becomes a question about the shape of the containment tree**: the whole must host at an LCA of a room and a character, so characters have to be interior nodes of a structure AB-25 frames purely as Room/Area. Same sentence, different question. **Recorded as a caution beyond this row:** adopting a hypothesis silently re-poses settled questions that were settled against the *previous* model, and nothing flags them --- so the settled notes predating [H1](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) are worth a deliberate sweep rather than trusted by default. **Also carries a doc-correction obligation:** the contract's at-most-one-room-per-`OBJECT#` clause contradicts the intended design and must be reconciled in Phase 1. | AB-1, AB-2, AB-4, AB-16 | ~~Open --- provisionally framing (c) under [H1](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node); `H1-dependent`, and the first member of the rollback set. The row stays Open deliberately: H1 is a hypothesis being tested, not a decision, so (a) and (b) remain live and this row reverts to unframed if H1 is withdrawn.~~ **Reverted 2026-08-07: H1 withdrawn, exactly as this row's own text anticipated.** Framing (c) --- decomposition into parts --- survives as a modeller's *option* under [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice), not as a forced framing; see [C11 Finding 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-2-decomposition-is-demoted-from-correctness-to-convenience). (a) and (b) remain live alongside it. Row returns to Open, unframed. **Re-framed 2026-08-09 toward (a)-with-ports, from AB-4's held-and-present resolution.** Presence becomes *the set of graphs holding a qualified reference*: `Theseus -Held-> thread:1`, `room -In-> thread:2`, **one thread graph**, interior not duplicated. **This is framing (a), and C7's objection against it does not apply to this version** --- the *where along* record that a bare one-node (a) could not hold lives in the thread's own interior, and the port says which part each referrer has. The plan already priced this: *"the rope is listed in two rooms but there is exactly one rope graph … closer to a presence index than to real multi-hosting."* **Framing (c) survives as a modeller's option under H3 clause 3, not as a rival**; (b) is unaffected. **The doc-correction obligation gets a fifth site and a clear direction:** multi-room presence is legitimate, so `evaluateComplexityPreGates`' terminal `multiPresent` error is wrong as written. Already booked in Phase 1 --- a fifth motivation, not new work |
| **AB-9** | **Which level gets presented, and is that manipulation truth or a projection?** The stated motivation is LLM reasoning scalability, which is a *read-side* problem: the value is in being handed the right level without seeing all levels. Projections are already filters, not the graph (hook 6). **This row most controls total scope** --- if summary is a projection concern, much of axes 1 and 3 never becomes stored truth. Also: is the chosen level perspectival (a novice sees a place setting, an inspector sees five utensils)? **Guardrail, relocated here from AB-14 on 2026-08-05:** description **selects among levels that already exist and must never create one**. If merely looking at something can mint durable structure, then description-time LLM inference becomes a write path, and this row stops controlling scope at all. **Escalated 2026-08-05 by [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends), and the change is one of frequency, not kind.** [P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) already noted it makes multi-level graphs acute; C5 shows co-located parts persist **permanently** whenever they are load-bearing, so a chain mounted at one end and attached to a chest at the other is three nodes in the cell's graph indefinitely. Multi-level is therefore the **steady state of anything anchored**, not a transient during manipulation --- so level selection is a default read-path concern that every describe must answer, which raises this row's weight without changing its question. **The guardrail now has a case that contests it directly --- [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it), 2026-08-06.** "Look *carefully* at the flashlight" mints its interior. That is **description acting as a write path**, which this row forbids outright; but AB-14 holds that operational necessity licenses minting and AB-20 that the player's own reference *is* the licence --- and a player asking for detail plausibly qualifies. **Both rows are defensible and they disagree about this case**, which is the first time the guardrail has been contested by anything other than an argument. Candidate resolutions, none chosen: the guardrail holds and close examination is not description but a *command* that happens to read; or the guardrail admits an exception for player-initiated detail requests, which weakens its scope control; or minting is deferred (AB-18's tier 2) so the look itself creates nothing. **Substantially deflated 2026-08-06 by [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) clause 3, though not answered.** "You see a rope, a rope end, and a rope end" is a problem only because parts must be **nodes** to carry relations. With part-addressed edge endpoints the rope is one node and its ends are endpoint qualifiers, so **there is nothing at the membership layer to select between** --- and C5's "permanent multi-level steady state," which is what escalated this row, turns out to have been an artifact of that same constraint rather than a fact about anchored things. **What survives untouched:** the perspectival question (novice versus inspector), and the guardrail collision with AB-14 --- both are about which level to *describe*, which no representation change settles. **Track the deflation honestly: this row was escalated on a premise P2 removes, so re-weigh it before treating it as scope-controlling.** **The guardrail gains a structural enforcer and owes a carve-out, 2026-08-10 from AB-5(A).** *Enforcer:* if minting is reachable only as a planner-emitted mutation op, description cannot create a level **by construction** --- the presentation kernel filters to `describe` / `narrate` and never enters the mutation walk --- so the guardrail stops being a rule someone must remember. **That is a strengthening, and it makes the remaining exception cheaper to state precisely.** *Carve-out owed:* AB-5 adopts [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it) as **this row's own first candidate resolution** --- *look carefully* is a **command that happens to read**, routed through the planner, not description --- **on a stated product intent rather than on the balance of argument**, which stays even. **This row owns the wording**, and the wording matters more than usual: a carve-out phrased as *"description may mint when the player asks for detail"* re-opens the write path the guardrail exists to close, where *"a detail request is a command, and commands mint"* does not. **Also gains a concrete consumer for the perspectival half:** AB-5 case (1) renders *Contents: Snare trap* rather than *Contents: Rope, Stick, Loop*, which is a level-selection rule landing in affordance compose. **Carve-out decided and committed to 2026-08-10. Stated normatively: a *perception command* that is actively seeking detail beyond what is currently represented --- at abstraction levels either above or below the coarseness now held --- may mint the level it seeks.** **The line this draws is passive rendering versus active seeking, and that is why it does not re-open the write path.** Ordinary description --- room rendering, affordance compose, anything the system emits without being asked for more than it holds --- **still mints nothing, ever**. What mints is a **command**, player-initiated and planner-routed, which keeps AB-5(A)'s structural enforcement intact: minting remains reachable only as a planner-emitted op, and the presentation kernel is still incapable of it. **Stated in terms of player intent rather than of call site**, because intent is what the parse can actually see, and a call-site rule would have to be re-derived at every new read path. **Bidirectional deliberately, and this is not a detail.** A carve-out permitting only *downward* detail-seeking would install exactly the asymmetry AB-0's departure 3 was repaired to avoid --- *things may decompose but may not compose* --- inside the row that controls what gets presented. Above and below are the same act at different signs. **It unifies two AB-5 cases that were listed separately:** *look carefully at the flashlight* ([C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it)) and *does the kitchen have a chef's knife* are **one rule**, not a precedent plus an exception --- which is the strongest evidence the carve-out is drawn at a real joint rather than fitted to C8. **Naturally idempotent, and worth recording as a property rather than leaving to be noticed:** the licence is to mint *what is not yet represented*, so a second look at an already-decomposed flashlight **selects** rather than mints. Repeated seeking cannot fork the world, which is AB-18's convergence obligation satisfied from a second direction. **The cost, stated honestly rather than minimized: this does weaken this row's scope control**, exactly as the candidate resolution above predicted. Perception commands can now create stored truth. **Bounded, though, on three axes at once** --- active seeking only, beyond-current-coarseness only, planner-routed only --- so what is given up is a clean *never*, not the scope argument itself. **One gap this wording does **not** close, and it must not be cited as though it did.** The rule licenses **seeking**; nothing here bounds **what may be found**. *Does the kitchen have a chef's knife* and *does the kitchen have a nuclear reactor* are the same command shape, and only the coarse representation already in place (*a kitchen with all the expected fixings*) distinguishes them. **That is a plausibility question, not a perception question** --- it belongs to AB-19 (bounding the LLM step) and to the P5 licence surface AB-41 names as open. **Note it differs from AB-20's on-demand rule** rather than inheriting it: AB-20 holds that the player's *reference* licenses existence (*they said "the north wall," so there is one*), and a **question is not an assertion** --- so the licence here comes from the existing coarse representation, not from the asking. | AB-1, AB-5, AB-14, AB-19, AB-20, AB-41 | Open --- scope-controlling claim needs re-weighing under P2; guardrail contested by C8; **the `H1-dependent` tag on the C6 escalation lapses 2026-08-07 with H1's withdrawal** (the question itself was never H1-dependent, so the P2/C8 tensions stand unaffected). **C8's collision closed 2026-08-10 by the committed carve-out** --- active seeking, either direction, planner-routed --- which also unifies AB-5's flashlight and chef's-knife cases into one rule and discharges AB-5's last open fork. **Row remains Open on its own two questions:** the perspectival half (novice versus inspector), and the scope-controlling claim's re-weighing under P2. **What may be found** --- as distinct from what may be sought --- is explicitly *not* here: AB-19 and the P5 licence surface |
| **AB-10** | **Authored vs. play, and template vs. instance.** "A place setting" as a recognizable *pattern* is plausibly authored (WML); "this table's place setting" is play truth. Nothing in the model has a template/instance split today. Relates to the deferred WML `Relational` / `EdgeList` convergence in [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wml-convergence-future) --- authority stays split even after wire types align. | AB-1, AB-6 | **Open** |
| **AB-11** | **Manipulation semantics: operand or new step kind?** "Clear the place setting," "dismantle the flashlight," "pull the rope." Carry closure already computes and transfers *sets*, so an abstraction as a named set may make `transferMembership` work nearly unchanged --- but "pull" is neither a membership nor a relational change. Determines whether the kernel's step vocabulary grows at all. **Candidate answer 2026-08-05, and it is the cheap one: no new step kind.** Under [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger), "pull the rope" grounds on a locally-present part, walks up to the whole, and the whole **expands** into per-part steps --- which is exactly Synthesize's **Expansion** sub-role, already growing one ungrounded change into an ordered sequence for carry closure (BD-13) and `sameHost` repair (BD-16). The resulting multi-host mutation also has a shipped shape: `MultiKeyUpdate` folds N effects into one `transactWrite`. **So the kernel does not change; a new expansion is written instead.** Notable that the scheme was not designed to answer this row and answers it anyway --- weak evidence the joint is real. **What stays open:** whether *every* abstraction-level operation decomposes this way, or only propagation-shaped ones like `pull`. | AB-1, AB-4, AB-8 | ~~Open --- candidate answer recorded (`H1-dependent`); generality untested~~ **Reverted 2026-08-07: H1 withdrawn.** The Expansion-based answer was argued from H1's whole-hosted-at-Area framing; plausibly re-derivable under [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) --- a "pull" still needs to reach each port's local part --- but not re-argued here. Row returns to Open. **Partial answer restored 2026-08-10 from AB-5(A), on independent grounds --- for the *lifecycle* operations only.** Establish-whole and dissolve-whole are **compile-layer abstract ops** in the [`compilePositionKernelOp`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/compile/compilePositionKernelOp.ts) family, so `MutationKernelStep` stays closed --- reaching this row's cheap answer **without** part-mediated reference, which is what the H1 withdrawal took away. **Explicitly not a re-derivation of the `pull` answer:** AB-5 argues from the compile layer's op/step split, not from Expansion, and says nothing about whether propagation-shaped operations decompose. **Verifications owed, not forks --- and one of the two first listed here is retired.** AB-40(a)'s interior addressing is **not** needed for an ordinary mint (the source is an ordinary room host), only for nested wholes and C13's fork-taking; `establishRelation` carrying AB-4/AB-48's `PartOf` kind **is** still owed, since `transferMembership` moves members and does not create edges. Both sit in [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark); if the second refuses, one step kind is added. **Row stays Open**, now on a narrower question: this row's *original* subjects (`pull`, `clear the place setting`) are untouched. **Sharpened the same day by AB-5's A-and-B framing, and it lands squarely in this row's territory.** Mint is *interpose a level between host A and a subset B of its members*, which compiles to an ordinary `transferMembership` (`entityIds` = B, `fromHostIds` = {A}, `toHostId` = the new whole) --- so **the *membership* half of the lifecycle operation genuinely does not grow the step vocabulary**. **Do not read that as the whole answer** --- an earlier draft of this note did, and it contradicted the verification recorded two sentences above: members move by transfer, but the `PartOf` edges do **not** move with them, so mint remains transfer **plus** establish and the zero-new-step-kinds claim still rests on `establishRelation` accepting a containment kind. **The work relocates to exactly what this row asks about second: the planning and compilation pathway** --- identifying A and B, expanding *interpose* and *release*. **Do not import improvisational object spawn as the model**; that is the **pure-add** shape (nothing pre-existed) and its coordination-layer problems are not these. |
| **AB-12** | **Is there a privileged level at all --- and are `<Object>` / `<Room>` / `<Feature>` already abstractions?** Candidate reframe (2026-08-05): the battery / flashlight / casing regress has no natural floor or ceiling, so pinning `<Object>` as origin with `<Part>` below and `<Abstraction>` above would build two type hierarchies for one relation pointed in opposite directions. If instead everything is an Object at *some* level, the composition case needs **no new type**, and nothing converts when a part is separated --- only the composition edge changes. **The reframe is clean for Object and much larger for Room and Feature:** Room is a *host*, so treating it as another level unifies hosts and nodes (AB-2's most radical answer, rooms nesting in rooms, Area as a bigger Object). It also makes Feature a part-of-Room, which would *explain* rather than merely assert the documented "Features are never membership-moved" narrowing --- while contradicting that documented decision. **Take Object and Room/Feature as separable bets; do not let the second ride in on the first.** **The second bet is itself two bets, split 2026-08-09, and only one of them is now settled.** **(a) Hosts and nodes unify** --- a host is a node in its own graph --- is **locked** as [locked-frame clause 3 of the locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06). **(b) Room/Area/Feature as levels in the part-of ladder** --- rooms nesting in rooms, Area as a bigger Object, Feature as part-of-Room --- **stays open, and is explicitly on the lock's exclusion list.** **The "contradiction of the documented Feature narrowing" is withdrawn 2026-08-10: there was no representation rule to contradict.** This row (and AB-30) read *Features are never the subject of a relation* as a constraint on **edge legality**. It is not --- the source states it as participation in *"only a **subset** of the **instruction structure**"*, alongside *look-at-able* and *not `transferMembership`-able*. **It is an affordance rule: so scenery can be described without the engine offering to put a mountain in a backpack.** A world-model reading was inherited and never checked against what the restriction was drawn *against*. **Consequence: Feature-as-`PartOf`-subject contradicts nothing**, the graph layer imposes no Feature restriction, and the guard that *is* worth keeping moved to **AB-41** (ephemeral wholes may not absorb Features). **Doc-correction obligation, booked on AB-8's precedent rather than left implicit:** [`positionGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/AGENT.md) states the narrowing in representation-sounding terms and should be re-scoped to say plainly that it governs the **offer surface**, not edge legality. Not edited during design; Phase 6. **The split is the useful part of this row's history:** (a) is about graph *internals* and (b) about the *containment ladder*, and everything that made this bet risky --- the authored-merge provenance seam above the room --- is in (b). **A uniform graph interior is not a uniform ladder.** **The everything-is-an-Object reframe is separately under pressure from two directions** (**AB-42**'s negatives and locked-frame clause 3's Room root both want a node tag that is not `'Object'`), and **neither threatens locked-frame clause 3**, since *every graph has a root* is strictly weaker than *everything is an Object*. **Part (b) itself splits by thirds, and they are moving at different speeds (2026-08-10).** ***Feature as part-of-Room* is in active play** --- **AB-30** now leans wall-as-`PartOf` and supplies the evidence (b) was always going to need. ***Area as a bigger Object* is untouched**; note `Area.positionGraph -> Room.positionGraph` **already exists** and is authored, so the ladder above the room already has one rung and (b) is not the same as *no ladder*. ***Rooms nesting in rooms* is parked --- representable, unsupported, and gated by redirection rather than prohibition.** **The park, stated so it is an outcome rather than debt.** Room-in-room structure is genuinely useful in prospect (a ballroom with a lemonade table, a dance floor, a musicians' stand) but its **player-facing UI and its apprehension reasoning** --- *how much can people on the dance floor perceive of action at the lemonade* --- are daunting, and **no corpus case demands it: zero of twenty-one.** So the **schema may store it; the modeller and UI neither create it nor promise to handle it well.** **The mechanism is a redirection, not a licence narrowing, and that distinction is the whole of why it works.** Under [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item) licence is unbounded by default, so *not building* room-nesting would leave it **permitted** and improvisation would produce it --- *a ballroom with all the expected areas* -> *is there a quiet nook?* is [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about)'s kitchen pattern one scale up, and the licensed move. **A narrowing to zero would fix that at the cost of the fiction** and would install exactly the level asymmetry (objects decompose, rooms do not) that AB-0's departure 3 is already carrying. **Instead: the modeller is prompted, and then deterministically gated, to mint an improvised sub-space as a *Feature* `PartOf` the room rather than as a nested Room.** **Three properties this has and a prohibition does not.** (i) **The gate is a type check, not a semantic judgment** --- *is the minted entity a Feature or a Room* is decided at the mint site, where *does this count as a sub-space* would require the compiled-fictional-judgment move **AB-19** rejects. One place, fully enforceable. (ii) **It reuses AB-30's construct rather than adding one** --- a wall is a Feature `PartOf` a room and so is a nook; one mechanism, and it is fractal-consistent (rooms decompose into parts like everything else) where a ban would not be. (iii) **The exit ramp is a re-tagging, not a re-modelling**, since under locked-frame clause 3 and roles-not-kinds a Feature with a `positionGraph` and a Room with one are structurally identical --- the difference is the host-kind union and what apprehension does with it. **One rule the park depends on: characters take a *spatial* relation to the sub-space, never a containment one.** `character In room` stays **direct**, with `character Near/Against nook` as a spatial qualifier. If a character were `In` the nook and the nook `PartOf` the room, character-room membership would become a **two-kind derived chain** on the system's hottest fact (perception, movement, navigation). Spatial kinds are not shard-defining, so this leaves that path untouched --- [the family split](#the-relation-kind-register) doing load-bearing work. **What the park does not buy, and must announce:** a nook exists but does not gate perception, so *who can hear this* answers *everyone in the ballroom* --- correct at room scale under scale-relative truth, but the nook's **existence** is a finer fact and the **no-silent-omission** rule applies. The reasoning path must be able to say *"there is a nook by the lemonade; whether it affects audibility is not modelled."* **Silence here is the specific hazard the lock's own warning names** --- a reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained. **Unpark trigger, named:** a case demanding sub-room structure that a Feature cannot carry, **or** apprehension-scale ([H3 clause 8](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)) being built for other reasons. **The unpark *shape* is sketched at [Out of scope](#out-of-scope-parked-capabilities-and-what-unparking-them-would-look-like-2026-08-10), and explicitly not owned by this plan** --- structurally the exit is free (re-tag Feature -> Room, remove one gate), and everything expensive about it (apprehension policy, perception, UI) belongs to other lanes. **What *is* in scope is keeping the exit cheap:** the three constraints recorded there --- no code may assume a Room's graph contains no Rooms, the gate stays one labelled site, and Feature sub-spaces stay structurally identical to Rooms. | AB-0, AB-19, AB-29, AB-30, H3 clause 8, P5 | **Open --- reframes AB-1; second bet split 2026-08-09, part (a) locked as locked-frame clause 3, part (b) open and excluded from that lock. Part (b) further split by thirds 2026-08-10:** Feature-as-part-of-Room in play at AB-30, Area-as-bigger-Object untouched, **rooms-nesting-in-rooms parked with a redirection gate and a named unpark trigger** |
| **AB-13** | **Partial order, not a ladder.** "Levels of abstraction" invites a scalar, but composition has overlap and multiple parents: the fork is part of the place setting, of the silverware set, and of grandmother's inheritance, none ranked against the table the setting sits on. Designing for "level N" would build an integer where a DAG is needed. The codebase already carries the artifact --- `computeCarryClosure` guards with `closureSet.has(...)` exactly because a node is reachable by more than one path, which is why `rootId` is **recorded, never derived** (hook 1). Decide whether multiple parents are permitted, and if so what that does to closure, transfer, and description. **Worked example added 2026-08-05 --- [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer), and it is stronger evidence than the fork.** The fork's three wholes (place setting, silverware set, grandmother's inheritance) are *categories* of the same kind, so a reader can talk themselves into a nesting. C6's two wholes are different **in kind**: the rope is a physical object, the impromptu timer is a mechanism, and neither contains the other. A DAG is not a modelling preference there --- a tree cannot express it at all. **One shape decision can be taken ahead of the question, 2026-08-05:** [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger)'s parent index should be **plural from the start**. A part belonging to both a rope and a knotted pair makes "pull the rope" ambiguous --- but the *ambiguity* can be deferred while the *field shape* cannot, since widening a singular field later is the more expensive order and is exactly the mistake `MutationKernelTransferStep`'s singular `toHostId` now represents. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), and both halves move.** *The question:* whether multiple parents are permitted is **already answered** --- [H3 clause 6](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) states membership **is** a DAG as a hard requirement forced by C6. *The decision:* the plural **parent index** is H1 machinery, and this row was not among the six `H1-dependent` rows [rolled back](#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07), so it was never re-homed. **The insight outlives its carrier** --- under locked-frame clause 2 the field it applies to is the **port list**, plural by construction, so the decision is *discharged by the design* rather than relocated. | AB-3, AB-12 | ~~**Open --- field shape decided ahead of the question**~~ **Open --- DAG half answered by H3 clause 6; field-shape decision discharged; what remains is what multi-parenthood does to closure, transfer and description** |
| **AB-14** | **One mechanism, two minting modes --- and the axis is criticality, not timing.** Refined 2026-08-05 (twice; see Discussion record for both supersessions). **The action is improvisation in both cases:** a sure-enough recognition that cups, plates and flatware form a table setting mints a `Table Setting` component of which the others become (at least temporarily) parts --- the same act as minting a north wall.
