What differs is **whether a pending operation is blocked on the mint.** **On-demand:** "lean the table against the north wall" cannot resolve until the referent grounds, so the mint is synchronous and on the command's critical path. **Recognition:** a loop of rope laid on a road may be understood as a snare immediately, thirty seconds later, or never, without materially harming responsiveness. Synchrony is a *consequence* of criticality, not the axis itself --- which matters because it predicts the three rows below. **Same persistence layer** (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, so provenance is invisible downstream), **potentially very different decision processes**, which sit entirely upstream of persistence. **The narration obligations also differ:** on-demand narration *is* the minting event (the parts come into existence for the player by being described); recognition's narration is a **contract with the player** --- how they learn the world accepted their construction as meaningful --- so it can never be a silent internal summarization pass. **Collides with AB-9's guardrail on a concrete case as of 2026-08-06 --- [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it).** "Look carefully at the flashlight" is on this row's account an on-demand mint (the player asked for detail, so detail is licensed) and on AB-9's account forbidden (description must never create a level). **Resolve the two rows together rather than separately**, since each is individually defensible and they give opposite answers. See AB-9 for the candidate resolutions. | AB-5, AB-9, AB-16, AB-17, AB-20 | **Open --- shape proposed; collides with AB-9 on C8** |
| **AB-15** | **What does authoring privilege, if not ontology?** If no level is ontologically primary, an authored `<Object>` is plausibly the level the author asserts is worth having **by default** --- the level unqualified reference binds to, revisable by circumstance. ("Barring specific instructions, you pull out the whole coil.") That would give authoring a real role that survives AB-12's reframe, and answers part of AB-10's template/instance question without a template/instance split. Decide whether default-binding is the whole of what authorship contributes, or whether authored levels are also privileged for persistence, description, or legality. **A concrete instance arrived 2026-08-05 --- the naming seam, and it is the one step of [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger) that does not fall out of existing machinery.** The referent catalog matches on `normalizedShortName`. A player says "pull the **rope**"; what is present in the room is a part. If the part's short name is "rope end," the whole's name is **not in the catalog at all** and grounding has nothing to bind to. So under H1 the authored level --- exactly the level this row proposes unqualified reference should bind to --- is precisely the thing that is *not locally present*. Three candidates, none chosen: **(a)** parts alias the whole's short name; **(b)** a two-phase match consults the parent index after a local miss; **(c)** the catalog widens to include reachable wholes. ~~Note (c) reintroduces the non-present things in the catalog that part-mediated reference exists to avoid.~~ **Corrected 2026-08-05 by [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread): (c) is better than that dismissal allowed, and it needs splitting rather than rejecting.** A thread passing through a labyrinth room **is** present there --- physically, describably, trippable-over. What part-mediated reference exists to avoid is surfacing Area-hosted **bookkeeping** wholes in rooms they do not touch. Those are different sets. So (c) resolves into two candidates: widen the catalog to wholes that **pass through this room** (defensible, and required if presence is derived rather than stored), versus widen it to **all reachable** wholes (which is the version that reintroduces the problem). **The naming seam dissolves entirely under multi-host + ports --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in), 2026-08-06.** The room's node **is** `OBJECT#ROPE`: the rope is present *by name* in every room it passes through, and the ports carry the where-along. So the catalog needs no widening, no aliasing and no two-phase match --- **all three candidates (a)/(b)/(c) become unnecessary rather than one of them being chosen.** Note precisely what that costs: the seam was created by single-hosting the whole, so **its dissolution is evidence about H1 clause 1b, not about this row's own question**, which (what authoring privileges, if not ontology) is untouched and stays open. | AB-10, AB-12 | **H1 withdrawn 2026-08-07 --- no change needed.** This row's text already anticipated multi-host + ports ([H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s mechanism) and scoped the `H1-dependent` tag to the concrete instance only, already noted as dissolving under that scheme. Tag lapses; the row's own question stands Open exactly as before |
| **AB-16** | **Are all "parts" alike?** Two apparently distinct kinds surfaced 2026-08-05. **Heterogeneous separable parts** (flashlight -> battery, bulb): distinct nameable things, no required ordering or adjacency, able to go their separate ways permanently. **Homogeneous extent** (rope, chain, cable, possibly river or wall): "parts" are slices of a continuum, identified by *where* they are rather than *what* they are, and inseparable without an act that changes the whole (cutting a rope makes two ropes). **Correction 2026-08-05:** an earlier version of this row said such parts are "ordered along a path," and that is wrong --- see **AB-26**; the structure is an adjacency-mediated **graph**, which branches. **Retired 2026-08-06 --- the position model does not distinguish the two kinds, and every candidate difference either dissolved or belongs to another row.** Worked through in order. **(1)** ~~**Positional disagreement**~~ --- withdrawn as circular (below).   2. ~~**Cardinality**~~ (extent spans hosts, containment does not) --- **confounded by size, and refuted from inside this plan.** Every extent example chosen was a *long* thing and every containment example a hand-sized one; spanning tracks extent-relative-to-host-granularity, not the part relation. [The granularity insight](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#discussion-record) already records a **coiled rope in a backpack** --- homogeneous extent, single-hosted --- so `extent -> spans` is false, refuted by the observation the rest of the design turns on. The converse is as easy: **a wagon standing in a doorway with a barrel in its bed** spans two rooms while relating to its contents by containment. **Spanning-ness and containment are independent axes, not alternatives** --- which is the separability (the ship's rigging premise, still open on its own terms) worry arriving from a second direction and landing harder: "both" is the *ordinary* case.   **(3)** ~~**Identity scheme**~~ (extent parts identified by *where*, containment parts by *what*) --- **withdrawn 2026-08-06; it was a misreading of ports.** `ROPE:1` names a **boundary slot on the rope**, not the part. This plan's own two worked examples are already symmetric: `port 1 -[TiedTo]-> OBJECT#RopeEnd1` and `port 1 -[SolderedTo]-> OBJECT#BatteryCase` --- both interior nodes are ordinary nominal ids. Numbering belongs to **ports** ([PQ-9](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)), never to parts. See [what a port number is not](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06).   **(4)** **Separability semantics** --- cutting a rope makes two ropes; pulling a battery does not make two flashlights. **Real, and the only survivor** --- but it is a fact about what a *divide* operation yields, so it is manipulation vocabulary rather than representation. **Relocated 2026-08-06** to the object-manipulation ladder's [Deferred, not rung-sized](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) list, as future planning gated on this initiative settling the fractal.   **(5)** **Minting license** --- no asymmetry: the flashlight's parts are minted too (see the mirror-image note in [Discussion record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#discussion-record)). Both kinds mint; that axis is **AB-14**/**AB-17**. **What retirement does *not* mean.** The two labels stay useful as **descriptive vocabulary** for corpus annotation --- a case can still be tagged homogeneous or heterogeneous. What is retired is the claim that the tag **decides** anything in the position model. **Re-open only on a difference that forks code.** By the test [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) was granted --- differing hop count is one mechanism, not two --- differing *row count* is not a fork either: under [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) + [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) a rope in three rooms and a flashlight in one are the same structure with N=3 and N=1 presence rows. | AB-1, AB-8, AB-26, AB-34 | **Retired 2026-08-06** --- see the row; the survivor moved to the manipulation ladder |
| **AB-17** | **Must the transition run backwards, and how far?** Haul the rope up, coil it, stow it: it must re-collapse to one object. The flashlight hides this (nobody un-dismantles often enough to notice), so designing from that example alone would produce a one-way ratchet accumulating orphaned parts. **The rope is therefore the better design driver for AB-5/AB-6 than the flashlight.** Decide the reabsorption criterion (candidate: no part is separately referenced or separately positioned), whether reabsorption is lossy, and whether a part that acquired its own history or relations can be reabsorbed at all. **Split into two criteria 2026-08-05 by [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends), and note this row's disjunction was right all along --- it was [P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) that collapsed it.** The two conditions are independent, so **migration-down and reabsorption are different events** and neither implies the other. **(i) Hosting** keys on *separately positioned* only: the whole hosts at the node containing all its parts, and migrating down happens the moment they co-locate. **Mechanized 2026-08-06 by [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in): under ports, "all parts co-located" is "all ports share an egress host"** --- a read off the egress list rather than a judgment, in the same way the relational half of criterion (ii) turned out to be a graph query. **And criterion (ii) gains a second mechanical clause from the same case:** "separately referenced" now means `nodeHasRelationalEdge` **plus** *does any port carry an exterior edge* --- a presence-only port does not block reabsorption, an attached one does. **(ii) Reabsorption** requires *both* conditions false --- and the **relational half of "separately referenced" is already enforced by shipped code**: `removeObject` throws `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` on a node a Relational edge still references (BD-33/35, no silent-strip variant), so the check is a graph query via `nodeHasRelationalEdge`, not a judgment, and the unsafe reabsorption is already impossible. **Substantially cheapened 2026-08-06 by [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings), if ports hold.** Under direct part-addressing, coiling the rope dissolves `RopeEnd1` and every external edge naming it must be found and rewritten **across hosts** --- which is also AB-36's missing index. Under ports, port 1 rebinds to the root and **nothing outside the rope changes**: reabsorption stops being a cross-host graph rewrite and becomes one internal edit. **Note ports were not designed for this row**, which is the weak evidence-of-a-real-joint signal this plan has twice taken seriously. **This is the half of the encapsulation claim that survives the [single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) correction**, and it survives *because* reabsorption is interior-only --- the rope keeps its identity, so no external reference has to change. AB-31's fission does not have that property and lost its version of this payoff. **What remains genuinely open** is the non-relational half --- what "separately referenced" means for a part carrying no edge but recently named by a player, or one that acquired its own history --- plus whether reabsorption is lossy. **And a third gap found 2026-08-05 by [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer): dissolution is *per-whole*, not per-part.** This row currently reads as though a part is reabsorbed or it is not. With multiple parents (AB-13) that is not expressible: the impromptu timer dies when the candle burns out while the rope persists, so the shared span is **released by one parent and retained by another**. The criterion therefore has to be evaluated per (part, whole) pair rather than per part, and "reabsorb the whole" has to mean "release my claim on these parts," not "delete them." **Both halves of that sentence stop being cautions 2026-08-10, via AB-5's A-and-B framing.** Mint *interposes* a level between a host A and a subset B of its members; dissolve is **the exact inverse, A and B swapped in role** --- so the ratchet this row was opened against cannot form, because there is no asymmetric pair of operations to get out of step. **And *release, do not delete* becomes structural**: dissolve **transfers** members from the whole back to A, and transfers do not delete. **What survives as a real obligation is smaller and sits at the call site**, not here: dissolve also retires the whole's **own** identity rows, so *deletes itself / transfers its members* must be stated together or the adjacency will get them conflated. **Unaffected:** the non-relational half of *separately referenced*, and whether reabsorption is lossy. | AB-5, AB-6, AB-16 | ~~Open --- hosting criterion settled under H1 (`H1-dependent`); reabsorption criterion open, but its relational half is discharged by shipped machinery rather than by this initiative~~ **H1 withdrawn 2026-08-07.** [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) already reframed hosting criterion (i) as a ports read --- "all ports share an egress host" --- which is [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s mechanism rather than H1's specifically; plausibly survives re-tagged `H3-dependent`, but not asserted here without re-checking against H1's original wording. The reabsorption criterion (`nodeHasRelationalEdge` plus an exterior-port check) is unaffected, resting on shipped machinery. Row returns to Open pending re-tag. |
| **AB-18** | **Recognition's scope, trigger, and convergence.** The two modes in AB-14 are **not** symmetric in difficulty. **On-demand minting is bounded by its operation** --- a screwdriver applied to a flashlight, or a referent that must ground, scopes exactly what to mint. **Recognition is an unbounded search**: noticing that several operations, possibly separated in time, have composed into a thing. Decide the scope (one host? a room? a carry closure?), the trigger (evaluated on each state *transition*, or periodically over *state* --- note an authored room whose table already bears six utensils has no transition to fire on), and the cost/latency policy when an LLM is in the loop. **Convergence requirement, found 2026-08-05:** the mode is a property of *the moment, not the thing*. "Check the snare" puts recognition on the critical path, so the same mint is reachable from a **blocking referent-resolution path and a deferred background path**, and the two **must produce the same component** --- otherwise a snare noticed one way differs from the snare noticed the other, or both fire and the world gets two. Ties directly to AB-21's identity obligation. Failure-cost asymmetry moved to AB-20. **The convergence requirement gains a mechanism 2026-08-10 from AB-5(A), and it had none before.** If minting is a **planner-emitted compile-layer op**, then the blocking path and the deferred path **emit the same op through the same expansion**, so producing the same component is structural rather than an obligation on two independently-written paths. **This is the single largest gain AB-5(A) delivers to another row**, and it should be weighed as evidence *for* the op reading rather than only recorded as a consequence of it. **What it does not touch --- and it is this row's harder half.** Scope, trigger and cost/latency are untouched: an op needs an **emitter**, and recognition's emitter is exactly what AB-5 leaves Open (its own producer, or [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s settling lane). The authored-room-with-six-utensils problem --- **no transition to fire on** --- is unaffected by anything here. | AB-5, AB-14, AB-19, AB-20, AB-21 | **Open --- convergence mechanized via AB-5(A); scope, trigger and the emitter itself untouched** |
| **AB-19** | **Recognition for description, or for mechanism --- and what must an abstraction carry to bound an LLM step?** Reframed 2026-08-05; an earlier version of this row proposed that recognition-for-mechanism be tractable via an **authored pattern vocabulary with behavior attached**, and that is **rejected**: it is the compile-fictional-physics-into-deterministic-structure move, which is both the wrong tool and not what the fiction is about. **Settled direction:** most player-assembled contraptions will require an **LLM step** to reason about consequences. The abstraction's job is to make that step **bounded and manageable** --- to be the right-sized packet of context --- **not** to replace it with a lookup. **Description vs. mechanism therefore separates by stakes, not by machinery:** description-recognition is a nice-to-have first pass with no causally complicated consequences (a place setting, Jane Austen aside) and stays pure projection under AB-9's guardrail; **mechanism-recognition is essential** --- reasoning about a snare *as* a snare, in the abstract, is the whole point, and deriving its result from first principles every time is a fool's errand. **The real question this row now asks:** what must a recognized abstraction carry so that an LLM step over it is bounded? Prior art and its limits: [Coyote as prior art](#coyote-as-prior-art-bounded-llm-reasoning-that-is-not-bounded-structurally). **This row's rejection was applied as precedent 2026-08-06, in a place it was not written for.** [P3](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06) asks whether relation kinds across a port boundary must be *compatible* --- `ThreadsInto` outside, `SolderedTo` inside --- and the answer is no compatibility matrix, **because a matrix would be the compile-fictional-physics move at a smaller scale.** Worth noting as a use of this row rather than a new decision: the rejection generalises, and its next few applications are likely to look like small, reasonable-seeming validation tables. **Half answered 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), and it is the structural half:** *bounded* has two components, and locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 settle the **extent** --- the packet is one level of interior graph and its boundary is the port set, which is [H3 clause 4](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s grounding invariance read from the reasoning side. **The *content* question --- what the abstraction must carry, in what vocabulary --- is untouched and is now the whole of this row.** | AB-7, AB-9, AB-10, AB-14, AB-15, AB-22 | ~~**Open --- direction settled, shape open**~~ **Open --- direction settled; extent answered by the locked frame; content open** |
| **AB-20** | **Failure semantics and authorization: the two consequences of AB-14's criticality axis.** **Failure:** an on-demand mint is a **correctness obligation** --- if it fails the command fails and the player is owed an error; recognition is **discretionary world enrichment** --- if it fails nothing happens and nobody is owed anything. Both disciplines already exist side by side in the objects lane (blocking-and-compensated existence+placement under **S1**, versus best-effort non-blocking embeddings and enrich prose), so this is a choice between shipped patterns, not new machinery. **Authorization:** on-demand minting needs **no confidence gate at all** --- the player's reference *licenses* the existence; they said "the north wall," so there is one. Recognition is **unlicensed**: the system volunteers an unprompted claim about what the player built. **This is why AB-18's false-positive cost lands only on recognition** --- the asymmetry follows from who authorized the mint, and should not be re-derived as an arbitrary policy choice. | AB-14, AB-18 | **Open** |
| **AB-21** | **Latent vs. invented, and the identity obligation.** The north wall may not be *invented* at all --- a room has walls whether or not anyone wrote them down, as the flashlight always had a battery. That is different in kind from conjuring something genuinely new, and it carries an obligation recognition largely does not: **identity**. Two commands referencing "the north wall" in one room must get the **same** wall, and so must a re-reference after a Lambda cold start; otherwise repeated reference silently multiplies the world. `stableKey` on `Meta::Object` exists for exactly this correlation problem (Coyote) and is the obvious hook. Decide whether latency-vs-invention is a real distinction worth modelling or only a narration nuance, and where the idempotency key comes from when the thing was never authored. **A second instance one layer down, 2026-08-06 --- [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s PQ-4.** Numbered ports invite reuse: release port 1, mint port 1 again, and a stale external reference silently rebinds to a *different* attachment --- repeated reference multiplying the world, in the same shape this row already names for the north wall. Candidates there are monotonic never-reused allocation, or keying ports by what they bind rather than by ordinal. ~~**Worth resolving with this row rather than separately**, since the obligation is identical and only the scale differs.~~ **Withdrawn 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the obligation is *not* identical, and the merge is what makes PQ-4's weaker candidate look natural.** The north wall needs a **nominal** identity, so two descriptions reach one thing --- `stableKey`'s problem. A port needs an **allocation** discipline, so one boundary slot is not silently reused. **They rhyme; their fixes do not**, and a port number is [not a name for the node behind it](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06). Keep them as an analogy, resolve them apart. | AB-14, AB-15, AB-20, AB-34 | ~~**Open --- second instance at port scale (PQ-4)**~~ **Open --- PQ-4 is an analogy at port scale, not the same obligation** |
| **AB-22** | **When is affordance annotation computed, and in what vocabulary?** Coyote's answer, and the part that generalizes: **at mint time, once** --- `tropeAffinities` are written by the Acme enrich call and live durably on `Meta::Object`, so the prediction pipeline consumes structure rather than re-deriving it. That directly answers AB-18's cost worry. **(i) Timing:** annotate at mint, lazily on first reasoning need, or continuously --- **and see AB-23, which shows mint-time can only be a partial answer.** **(ii) Vocabulary:** Coyote's structural roles (`terminal`, `trigger`, `delivery`, `autonomous_agent`, `prep`, `creation`) and six tropes are genre-specific; `EnvironmentAffordanceObject` literally enumerates `boulder`, `cactus`. Keep the closed-structural-vocabulary / open-narrative-content split (`gimmick` is deliberately free text) and graded `aptness` over booleans. **Bonus mechanism:** member annotations are a cheap **filter** for whether an arrangement is worth recognizing at all --- fork + plate + cup carry no structural roles and are causally inert; a rope with `trigger` aptness beside a branch with `delivery` is worth a look. That attacks AB-18's unbounded search **without** an authored pattern vocabulary. **Caveat from AB-23: the filter is itself context-dependent** --- at the Faerie Court the place setting stops being inert. | AB-14, AB-18, AB-19, AB-23 | **Open** |
| **AB-23** | **Relevant meaning is situational, not a fixed world vocabulary.** Reframed 2026-08-05, superseding AB-22's implicit "is the vocabulary general or authored per world?" --- a **static** question with no good answer. The real question is **"what kind of meaning is relevant *right now*?"** Coyote may shortcut this (as a first iteration deserves) because its genre supplies a constant, unvarying interpretive context. A general world cannot: ambushed by goblins, meaning is relative to combat; dining at the Faerie Court, whether the place setting has causal complexity becomes very real --- **same world, same objects, different relevant meanings.** Consequences: **(a)** mint-time annotation can only capture *durable* properties, so situational significance must be layered on top --- annotation is plausibly **base + overlay**, not one pass; **(b)** an existing axis already models exactly this shape one layer down --- the render cache keys records on `n` (mark state, `Array<{mark, value}>`) plus perspective and carries `situationId`, documented as "one cache row per distinct render (component + n + perspective)." **Premise corrected 2026-08-05 from [C4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c4-the-place-setting-at-the-faerie-court):** this row previously said the axis is "built and unpopulated," and that is wrong in a way that matters. It is **populated for Rooms** (`Meta::Room.state.marks`, merged by `handleApiStateChange`, defaulted by `computeDefaultMarksForRoom`) and **structurally absent for every other host kind** --- `requestIntake` pins Feature / Knowledge / Object / Character to `{ markValue: [] }` and `allowGeneration: false` **because they have no `Meta::Room` to carry state**, and `findRender` gates generation on `isEphemeraRoomId`. So the pin is not a switch awaiting a flip; it is downstream of a missing carrier. **Consequence: situational meaning below room scale needs a diegetic-state carrier for non-Room hosts, which is AB-7** --- flagged there as possibly out of scope, and now with a second consumer. This question remains that axis raised from *how a thing renders* to *what it means*. | AB-7, AB-15, AB-19, AB-22, AB-24 | **Open** |
| **AB-24** | **A third scheduling tier: ambient context-shift detection.** Proposed 2026-08-05. Beyond blocking on-demand minting and deferred-but-promotable recognition, a **low-immediacy scheduled thinking task** would periodically ask: *has the context or vocabulary for this room / these players shifted such that we should be watching for different kinds of meaning?* **It parameterizes recognition rather than performing it** --- tier 3 sets what tier 2 looks for. Substrate: [`thinking` as the scheduling substrate](#thinking-as-the-scheduling-substrate-shipped-ledger-unbuilt-queue). **Two design constraints worth stating up front. (i) Its failure mode differs in kind:** a missed snare is one missed snare, local and recoverable, while a stale interpretive frame makes tier 2 hunt for the wrong meanings *systematically* until it corrects --- silent and pervasive, which argues for cheap-and-frequent over expensive-and-rare. **(ii) The author already declares most of the frame:** a Faerie Court room is *authored* as a Faerie Court, so this task's job is detecting **deviation** (goblins ambush a room authored as a social space) rather than deriving a frame from nothing --- much cheaper and more verifiable. That second point is AB-15 again; see the note under [Constraint matrix](#constraint-matrix). | AB-15, AB-18, AB-23 | **Open** |
| **AB-25** | **Is the host containment tree actually a tree?** **Note (2026-08-05): AB-26 shows this row shares a substrate with part-connection licensing.** A **nesting** relation does double duty --- boat-in-bay both places the boat in the containment tree *and* licenses a connection across the boundary (over the rail). An **Exit** edge licenses a connection without implying containment either way. So the two rows overlap without being identical, and a nesting representation should be designed for both consumers at once rather than twice. Split out of [Proposal P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) 2026-08-05 as its load-bearing unknown, and **worth answering even if P1 is rejected** --- any scheme that hosts a whole "above" its parts needs it. **Framing residue flagged 2026-08-09:** *above* is H1-era height language --- a whole hosts its parts **in itself**, per [the whole/part dissolution](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). Nothing this row concludes changes; the phrase would re-teach the dissolved distinction to a reader arriving here first. Area membership is authored blueprint merged at participation order, so containment may be **asset-stack dependent**, while an object's host must be world truth rather than perspective-relative. Sub-questions: can one Room belong to two Areas (canon plus an overlay)? Do Areas nest, and how deep (`AREA#region` beside `AREA#downtown` suggests yes)? What is the root when two rooms share no Area? And since containment is authored **downward** (`StandardAreaData.positionGraph` lists members) with **no** room -> area index anywhere, where does the upward lookup live, given it must run on every part move? **A constraint arrived 2026-08-05 from [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer), and it forbids the shortcut this row is most likely to reach for: hosting height does not correlate with composition depth.** In C6 one part has two wholes hosted at **different heights** --- the rope spans room and garden so it hosts at the Area, while the impromptu timer's members are both in the room so it hosts in the room. So walking up from a part yields parents at inconsistent levels, and **no implementation may treat the parent index as "the next level up"** or index hosting by depth. This is AB-13's ladder-versus-DAG error one layer over, in the place it would look most tempting: *hosting* feels like it ought to be stratified, and it is not. Note the case does **not** bear on whether Room/Area containment is a tree --- that question is untouched and remains this row's core. **A second constraint the same day, and this one widens the row rather than restricting it: the tree is not Room/Area at all.** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is `Room | Character`, so characters are hosts, and a rope with one end held by a walker needs an LCA of a room and a character. **Characters must therefore be interior nodes** --- room contains character contains held object --- which this row's framing (Areas, nesting, participation-order merge) does not contemplate. Two consequences: the "is it a tree" question now spans a structure with **two node kinds of different provenance** (authored Area membership versus play-time character presence), and character movement --- the highest-frequency mutation in the system --- becomes a **containment-tree mutation**, so every whole with a part held by the walker re-hosts. Reopens the pre-H1 settled note on AB-8. **Deflation locked 2026-08-06, and the row narrowed with it.** Sibling links living inside the whole's graph gave AB-26 a home *below* the rooms; [locking parts and ports](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) makes that a property of the frame rather than a consequence of an unadopted proposal, so **Area is no longer summoned by part-connection licensing at all** and this row keeps only its own question --- is Room/Area containment a tree, now spanning two node kinds of different provenance. **This does not close the row and does not retire the trigger:** the character-as-interior-node widening is untouched, and AB-25 remains H1's only live falsification test. **PQ-8 is the acknowledged loose end**, and locking raises what being wrong about it costs, since it is now a loose end in something fixed. **Second consequence of the lock, and it promotes this row's importance while narrowing its scope: AB-25 is now the boundary the fractal stops at.** Objects below the room are ported graphs; Room/Area containment is not, and is of different provenance (authored asset-stack merge versus play-time graph mutation), so a walk upward from a part still changes representational regime here. **This is the next boundary that would have to be locked for the scale-invariance claim to be general** --- see [What the lock delivered](#what-the-lock-delivered-for-scale-invariance-and-where-it-stops-assessed-2026-08-06). **A hazard specific to that assessment:** encapsulation means external code cannot see what scale a thing is at, so this asymmetry is now **less visible without being less real**, and nothing will complain about it. Do not read quiet as resolution. The character-as-interior-node widening is the sharpest form of the question --- nothing says a character is a ported graph, and an LCA of a room and a character needs one story, not two. | AB-4, AB-8, AB-13 | **Open --- narrowed by the locked frame to the containment-tree question proper; still [H1](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node)'s only live falsification trigger** |
| **AB-26** | **How do parts on different hosts connect? (Adjacency-mediated, not sequenced.)** Corrected 2026-08-05, superseding this plan's earlier claim that extent parts are "ordered along a path." **A sequence is the wrong shape** --- it looks right only because a rope is linear, and a net spanning four rooms, wiring from a generator to a powered door, or two ropes knotted together all branch. This is the same error as AB-13's ladder-vs-DAG, one layer down: a scalar imposed where a graph belongs. **The right shape is a graph of parts whose edges are labeled by the spatial relation licensing them** --- "the clifftop rope end connects to the shaft section *by way of the 'into the abyss' exit*." Four properties follow: branching is free; the edge *explains* the adjacency rather than asserting it; narration gets direction and wording from the exit (as the shipped move compiler already does with `exitName` as an ingredient) rather than from an index; and `pull` becomes propagation over a graph instead of an index walk. **Already scaffolded:** [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#fractal-position-graphs-container-scale-and-edges) notes Area edge kinds beyond Exit "may express **non-traversable** spatial facts (e.g. 'north of' without a door)" --- exactly what a rope through a hole in the floor needs. **Two rules to pin: (i)** connections are **explicit but constrained** --- adjacency *licenses* an edge, it does not derive one, since two ropes in adjacent rooms are not connected; **(ii)** an edge **must** reference **world truth** and **never** the perspective-filtered output of `projectRoomExits`, or a rope's physical shape would vary by who is looking at it --- the authored-vs-projected line the graph-roles table already draws, in a new place. **Correction 2026-08-05:** rule (ii) first read "the *authored Exit* edge," which collapsed two independent axes into the one real constraint. **The licensing relation may be of any kind and any provenance:** *kind* --- an Exit edge (a hard-coded connection affordance), or an adjacency/nesting relation (someone goes over the rail of the boat and ends up in the bay); *provenance* --- authored **or improvised**, since an improvised relation is world truth the moment it is minted (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, not a flag). Only *projected* is excluded. **A write-side consumer appeared 2026-08-06 --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in), and it widens this row.** Hauling a rope in moves its far end one room along the path, not straight to the hauler --- so if that is an invariant rather than a convention, **the licensing relation constrains where a part may relocate to**, and this row's adjacency-licensing stops being purely read-side structure. **PQ-12** carries the fork. Note the asymmetry: on the read side adjacency *licenses* a connection that must still be explicit; on the write side it would *restrict* a move, which is a stronger use of the same relation and should not be assumed to follow from it. **Candidate home found 2026-08-06, and it is *below* the rooms rather than above them --- [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings).** Part-connections become **sibling links inside the whole's own graph**, so this row no longer needs an Area-level carrier, which deflates AB-25 in turn (Area was largely being summoned to host things above rooms). **One loose end, recorded as PQ-8:** these edges are labeled by the *licensing spatial relation*, which is exterior world truth --- so a sibling link sits inside the rope while its label points outward, and AB-28's floor-hole repair now has to reach a dependent link buried one level deeper. Ports do not help there. **Title and premise stale as of [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), 2026-08-10; findings intact.** Under locked-frame clause 1 a whole's parts are nodes in **its** graph and are never on different hosts --- the *whole* has ports into different hosts. **The question this row actually answers is how sibling parts inside one whole record the exterior relation licensing their connection**, which is what its own candidate home and PQ-8 already describe. **Rename before graduating**, and see the same correction applied harder at **AB-27**, whose central distinction does not survive it. | AB-8, AB-11, AB-16, AB-25, AB-28, AB-34 | **Open --- shape settled, rules proposed; candidate home inside the whole (PQ-8 open); title reframed 2026-08-10** |
| **AB-27** | **Composition edges look host-relative, and only one half is new.** Observed 2026-08-05 while resolving AB-26. **Parts sharing a host** (a flashlight's battery, bulb, casing) can relate through the **existing** intra-host relational vocabulary --- shipped and working. **Parts on different hosts** (rope segments) cannot, because `applyStepSequenceCore` derives a relational step's shared host and **throws** on mismatch; those need AB-26's adjacency-mediated connection. So the genuinely new edge kind is required **exactly and only where the current model already refuses to go**, which suggests a real joint rather than a parallel mechanism. Decide whether these are two edge kinds or one kind with two validity rules, and what happens at the boundary --- when a co-located assembly is pulled apart across hosts, does a same-host relational edge **become** an adjacency-mediated connection, and is that a migration or a dissolve-and-recreate? **Mostly dissolved 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) --- this row's central distinction is a pre-lock premise.** Locked-frame clause 1 puts every part of a whole in the whole's own graph, so **there is no parts-on-different-hosts case**: `applyStepSequenceCore`'s throw-on-host-mismatch never fires for parts of one whole, and the joint this row locates at the host boundary is not there. The closing question describes an event that does not occur --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) established there is no whole-migration under ports, only a port whose egress changes. **What survives is narrower and worth keeping:** do the sibling links inside a whole reuse the shipped intra-host relational vocabulary, or do they need their own kind? | AB-4, AB-16, AB-26 | ~~**Open**~~ **Open --- narrowed 2026-08-10 to whether interior sibling links reuse the shipped relational vocabulary; the cross-host half dissolved with its premise** |
| **AB-28** | **Licensing relations are mutable --- and this is the *shipped* boundary-edge discipline, not a new problem.** Found 2026-08-05 as a consequence of AB-26 admitting improvised adjacency: repair a floor-hole and the relation licensing a rope threaded through it **disappears**. **Recognized the same day as an instance of an existing pattern** --- *a mutation that would sever a relationship is not legal until the severing is made explicit.* `removeObject`/`removeCharacter` assert-and-throw on residual relational edges (`RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError`, BD-33/35) with deliberately **no** silent-strip variant; [`boundaryEdgeOutcomes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) classifies; expansion classifies and the compiler sequences `dissolveRelation` steps **ahead of** the mutation. **The three candidate outcomes are the existing verdict channels:** "you cannot seal it" is `error`, "sealing severs the rope" is `dissolve`, "can you seal a hole with a rope through it?" is `defer`. **Carries over unchanged:** assert-and-throw over silent strip; classify-before-legal; commit-time re-validation on locked graphs; severance narrated in step order via `factsForStep`. **Three real differences (below) and one bifurcation (AB-29).** **A fourth difference, and this row was the last to hear about it --- surfaced 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10).** Under locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 the link that the floor-hole repair must sever is a **sibling link inside the rope's own graph**, labeled by the exterior Exit --- so discovery has to reach from an exterior relation to an interior dependent, and the shipped `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` classifier operates on same-host edges. **AB-26 recorded this in 2026-08-09** (*"AB-28's floor-hole repair now has to reach a dependent link buried one level deeper. Ports do not help there"*) and it never reached this row. **Whether that reach is merely harder or outright illegal is [the open question about clause 2's direction](#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction)**, on which this row, AB-26, AB-36 and PQ-8 all depend. | AB-17, AB-26, AB-27, AB-29 | **Open --- pattern identified, extensions open; the interior-dependent case added 2026-08-10 and gated on locked-frame clause 2's direction** |
| **AB-29** | **Extending the boundary-edge discipline to licensing relations: three gaps and a bifurcation.** **(1) Discovery runs backwards.** Today: remove a *node*, find edges incident to it --- local, on a graph already held. Here: remove a *relation*, find the part-connections referencing it. That is a reverse lookup from a relation to its dependents, and **nothing indexes it**. It is the parked reverse-edge-reference index on its **third** appearance in this initiative, which makes it the most load-bearing parked item so far. **Fourth appearance 2026-08-06 ([P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s PQ-6), at which point it was split out as its own row --- AB-36 --- and promoted from parked to prerequisite.** **(2) The footprint reaches a third host.** Sealing a hole in Room A must lock Room A, Room B (the rope's far end), *and* the Area carrying the license --- all discoverable at plan time, since `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once and cannot be re-entered. **(3) The mutation may not route through the positions kernel at all** --- "repair the hole" is not a `transferMembership`. **The bifurcation, and it is the important part:** **play-driven** license removal has an operation to block or transform, so the shipped discipline applies directly and the conflict is **content**; **asset-withdrawn** license (an overlay deactivates and the adjacency it authored vanishes) has **no operation to make illegal** --- nobody did anything --- and needs the **eviction ladder's** trim-and-repair posture instead, one layer over. Only the first half is the existing pattern. **A third branch, added 2026-08-09 from [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about): asset-*added*.** An improvised negative established in play (*there is no deep fryer*) contradicted by a later authored edit that adds one. **Neither existing posture reaches it** --- there is no operation to make illegal, so the boundary-edge discipline has nothing to act on, and nothing is being *withdrawn*, so the eviction ladder's trim-and-repair has nothing to trim. **And the mechanism the bifurcation would otherwise inherit does not apply either:** layered merge resolves conflicts between layers *of a component that exists*, but existence is not merge output (improvised objects exist by pair row plus placement, authored ones by asset participation), so **a negative fact has no component to be a layer of** and the participation stack cannot rank the two claims. **This is a clearer instance of the play-versus-asset conflict than the licensing-relation cases that opened this row** --- those are second-order consequences of an edit; this is a direct contradiction between two assertions about one fact. Note the mirror direction is the easy one and should not be conflated: an improvised *positive* contradicted by an authored edit leaves a component to evict, which is branch 2 behaving normally. | AB-26, AB-28 | **Open --- third branch (asset-added) opened 2026-08-09, no candidate mechanism** |
| **AB-30** | **Addressable but not a member: what makes something a graph participant at all?** Opened 2026-08-05 out of a retired falsification case (see [Working hypotheses](#working-hypotheses)). A wall is not *in* a room, it **bounds** rooms --- so asking which host contains it is a category error, and H1's "host at the node containing all its parts" has no purchase because containment is not the relation. This is why a wall cannot migrate down; **not** because it is born spanning. **Two corpus cases need it addressable, from opposite directions, within one session:** [C1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c1-look-at-the-character-standing-here-look-at-the-north-wall) needs the north wall *referrable* ("look at the north wall") and [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) needs it *relation-targetable* (the chain's more interesting end mounts to it, and is currently unrepresentable). Neither needs it to be a member of anything. **That is a fourth answer AB-2's node / host / projection trichotomy does not offer**, and it is independent of AB-12: whichever way the privileged-level reframe goes, the criterion for what belongs on the graph still needs one. **Candidate answer already scaffolded:** [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#fractal-position-graphs-container-scale-and-edges) notes Area edge kinds beyond Exit may carry **non-traversable spatial facts**, so a wall may be better modelled as an **edge** than a node --- which would *explain* the documented Feature narrowing (never membership-moved, never a relation subject, possibly a relation target) rather than asserting it. **The discriminator, and it is the sharpest test available: a wall separating two rooms.** Under the **edge** answer, spanning is free --- an edge between two rooms is inherently about both, and nothing needs a host. Under the **node** answer, the wall must exist in two graphs, which is exactly the multi-host problem H1 abolished for Objects, now needing per-graph duplication and a sync story --- and it would make [P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node)'s "Features stay multi-host" sub-proposal an unremarked asymmetry rather than a decision. **No separate row for spanning Features:** the spanning case is this row's test, not its own question. **Re-framed 2026-08-10 toward a third answer the row could not state when it was written: the wall is a `PartOf` the room.** **Why the option was unavailable, not overlooked.** This row's founding claim --- *a wall is not **in** a room, it **bounds** rooms, so asking which host contains it is a category error* --- was **correct**, and drove straight to the edge answer because on 2026-08-05 the model had no containment vocabulary **at all**. `PartOf` became sayable on 2026-08-09 (**AB-48**). **The category error was against `In`; it is not an error against `PartOf`.** Reached as the re-check this row was flagged for on [locked-frame clause 3's dependent set](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09). **The parallel holds on the register's own columns:** *intensional* --- room-ness implies walls as crate-ness implies a lid, where it implies no crowbar; *coarsening sound* --- "mounted to the room's north wall" -> "…to the room", the same shape as "the bag's strap" -> "the bag"; *shard-defining* is **vacuous rather than failed**, since rooms do not move. **It also *explains* the documented Feature narrowing rather than asserting it** --- parts move only with their whole, so *never membership-moved* follows --- which is the bar this row and AB-12 both set for a good answer. **Superseded 2026-08-10, and the bar turned out not to apply: the narrowing is an affordance rule, not a representation rule** (see **AB-12**), so there was never a documented decision here to explain *or* contradict. **The wall needs no exception, because the graph layer imposes no Feature restriction.** The one guard worth keeping --- stopping carry-style absorption from moving scenery --- lives at **AB-41** as *ephemeral wholes may not absorb a Feature*, which is a rule about an **operation** rather than about which edges may exist. **The decisive argument is against the edge candidate, and this row never ran it: the edge answer cannot satisfy [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends).** C5 needs the wall **relation-targetable** --- a chain mounts to it --- and if the wall is an edge between rooms, that requires **an edge terminating on an edge**, a capability nothing in this design has or has proposed. **This row lists C5 as one of its two driving requirements and never checked its own favoured answer against it.** Under wall-as-part the wall is a node and an ordinary endpoint. **The discriminator resolves too, because its cost was priced under a model that is gone.** *A wall separating two rooms* was charged to the node answer as *"per-graph duplication and a sync story"* --- true under H1. Under **AB-8**'s (a)-with-ports framing the wall is **listed** in two room graphs with exactly **one wall graph**: the node appears twice, the interior lives once, *"closer to a presence index than to real multi-hosting."* No duplication, and the port list is the sync story. **What it inherits rather than resolves: [H3 clause 6](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice).** Two-room parenthood is *licensed* by the DAG (AB-13; [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer) is the precedent) but H3 clause 6 is **Open with no mechanism** for how a part records which of several wholes claims it. **Licensed is not accounted-for** --- the wall does not get that mechanism for free, though it needs building regardless. **Two cautions.** (i) **This is AB-12's part (b)**, on locked-frame clause 3's exclusion list as *open, not rejected* --- so supplying evidence for it is the correct route, but **Feature-as-part-of-Room must not drag rooms-nesting-in-rooms with it**; a room *having* parts is directionally different from a room *being* a part, and roles-not-kinds cuts slightly against that separation, so decide it rather than assume it. (ii) **The provenance seam**, which is where (b)'s risk always lived: walls arrive by authored asset-stack merge, so a room's part-set is authored and improvised parts must stay distinguishable across re-merge --- **AB-29**, already required by [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about), so the same machinery rather than new. **One cost, and it is the edge answer's only remaining advantage: adjacency.** *These two rooms are separated by this wall* is **stated** under wall-as-edge and **derived** from shared parenthood under wall-as-part --- a reachability query rather than a lookup. Likely small, since Exits already carry traversable adjacency and a wall is non-traversable by definition, so the two do not compete --- but **AB-26** owns adjacency licensing and should look. | AB-2, AB-8, AB-12, AB-13, AB-26, AB-29, AB-48, H3 clause 6 | ~~**Open --- candidate answer recorded, discriminator named**~~ **Open --- re-framed 2026-08-10 toward wall-as-`PartOf`; edge candidate found unable to satisfy C5, and the discriminator's cost dissolved by AB-8's re-framing. Not closed:** gated on H3 clause 6's unbuilt shared-part mechanism and on AB-12(b) being decided on its own evidence |
| **AB-31** | **Fission: one whole becomes two, and no row owns it.** Opened 2026-08-05 from [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer), whose entire payoff is the rope **burning through**. The lifecycle cluster has minting (AB-5), reabsorption (AB-17) and decay (AB-6); AB-16 remarks in passing that "cutting a rope makes two ropes" but treats it as a property of homogeneous extent rather than as an operation. **Fission is neither minting nor the inverse of reabsorption** --- it is sideways, and under [H1](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) with stored member sets and parent indices it is a concrete multi-step write: mint a second whole, **repartition** the member set, rewrite the parent pointers of the moved parts, and recompute **both** hosts (which may migrate in opposite directions --- in C6 the severed lower length falls into the garden and becomes single-room, while the upper remnant becomes single-room too, so *both* halves migrate *down* out of the Area as a consequence of one event). **Questions this row owns:** does fission mint one new whole or two and retire the original --- and does the answer matter for identity (AB-21) and for anything holding a reference? What happens to a **third** whole whose member set straddles the cut, as C6's impromptu timer does? Is fission always player-caused, or can decay produce it? Does the severed pair retain any relation recording that they were once one thing? **Note the footprint problem is acute here:** both resulting hosts, plus every host of every affected co-parent, must be known at plan time, and `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once. ~~**Partly answered 2026-08-06 by P3**: "does anything holding a reference survive?" --- under ports, **yes by construction**.~~ **Overstated, corrected the same day under [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).** Ports make **interior repartitioning** non-breaking, not **identity changes** --- and fission is an identity change. When the rope becomes `ROPE-upper` and `ROPE-lower`, an external edge naming `OBJECT#ROPE:1` must be rewritten to `OBJECT#ROPE-upper:1`, because the object it names no longer exists. **That is true with or without ports**, so this row gains nothing on reference survival. **Contrast AB-17, where the payoff is real** precisely because reabsorption is interior-only. **What ports do give this row is the footprint half** --- the reverse of what was recorded: a whole's port list enumerates every host referencing it, computable at plan time, which is what `computeStepSequenceFootprint` running once demands. **One uncorrected layer found 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10):** this row's concrete write sketch --- *mint a second whole, repartition the member set, rewrite the parent pointers of the moved parts* --- is scoped **"under H1"** in its own words, H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07, and AB-31 was not among the [rolled-back](#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07) rows. **Under locked-frame clause 1 fission is *split one graph into two, each with a root, and re-bind ports*; there are no parent pointers and no member-set repartition.** The row's corrected port findings currently sit on top of that uncorrected sketch, which is more misleading than either state alone. | AB-5, AB-6, AB-13, AB-16, AB-17, AB-21, AB-36 | **Open --- footprint answered under P3; reference survival *not* answered, earlier claim withdrawn; write sketch still H1-era and owed a restatement under locked-frame clause 1** |
| **AB-32** | **What must the composition representation be able to *express*?** Split out of AB-4 on 2026-08-06, which was asking only what the representation *is*. **This is not a new question --- it is the answerable half of an existing one**, and separating them matters practically: requirements can be settled from the fiction with confidence, representation cannot, and one row asking both left the confident half unreachable. **First requirement, answered the same day: yes, a whole can be part of a bigger whole.** Abstractions must ladder up *and* down --- resoundingly, not marginally --- which is the requirement behind AB-0's *Abstraction Fractal* naming. **Note what that does and does not settle.** It does **not** force composition into graph edges: a record-based whole holds a member set of ids, an id can be another whole's, parent index points up and member set points down, so nesting is free and [the non-positional-whole candidate](#candidate-drop-h1-clause-1b-and-the-whole-chain-with-it) survives it intact. An earlier framing of that candidate named wholes-nest as its decider; **that framing made this very conflation** --- nesting is the requirement, edges-versus-record is the representation. **The actual decider, restated as a requirement: must a relation ever attach to a *whole* rather than to one of its parts?** Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes. Every case so far says no --- [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends)'s chain relates through its ends, [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s candle sits under a span, and that is the third decomposition trigger doing exactly this work --- but **"the place setting is on the table" is the shape that might not decompose**, and it should be tested rather than assumed. **Second requirement, answered 2026-08-06 and consequential: reference and perception must reach a spanning whole and a local whole by the *same mechanism*.** "The thread in room B" and "the flashlight on the table" must not resolve by different machinery, or the difference propagates into every consumer. **This is the sharpest pressure H1 clause 1b has taken**, and it produced [three positions with their prices](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) plus a narrowed dichotomy --- *single-hosting or lookup-as-the-clean-rule, not both* --- and the finding that **the plan is currently in the losing position by accident.** Note the shape of the win: an argument from requirements settled more than four constructed scenarios did, which is the case for this row existing. **Other requirements to collect here:** must a whole be describable without enumerating its parts; must a part be manipulable without loading its whole; must the representation record *where along* an extent something attaches (yes --- C7); must a relation ever attach to a whole rather than a part (above). **Third requirement, answered 2026-08-06 and it changes the decider's force: a relation must be able to attach to a *part* without that part being a graph node.** [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation)'s part-addressed endpoints supply it. Note the interaction with the decider above --- "relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes" assumed endpoints are node references. If endpoints may be **addresses**, then even "the place setting is on the table" does not force the place setting to be a node, and the decider weakens from settling the question to merely pressuring it. **Re-test the decider under P2 before relying on it.** **Retired 2026-08-09 rather than re-tested: the decider cannot discriminate.** See [the whole/part dissolution](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). *"Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes"* has no force once every whole is a node by the locked frame's clause 1 --- the requirement can come back either way and the representation does not move. **The row half-caught this itself** (the appended note that the decider *"assumed endpoints are node references"*) and did not follow through. **Both answered requirements stand untouched**; what is retired is the unanswered decider, not the row. | AB-1, AB-4, AB-13 | **Open --- three requirements answered, collecting the rest; the fourth-listed decider retired 2026-08-09 as non-discriminating** |
| **AB-33** | **Does the host link become a real, labeled edge --- and does the host become a node in its own graph?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation). Today membership is containment in a collection: `hostId` is a **scalar sibling** of `nodes`, so the implicit *contents-of* link is not merely unlabeled but **untypeable** --- `EphemeraPositionRelationalEdgeData`'s endpoints are both `EphemeraObjectId`, and the host has no node to be one. **Evidence that this was a chosen shape rather than an oversight:** the link is already materialized as `EphemeraPositionAdjacencyRow` = `{ EphemeraId, DataCategory: 'POSITION#' + hostId }` --- an edge record with a from, a to, and no third column. **Two things ride on this row.** (i) It is **AB-3**'s pre-registered trigger arriving from the hosting side: a host node is the root `EphemeraPositionGraph` lacks, and `CarryClosureFragment` collapses into it per its own pinned note. (ii) There is **no containment relation kind at all** --- `HostRelationalEdgeKind` is `'On' \| 'Under' \| 'Against' \| 'Custom'`, so the model cannot say "in" about anything, and **AB-4 is being asked in a vocabulary with no word for its answer.** **Distinguish two readings before deciding, because only one is cheap:** the host becomes a **node inside its own host-bound graph** (storage untouched, `MultiKeyUpdate`'s `transactWrite` unit intact, graph gains a root), versus a **global edge space where edges cross hosts** (breaks the same-host-incident-edges limitation, the `removeObject` assert-and-throw discipline, and the sharding key itself). P2 argues only the first. **Narrowed 2026-08-09: the requirements half is split out as [AB-48](#open-decisions-design--plan-only), and this row keeps representation only.** **Why the split was needed, stated as a criticism of this row rather than an addition to it: every evaluative move above is indexed to current code, and none to requirements.** *Chosen shape rather than an oversight* is a deference argument from the adjacency row; *only one is cheap* prices both readings by what breaks. **No axis is named anywhere in it.** **The specific error: mutation is the only axis this row argues on, and mutation is a veto rather than a ranking** --- [the axis table](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to) is explicit that it is *"not a benefit flexibility delivers; it is a constraint flexibility must survive."* So the cost analysis validly eliminates the global-edge-space reading and does **no** work recommending the status quo. A row can only be decided on a veto in the negative. **Deciding whether the code should *change* is a stronger claim than observing that a proposal *already matches* it, and this row was making only the second.** **The clean/dirty test run 2026-08-09, which this row never applied and which reframes it.** Per [the frame](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation), a deviation is legitimate dirt iff it can be stated as *"the clean answer, computed faster"*; otherwise it is a model change wearing dirt's clothing --- and **deliberate is not the test**, so *chosen rather than overlooked* does not settle it. **Note what the locked frame already fixed:** the clean model **has** the root node (locked-frame clause 1), and since [whole and host are the same construct](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09) that is a claim about hosts. So the question was never *is the widening worth it*; it is **is the scalar `hostId` dirt or drift.** **Answer: both, because the field is doing two jobs** --- P2's own *one construct doing two jobs* diagnosis arriving a second time, one level down, about the field instead of the construct. (a) **As "which node in `nodes` is the root" it is legitimate dirt**, and clearly so: the clean answer is derivable (the unique node with no incoming containment edge) and storing it computes that faster, which is the test passed on its face. (b) **As "which collection this graph belongs to" it is drift** --- an untypeable link with no node to be an endpoint, which is exactly P2's phantom. **Same field, and the two readings point in opposite directions:** out of the graph today, into `nodes` under the root reading. **The shipped code next door has already made the choice, with the right name.** `EphemeraPositionGraphData` is `{ hostId, nodes, edges? }` and [`CarryClosureFragment`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) is `{ rootId, members, edges }` --- **the same three-field shape, differing only in whether the identifier names something inside the collection or outside it** --- and the carry-closure form, which AB-3's pinned note says collapses into the root concept, already points inward and already calls the field `rootId`. **Consequence for what this row is deciding: the cheap reading does not remove `hostId`, it re-types and renames it** to a root pointer into `nodes`. That is materially smaller than "widen the model," and it is the shape [Phase 5 candidate (b)](#recommended-order) is already gated on. **Resolved 2026-08-09, later the same day, by [locked-frame clause 3 of the locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06):** every `positionGraph` has one root node, in `nodes`, relation-endpoint-capable, with the graph designating which. **The host does become a node in its own graph, uniformly across host kinds** --- this row's cheap reading, generalized. **The expensive reading is declined, not deferred:** a global edge space crossing hosts remains rejected on the mutation veto, which is the one thing this row's cost analysis was always good for. **What the row asked in its title is now split across two places:** *does the host become a node* is locked; *does the host link become a real labeled edge* is **AB-48**, since the containment kind that edge would carry does not exist. | AB-2, AB-3, AB-4, AB-12, AB-30, AB-34, AB-48 | ~~**Open --- newly named; defect verified against code**~~ ~~**Open --- representation only (2026-08-09), blocked on AB-48**~~ **Locked 2026-08-09 as locked-frame clause 3** --- host-as-node uniform across host kinds; global-edge-space reading declined on the mutation veto; the labeled-edge half handed to **AB-48**. **AB-3's root-node trigger is discharged by this**, on its fourth and fifth arrivals |
| **AB-34** | **Making addressing work in all directions: ports on the host boundary.** Opened 2026-08-06 as the half [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) clause 3 does not settle. Part-addressed endpoints (`rope: rope-end` in a `tied to` edge) work cleanly **within** a host: the rope is a node in the room, the tie attaches to one of its ends, and no part-node is minted. **The cross-host case is open** --- if a spanning whole hosts once, an edge in another room addressing one of its segments has an endpoint outside that room's graph, which is exactly the traffic P2 clause 1 forbids. **Candidate on the table, not yet assessed: ports.** A host object exposes named ports living on its **boundary**, whose job is to connect outside references to inside addresses --- so an outside edge terminates at a port rather than reaching through, and the host stays authoritative over what its interior exposes. **Two properties to check first:** whether ports are declared or derived (a declared port is authored surface area; a derived one re-poses AB-6's stored-versus-derived question one layer down), and whether a port is itself addressable as a part, since a yes makes the scheme recursive and a no makes it a special kind. **The failure mode to test against is the one P2 already flagged**: an address with no declaration is the phantom edge one scale down, and ports exist precisely to give the address a declared home. **Provisional answer recorded 2026-08-06 as [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)**, worked far enough to argue against, with **nine open sub-questions PQ-1..PQ-9** carried there rather than duplicated here. **Both properties this row said to check first are now among them:** declared-versus-derived is **PQ-9**, and is-a-port-addressable-as-a-part is **PQ-5** (leaning yes, making the scheme recursive and requiring a cycle guard). **The blocking pair is PQ-6 / PQ-7** --- egress discovery is unindexed, and without it `removeObject`'s assert-and-throw stops being enforceable across a port. **Status corrected 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), which found it stale on three counts** --- and this is the row the whole PQ table hangs from, so a stale cell here mis-sets the reader's picture of the entire port scheme. (i) P3's two **shape** claims are [locked](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06), not provisional; only its calibration is. (ii) **PQ-6** deflated 2026-08-06 (its own cell reads *no longer the blocking item*) and **PQ-7** was restated to not-open the same day, so the named blocking pair is neither. (iii) The sub-questions now number **fourteen**, not nine. | AB-4, AB-26, AB-27, AB-32, AB-33, AB-36 | ~~**Provisionally answered by [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings); nine sub-questions open, PQ-6/PQ-7 blocking**~~ **Shape claims [locked](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06); calibration provisional under [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings); fourteen sub-questions, none blocking --- PQ-13 is the one flagged as needing a decision before anything downstream** |
| **AB-35** | **`positionCache`: what is derived, what invalidates it, and what stops it becoming a second truth?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) clause 2 --- a denormalized read structure holding expanded graphs across multiple host nodes, so that host-nesting does not cost a read per level. **Not documented anywhere today; this is new design, not recall of an existing decision.** **One rule proposed with local precedent: the write path never touches the cache --- invalidate-and-rederive only.** [`mtw-wml`'s schema README](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/documentation/schema/README.md) records this codebase backing out of denormalizing child data upward as "an enormous source of unnecessary complexity," replaced by **selectors** computing condensed views from the source-of-truth tree. The analogy is limited --- that was authored source, not a runtime cache --- but the discipline transfers exactly, and without it the cache becomes a second truth and the phantom returns as a coherence problem one layer up. **Open sub-questions:** what the invalidation unit is (a host? a subtree? a shard?), whether expansion is eager or on-demand, how it interacts with `internalCache.Positions` which already wraps `get`/`set` at the ephemera boundary, and whether an expanded view is ever *written through* for a read-modify-write --- which the rule forbids and something will eventually want to do. ~~**Scope clarified 2026-08-06, and a second site now exists:** P3's ingress port list is derived data placed deliberately inside `positionGraph`, so the plan has two derived-data sites governed differently.~~ **Withdrawn the same day --- the ingress list moved into this cache, so there is exactly one derived-data site and one rule.** **What the episode leaves behind is worth keeping, though, because the general principle was stated and then not needed:** *derived data may live in truth when the read path demands it, provided a conflict rule and a repair story both exist* --- true, matching the shipped `Meta`-placement dual write, and the reason it did not apply here is that **the read path turned out not to demand it.** **A concrete gain for this row: the ingress list is now its first named consumer**, and it comes with a shape --- an object's live ports in a room are exactly those whose egress host is that room, so this entry is repaired by **recompute, not merge**. That is a materially easier invalidation story than the general case and worth designing the cache's contract around rather than treating as a special case. **A sub-question this row did not have, added 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) --- and it is the one finding that sweep produced that is *new* rather than stale.** This cache is specified as holding **expanded graphs across multiple host nodes**. **An expansion that crosses a port boundary is exactly what locked-frame clause 2 buys encapsulation to prevent, arriving as a performance measure rather than as a decision** --- and the lock's [own recorded risk](#what-the-lock-delivered-for-scale-invariance-and-where-it-stops-assessed-2026-08-06) is that external code cannot see what scale a thing is at, so *"the failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained."* **A flattening cache is the mechanism by which that happens silently. So: does expansion stop at a port --- and if it does not, what preserves the caller's knowledge that it crossed one?** | AB-6, AB-33, AB-34 | **Open --- invariant intact; one site; first concrete consumer named; port-boundary sub-question added 2026-08-10** |
| **AB-36** | **The reverse edge-reference index, promoted from parked to prerequisite.** Opened 2026-08-06 on its **fourth** independent appearance in this initiative --- AB-29 counted three and treated it as "the most load-bearing parked item so far"; [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s **PQ-6** is the fourth, and it is the one that makes the item blocking rather than expensive. **The need, stated generally: nothing in the system can find the referrers of a thing.** Membership adjacency indexes **node** placement (`getMembershipContainers`), not **edge-only** references on other hosts --- documented as the "same-host incident edges only" limitation in [`positionGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/AGENT.md), whose parked remedy is "a post-clear sweep of Coyote `gameRooms` graphs, or a reverse edge-reference index." **The four consumers, which want the same index for four unrelated reasons:** (1) stale Exit edges after take-hold, the original; (2) AB-29's licensing-relation removal, where discovery runs backwards from a relation to its dependents; (3) AB-31's fission, where every co-parent's host must be known at plan time and `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once; (4) PQ-6's egress discovery. **Why the fourth changes its status: it takes a shipped safety property with it.** `removeObject` / `removeCharacter` assert-and-throw on residual relational edges with deliberately no silent-strip variant (BD-33/35) --- enforceable today only because edges are same-host and locally discoverable. A cross-host port reference breaks that precondition, so **PQ-7 is not a new risk but the loss of an existing guarantee**, and it is downstream of this row. ~~**Four independent needs is the argument; do not re-derive it a fifth time.**~~ **Demoted 2026-08-06, one exchange after being promoted, and the count that justified the promotion was wrong by half.** [Single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) name the graph they exit to, so a whole's port list **is** a reverse index for its own referrers --- **which serves two of the four consumers**: PQ-6 (directly) and AB-31's fission footprint (which needs exactly "every host referencing this whole"). **The other two are untouched and remain the real content of this row:** stale Exit edges after take-hold, and AB-29's backwards discovery from a licensing relation to its dependents --- neither is a port case, and no port list helps either. **The lesson is about the argument, not the index:** "four independent appearances" was doing the work of a proof, and a design change halved it overnight. A recurrence count is evidence that something is needed, never evidence about *what*. **Its second surviving consumer got harder, noted 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10):** AB-29's backwards discovery from a licensing relation to its dependents must now reach a sibling link **inside a whole's graph**, one level deeper than when this row was written. **Whether it may reach there at all is [the open question about clause 2's direction](#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction)** --- if the strict reading holds, this consumer needs a mediating construct nobody has proposed, and the index alone would not be enough. | AB-28, AB-29, AB-31, AB-34 | **Open --- real but no longer blocking; two consumers, not four; the surviving pair aggravated 2026-08-10 by locked-frame clause 2** |

Rename before graduating; see [the whole/part dissolution](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). Opened 2026-08-07 from [C7's nesting question](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07). A nested whole gives an LLM a fact at the right scale --- *a long thread winding through many rooms* --- **sufficient** for questions the flat form can only answer by re-deriving continuity from twenty constituents. **The comparison did not price the summary's upkeep, and that is this row.** Authored, it goes stale the moment the thread changes; derived, the derivation has moved to write time rather than gone away. **This is the mutation axis charging for a reasoning gain** --- the first cross-axis cost [the frame](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to) has surfaced, and the reason the frame was worth writing. **Distinct from AB-6**, which asks stored-vs-derived about *member sets*; a summary is a different object with a different invalidation shape, and conflating them would hide that. **Two things already known.** (1) **By [the strength test](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06) a summary is N:1 and therefore *strong*** --- it saves reading every constituent, the same class as the membership node and unlike the ingress list that got moved to cache.
