(2) **Sufficiency is question-relative**, so there is no single summary: *a long thread winding through many rooms* answers *is it still attached* and not *can I climb it*. **That is the row's hard part**, and it is why [the structural half of C7's finding --- relations porting through the boundary --- is load-bearing and the curated summary is not](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07): porting generalises across questions and prose does not. **Do not design the summary before deciding whether porting alone suffices.** **Narrowed 2026-08-07 by [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork) / AB-40(b):** a second, independent case (a place setting losing and regaining a part) reaches for the same stored-vs-derived answer C7 and AB-6's contested note both pointed at --- **settling**, via P4's topology-change rule, neither fully stored nor recomputed at read time. But AB-14 also uses **Improvisation** for the **downward, operation-driven trigger**, paired with **Recognition** as the upward one. **The word does both jobs** --- the same conflation AB-14 was restructured to remove --- and if Improvisation is the mechanism then Improvisation and Recognition are **not peers**: Recognition is a trigger that invokes it, and operational necessity is the other trigger. **Two ways out, unchanged:** **(a)** Improvisation and Recognition stay the two triggers and the shared mechanism takes its own name; **(b)** Improvisation names the mechanism and the downward trigger is renamed. **Why it did not ride out on AB-0's lock, deliberately:** the corpus evidence that settled the concept name (no privileged level, roles not kinds) bears on *levels*, not on *triggers*, so nothing that resolved AB-0 touches this. The row's live residue, **PS-1** and **PS-3**, is about the reachability mechanism, not the cascade's staging, and stays open at any iteration depth. **Do not close it into P4** --- P4-iteration-1 is now an accepted Prototype rather than an unpromoted proposal, but it is still a bet with code behind it and a [named rollback trigger](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07); if it fires, this question returns intact |
| **AB-40** | **The detach/reattach step kind --- and the row split in two, 2026-08-07.** Opened from [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork). **(a) The structural move.** A part's node leaving `PlaceSetting`'s interior graph and landing in the character's --- this must stay **synchronous**: a player needs to reference and use the fork the instant it is taken, so deferring the relocation to eventual consistency would be a real gameplay defect, not a modelling nicety. **Reframed twice now.** First, narrower than initially stated: not a new step *kind*, but whether the existing transfer step can address a node inside another object's interior graph as a source or destination at all. **Second, 2026-08-07: not an open design question at all.** There is no candidate answer being weighed against another --- AB-11's Expansion answer was never a competing proposal for this, it simply doesn't reach here. This is scoped, known-needed, unbuilt functionality, which is what [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) exists to track and [Phase 5](#recommended-order) exists to schedule --- not a fork for this table to carry. **(b) The consequence.** What `PlaceSetting`'s own summary says about itself once the fork is gone, and the reverse when it returns --- **answered, in direction if not in detail, by [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s topology-change rule**: the `part` edge's deletion emits its own impulse (*you lost a member*) exactly as a cut cable does in C12, and a settling wave, not a synchronous step, is what eventually regenerates the summary. **This does not need to be fast** --- the character already knows they hold the fork; the room description catching up half a beat later is what settling exists for. | **AB-41** | **Ephemeral / virtual abstractions.** Opened 2026-08-07 from [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up). Construct an H3-shaped whole (root, parts, ports) **in memory, for the duration of one operation, never written to the graph** --- the same computation `computeCarryClosure` already does, retyped as AB-3's already-anticipated collapse rather than a bespoke fragment. **Answers AB-18's "a carry closure?" scope candidate with a concrete trigger**: the operation itself (pick up), not arrangement detection. **Dissolves the vacuous-abstraction threshold question C14 opened with**: constructing the ephemeral form is always cheap and safe, so there is no firing threshold to tune --- the open question moves entirely to *promotion* (ephemeral to durable), which C14 argues is [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s narrative heat's job (iteration 3, not built) rather than a new mechanism. **Distinct from AB-30**: AB-30's wall is addressable-never-a-member *permanently*, as an edge; this is addressable-never-a-member *for one operation's duration*, as a whole. **Noted, not developed:** the same construct plausibly serves [C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)'s reasoning-context packaging problem, a use on the reasoning axis rather than the mutation axis this row was opened from. **Clause added 2026-08-10: an ephemeral whole may not absorb a Feature, and this row is where the Feature narrowing's guard belongs.** **The loophole it closes, and it is specific to *ephemeral* wholes:** *"features move only with their whole"* is trivially defeated by **wrapping a whole around the feature, moving the whole, and dissolving it.** A **durable** whole containing a mountain is a modelling act someone performed and is separately governed; an **ephemeral** one is minted per-operation, for free, by the engine --- so the exploit is mechanical rather than authorial, and the guard belongs at the mint. **Behaviour: boundary, not error.** When absorption reaches a Feature the Feature stays put and the edge to it is handled by `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` (`dissolve`/`carry`/`defer`) rather than throwing --- reusing shipped discipline instead of adding a failure mode, and letting *take the book off the wall-mounted shelf* simply work with the shelf left behind. **Two representation-layer rules were considered for this job and both declined, because they guard the wrong layer.** **(A) *Features are never the subject of a spatial relation.*** Faithful to the mechanism --- `computeCarryClosure` absorbs the **subject** of an `On` edge when the target moves (`classify('On','target') = 'carry'`, `otherId = edge.from`) --- but it restricts **edge legality** to prevent an **operation**, and its fidelity is contingent on the transfer table, so a future kind that carries subjects differently would silently break it. **(B) *Features may be subjects only toward Room / Area / Feature targets.*** Declined on a sharper failure: **mobility is a per-instance fact, not a kind fact.** A ship's cabin is a `Room` and moves; a boulder is an `Object` and does not. B **permits exactly what it exists to prevent** in the moving-room case, which makes it a lossy proxy --- [a model change wearing dirt's clothing](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) rather than the clean answer computed faster. **The accounting that actually separated them, recorded because the first pass got it wrong:** both proposals carry the *same shape* of dirt --- a partition plus a rule naming one component kind --- so counting enumerations discriminates nothing, and *"A adds no kind enumeration"* was simply false. **The test is fidelity, not quantity**, and on fidelity A was exact-but-fragile and B lossy. **Both are moot under this clause**, which needs neither partition: the graph stays uniform and the guard sits at the one site that absorbs. **What this clause does not close --- the second hinge.** An LLM-proposed mutation could improvise a **durable** whole around the mountain and move that; no ephemeral abstraction is involved, so this gate never fires. **That is the P5/licence surface and it stays open** --- the ephemeral gate closes the *mechanical* exploit, not the *reasoning* one, and must not be cited as a complete answer. **Convenient landing:** this row is [Phase 5 candidate (b)](#recommended-order), so the gate is exercised in the first slice built, and it is safely **additive** against that slice's acceptance bar (*today's carry-closure coverage passes unchanged*) --- Features are not graph nodes today, so no existing test can cover Feature absorption and the gate cannot regress the bar it is judged against. | AB-3, AB-6, AB-18, AB-30, H3 clause 3, P4 | **Open --- newly named; construction (via H3's whole shape) has a clear answer, promotion criterion is deferred to P4 iteration 3 and not yet actionable** |
| **AB-42** | **Are established negatives ludic-graph nodes, or a collection beside the graph?** Opened 2026-08-09 from conversation, following [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about), which established that the **negative record is the one thing storage lacks** --- established-present mints rows and *not established* is correctly represented by nothing, so this row is the whole of the storage question. **Two candidates. (a) A negative collection** owned by ~~the room or the whole~~ **its containing host** --- the obvious shape, cheap, and no new node kind. **Corrected 2026-08-09: *the room or the whole* was not a fork.** A room is a whole of its contents and a whole is a host of its parts, so that was one slot asked from two directions --- see [the whole/part dissolution](#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09), which this row and [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about) reintroduced two days after it was locked. **The correction removes a phantom axis, not a candidate:** (a)-versus-(b), collection versus node, is untouched and is this row's real question. **(b) Negatives are nodes in the ludic graph**, carrying ordinary relation-kind edges like any other node. **The argument for (b) is one this plan has already made, one level up, and it is the strongest thing on the table.** [P2](#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge)'s conceptual error is *hosting creates a phantom edge*: membership expressed by **containment in a collection** rather than by an edge, leaving a link that is "not merely unlabeled but **untypeable** --- the edge could not be written into `edges` even without a kind, because one endpoint has no node to be." **A negative list is that shape exactly**, a scalar sibling of `nodes`/`edges` holding facts that cannot be edge endpoints --- so (a) recreates the phantom this plan diagnosed and set out to remove, one scale down and *after* the diagnosis. **The discriminating requirement is causal, and it is what opened the row:** absence has measurable causal effect --- a flashlight gives no light, deducible from *no battery or power source* **without knowing the flashlight's other details**. Under [P4](#retraction-dissolves-under-a-dataflow-reading-and-that-reading-is-the-ludic-native-one)'s dataflow reading, consequence propagates **along edges between nodes holding values**; a negative that is not a node cannot be an endpoint, so the cascade must consult a second structure that is not the graph --- the phantom again, now on the hot path. **[H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) clause 5 says the same from the boundary side:** ports bind any relation kind crossing a boundary, and a negative *inside* a whole with consequence *outside* it (no battery, flashlight dark, read from the room) needs an interior end for a port to bind to. A collection entry has none. **Two real costs on (b), neither fatal and both unpriced.** **(i) Accumulation, and it cuts against the initiative's own purpose.** Negatives are cheaper to create than positives --- *no* costs a sentence, *yes* costs a mint --- so they accrue faster, and every traversal, summary and LLM packet pays for them. A kitchen carrying five knives and five hundred *there is no X* nodes is **worse** reasoning context than one carrying five knives, which inverts the bounding the abstraction layer exists to provide. **(ii) A negative is not an `OBJECT#`** --- no `shortName`, no body, no placement --- and node kinds are tag-discriminated `'Character' \| 'Object'` today, so (b) needs a third tag. **It may be the first thing this initiative has found that genuinely cannot be an Object**, which *pressures* AB-12's everything-is-an-Object reframe rather than being absorbed by it. **Restated 2026-08-09 under [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), and the question is now about *licences* rather than about negatives.** **Both recorded costs move favourably.** *Accumulation* collapses --- one licence per entity whose content narrows, rather than one node per negative, and a licence **coarsens** where independent negative nodes do not. *Node-kind* becomes principled rather than awkward: a **licence** node is an ordinary modelling construct where a **negative** node was a third tag with no natural reading, so the pressure on AB-12 is real but no longer strange. **And P5 supplies a second argument for (b)** independent of P2's phantom: a licence is *manipulable* --- widened, narrowed, delegated --- and a thing that changes in play wants to be an edge endpoint. | AB-12, AB-43, AB-44, AB-21, P2, P4, P5, H3 clause 5 | **Open --- (b) strengthened; both costs cheaper under P5, and the question is now "how is a licence represented," not "how is a negative represented"** |
| **AB-43** | **What is an established negative keyed by, when its referent does not exist?** Opened 2026-08-09. **This is [AB-21](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s identity obligation in its hardest form.** *"Is there a deep fryer"* and *"is there a deep-fat fryer"* must reach the **same** negative, or the world answers inconsistently and the record does no work at all --- but there is no id to key on, because nothing exists. `stableKey` lives on `Meta::Object`, and there is no `Meta::Object`. **Candidates, none assessed:** a normalized phrase; a semantic vector (the objects lane already carries `EMBEDDING#IMPROMPTU`, so this **inherits improvisation gap 3** --- whether a `shortName`-derived vector is the right text --- in a harder form, since there is no `shortName` either); a term from a closed type vocabulary; or keying **the question** rather than the thing. **Note the closed-vocabulary trap explicitly:** an enumerated list is only correct where the vocabulary is genuinely enumerable, and *things a player might ask about* is not --- so a type-term answer needs a fallback rather than a table. **Deliberately separate from AB-42, and must not be used to decide it:** both horns need this, since a collection entry needs a key exactly as much as a node needs an identity. **A cost shared by both options cannot discriminate between them**, and folding it into the representation row would make the cheaper-looking option look cheaper than it is. **Split 2026-08-09 by [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), and half of it dissolves.** A **blanket** licence limit is keyed by **the entity it attaches to** --- an id that exists --- so the hard version of this question never arises there; the row's original framing was a category error, since a licence limit is keyed by its *holder*, not by what it denies. **What remains is the narrowing case, and it is better-posed than it was:** not *give identity to a nonentity*, which cannot be done, but *describe a closed region of licence space*, which is an ordinary predicate/description problem. Comparably hard, but no longer ill-formed --- and the closed-vocabulary trap below applies to it unchanged. | AB-21, AB-42, AB-18, AB-38, P5 | **Open for narrowing licences only --- the blanket case is dissolved by P5; question reshaped from identity to description** |
| **AB-44** | **Do all negatives carry causal force, or only some?** Opened 2026-08-09, and it is plausibly **upstream of AB-42 rather than beside it.** *There is no deep fryer* looks **inert**: nothing downstream reasons from it, and it exists only to keep the world's answers stable across repeated asking. *There is no battery* is **load-bearing**: a consequence follows from it and propagates. **This is [AB-19](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s description/mechanism split arriving in a place it was not written for**, after [C16](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c16-the-bare-table-and-the-room-that-is-just-a-mess) already used it to decide the messy room --- the plan has taken repeated arrival as weak evidence of a real joint before, and should here too. **If it holds, negatives are not one kind and AB-42 may have a per-kind answer rather than a global one:** a mechanism-negative earns a node because something depends on it; a description-negative may not, which would also bound AB-42's accumulation cost to the smaller set. **It also structures lifecycle, which is otherwise homeless.** An inert negative is a candidate for expiry --- [PS-10](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s *how does a lead die on purpose* --- while a load-bearing one **cannot** silently vanish without the cascade depending on it going wrong. **Do not settle decay for negatives before this row.** **One warning against the easy reading, on AB-19's own precedent:** *load-bearing* must not become a compile-fictional-physics judgment computed from structure. AB-19 already rejected deciding consequence deterministically rather than by an LLM step. What this row asks is whether the **representation** differs by kind --- not whether the system can decide which negatives matter. | AB-19, AB-42, AB-9, PS-10 | **Open --- newly opened; likely upstream of AB-42, and the lifecycle question is parked inside it rather than given its own row.** **Widened 2026-08-09 by [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin): the inert/load-bearing split does not cover *licence* negatives (AB-45), which are neither.** **Then narrowed the same day by [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), which is a better outcome than the widening:** if every negative is a recorded licence narrowing, this row is no longer asking *which negatives are one thing versus another* but the sharper **does closing licence for X also assert that X is absent?** Licence-closure is then **universal** and causal force **variable** --- the split survives, on a smaller and better-defined surface. **The authoring exception is what keeps the two from simply collapsing:** an author may add a deep fryer to a kitchen whose licence is closed, so closure cannot entail absence in general | 
| **AB-45** | **What licenses improvisation inside a whole --- and how is the absence of that licence recorded?** Opened 2026-08-09 from [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin). **The case, in one line:** three `PlaceSetting` wholes --- authored, minted from a coarse envelope, and recognized over five existing objects --- are **structurally identical**, and the first two may improvise a napkin while the third may not. **So licence is not derivable from the graph and must be recorded.** **The discriminator, sharper than provenance:** a whole with a coarse **intensional** description (*a place setting*, *what a tavern needs*) has open constituents; a whole that is **extensional** --- constituted by enumerating members, its name applied afterward --- licenses nothing further. **Improvisation gap 2 already states this** while describing a shortfall: *"its body is nearly empty and its member set is the content."* **What the record must not say, and this is the row's main trap:** *there is nothing else on this table* is a **completeness claim about the fiction**, and asserting it establishes a negative nobody established --- C17's closed-world defect one scale down, on the construct built to prevent it. The correct content denies the **whole's generative capacity**, not the napkin's existence: the napkin stays *not established* and remains improvisable by any other licensed route. **Consequently "blanket negative" is probably the wrong name** --- it is a licence statement, and calling it a negative invites the completeness reading --- **and naming it is part of this row.** **Not covered by the negatives cluster:** AB-43 has no referent to key (it denies an open class), AB-44's inert/load-bearing split contains neither option (it has no in-world causal consequence and is not inert --- it constrains the *system*), and AB-42 may answer it as a **property of the root node** rather than a node, which is a second independent route to that row's per-kind answer. **One collision to resolve rather than inherit:** AB-14 holds that *"`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, so provenance is invisible downstream."* **Licence is provenance-determined, so provenance cannot be fully invisible** --- this is the second dent in that claim after [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-7-the-negative-record-is-where-authored-and-improvised-truth-actually-collide-and-the-merge-stack-cannot-arbitrate-it) Finding 7, and the two should be settled together. **The intensional reading may soften it:** what must survive downstream is arguably *does this whole have an intensional description*, which is a property of the whole rather than a provenance tag --- if so the merge-layer claim holds and only the vocabulary needs widening. **Not asserted; it is the row's leading candidate.** **Reframed the same day, and the row is now secondary to [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item).** Treating licence as a **first-class graph item** rather than reasoning about it in the abstract resolves three of this row's four difficulties outright --- see P5's repair table --- and **inverts the framing recorded above**: licence is the primary construct and negatives are the shadow it casts, rather than licence being an odd third kind of negative. **The provenance collision is closed by it** (licence is carried by the whole; provenance only sets its initial value), and **the naming question is answered in substance**: *blanket negative* is the wrong name because it is a licence narrowed to zero. **What survives as this row's own work:** the exact vocabulary, and whether *licence closed for X* and *X is established absent* are one fact or two --- see P5's second open item. | AB-9, AB-14, AB-19, AB-29, AB-42, AB-43, AB-44, **P5** | **Open --- three of four difficulties resolved by P5; vocabulary and the closure-versus-absence identity remain** |
| **AB-47** | **Are flat genre-forbids the same construct as fact-derived permission narrowing, or a different kind?** Opened 2026-08-09 by [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c21-two-ways-to-say-there-are-no-firearms). *"A fantasy setting in which firearms have not been invented"* and *"a fantasy setting, without firearms"* exclude identically and behave differently on four probes --- the first is **defeasible in-fiction** (a player who researches gunpowder should succeed; that is a story), licenses near-misses, and answers *why*; the second does none of these. **The candidate framing, and it is what makes this a difference in kind rather than degree:** a bare genre-forbid is closer to a **social contract** --- an out-of-character agreement about what story is being told, in the family of lines-and-veils --- than to a fictional contribution. **Four consequences none of which the causal form shares:** it is **inviolable rather than weighted**, making it the first construct in this design that is **not a matter of degree** (which bounds **AB-46**'s reach); breaching it is **not a narrative-integrity fault** but the system doing what it was told not to do, a different failure mode with a different repair; it has **no diegetic explanation and none should be invented**; and it constrains **narration**, not only world-state --- [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item)'s licence governs what may be *minted*, this governs what may be *said*. **The unification argument, recorded because it is real and economical:** a social contract is a licence narrowed at the fractal's **root** with weight infinite and defeasibility none, and since no level is privileged a root licence is just a licence --- which absorbs three of the four consequences. **The fourth does not fit**, and the stronger objection is separate: **type separation makes the category error unrepresentable rather than forbidden.** If genre-forbids share a structure with world facts, something will eventually narrate one, improvise a reason for it, or reincorporate it as a contribution. **Weight-infinity is a rule that they must not be confused; a type distinction is an invariant** --- [C18 Finding 4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-4-the-scope-trap-and-why-it-is-structural-rather-than-a-rule-to-remember)'s move on its fourth arrival. **Settled 2026-08-09 after two reversals in one day: TWO constructs, sharing ONE enforcement point.** The movement is recorded rather than smoothed --- each turn supplied a *new* consideration rather than re-arguing an old one, which is what a balanced question looks like while it settles. **The argument that settles it is stronger than the one it was first opened on.** Draft 1 argued that type separation keeps a category error unrepresentable, **which depends on someone eventually erring**. The settling argument holds **under perfect discipline**: unify the two as *a licence narrowing with weight infinite*, and **six consumers each become a branch on whether the weight is infinite** --- the mint gate (is this overturnable by play at all?), the out-of-character response text, the escalation channel, reincorporation weighting, write authority, and the moderation signal. **Six for six, sharing only a storage shape. A sentinel that changes behaviour at every read site is not a unification but two constructs with extra steps** --- and the compromise (*one construct with a discriminating field*) collapses the same way, since a field determining every behaviour is a type distinction spelled as an enum. **The two differ concretely, not abstractly:** a genre boundary is not overturnable by any in-fiction effort and its response is *persuade other **players**, outside the fiction*, with an out-of-character channel to moderators; a weighted world fact is overturnable by effort proportional to its weight and its response is *nobody has ever done this --- it is **possible**, but a significant and long-term quest*. **Two consequences beyond the answer: AB-46 gains concrete semantics (weight is the effort required to overturn**, symmetric with *how much investment stands behind it*), and **the legibility caution below is answered rather than carried**, since both constructs now have a legible out-of-character response and the weighted one converts a refusal into a **quest hook**. ~~**Lean: two constructs.**~~ ~~**Reversed 2026-08-09** --- both arguments for two fell to one observation: a *mint-time* gate is sufficient.~~ **Draft 2's error, recorded because its shape recurs: the mint-gate finding removed *one* of two objections, and that was treated as making the case. An objection falling is not a conclusion arriving.** **What survives from it is the durable half --- one enforcement point:** **You cannot stop a player assembling a tube, powder and shot and asking for a gun --- but you can refuse to mint the abstraction, and then the system steadily refuses to reason about the assembly.** **That answers the narration objection by re-scoping rather than rebutting it:** the gate catches the **high-blast-radius** case (a minted gun, interacted with and load-bearing three sessions later) and lets through the low one (a stray NPC line about gunners) --- which is [the integrity->prevention / coherence->damping split](#the-feedback-regime-is-the-thing-to-measure-and-it-decides-which-solutions-to-look-for-2026-08-09) applied correctly, not a leak tolerated. **And the refusal is structurally silent rather than obeyed** --- an unminted abstraction produces no packet, so there is nothing for a reasoning step to comply with. **The unrepresentability argument shrank rather than fell:** of its three legs, *narrate* goes with the above, *explain* reduces to a **discriminating field** (does this narrowing carry a causal account?), and only *reincorporate* still wants weight-infinity --- from *needs a type* to *needs a field*. **What survives is a different distinction than the one argued: write authority.** A world fact changes by authoring or play; a social contract changes by **out-of-character table negotiation** --- plausibly a fourth provenance beside authored / player-driven / system-improvised, separating from *authored* only in multiplayer. ~~**Revised answer: one construct, a discriminating field, a distinct write authority.**~~ **Superseded --- see the settled answer at the head of this row.** **One caution carried forward, and one discharged.** *Carried:* blast-radius reduction **assumes a moderator eventually arrives**, which weakens as scale grows. *Discharged:* silent refusal being safe but not legible --- **answered by the two out-of-character responses above**, which are legible precisely *because* they are out-of-character, so nothing has to be explained in-world where there is no in-world reason. **One consequence deliberately left out of scope:** the gate yields **moderation instrumentation for free**, with graded *approach* detection falling out of near-miss behaviour --- see [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-7-the-gate-produces-moderation-instrumentation-for-free-and-that-may-be-the-larger-prize) Finding 7. The **table's social contract and the moderation practice around it are not this initiative's** to design. | AB-19, AB-46, P5 clauses 2/8/9, PL-1 | **Settled 2026-08-09 --- two constructs, one enforcement point.** Residual work is the write-authority question (table negotiation as a possible fourth provenance) and the out-of-character response surface, not a representation fork |
| **AB-46** | **Narrative weight: how much investment stands behind an established fact, and where is it carried?** Opened 2026-08-09 from [Narrative integrity is in service to reincorporation](#narrative-integrity-is-in-service-to-reincorporation-2026-08-09). **The requirement:** if narrative integrity serves **reincorporation of participant contributions**, then what a contradiction costs depends on **who invested in the thing contradicted** --- authored, worked-for-in-play, or improvised in passing. A tavern flipping well-stocked to poorly-stocked is near-costless when the system improvised both states and **expensive** when an author wrote it that way or players hauled the supplies in. **Weight is therefore the dial the damping rule needs**: protect high-weight commitments, let low-weight ones drift. **Where it goes:** *alongside or within the permissive structures* --- a licence record carrying not only what was established but how much stands behind it. **Why it does not fight [P5](#licence-transfer-import-is-a-copy-and-provenance-is-deliberately-not-traced-2026-08-09)'s no-cascade decision, which is the first thing to check and it passes:** weight is a **value carried on the record**, not a pointer to its source. Copy, not reference --- the same discipline licence transfer already adopted, so recording weight requires no provenance graph and no cascade. **The raw material already exists in storage:** authored (participation stack / WML), player-driven (kernel-committed step sequences and their events), system-improvised (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION`). **This row is not "how do we know" but "what scale, carried where, and consumed by whom."** **Three jobs and two directions, all landing on one scalar --- which is the row's best evidence that the quantity is real rather than an annotation invented per problem.** **(1)** How much contradicting a fact **strains** narrative integrity. **(2)** The **effort required to overturn** it --- and, [symmetrically for things not yet established, the effort required to *establish* them](#correction-a-mystery-caps-a-minimum-effort-not-a-maximum-time-2026-08-09), which is what a mystery's minimum-investment floor is. **One quantity measured forward and backward, covering the whole lifecycle rather than half of it.** **(3)** Whether a contradiction involving it may be **deferred** --- [registering a mystery is cheap only where nobody is vested](#how-fiction-actually-resolves-a-contradiction-it-registers-a-mystery-2026-08-09), so weight is what says whether *I wonder what's going on there* is an intriguing loose end or an unresolved social tension. **Concrete semantics supplied 2026-08-09 by [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-4-two-constructs-one-enforcement-point--settled-after-two-reversals-in-a-day): weight is the *effort required to overturn*.** Pleasingly symmetric with *how much investment stands behind it* --- the more that went in, the more it takes to undo --- and it gives the scale an operational reading rather than a comparative one. **It also converts refusals into hooks:** *nobody has ever done this; it is possible, but a significant and long-term quest* is a weight statement, and it is the response that distinguishes a weighted world fact from a genre boundary (**AB-47**), which no effort overturns. **Bound established by the same case:** a genre boundary is **not on this scale at all**, so weight is not the universal currency --- do not model it as one with an infinite value. **Open sub-questions:** is weight a scalar or a provenance tag; does it decay (a contribution from a year ago versus last session --- and note [PS-10](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) asks a structurally similar question for parked impulses); does player-driven outrank authored or the reverse; and does weight belong only on licence records or on any established fact. **Deliberately not designed now** --- the requirement is recorded so it is not rediscovered, and the plan has no consumer able to act on it until damping is being built. | AB-14, AB-29, AB-45, AB-47, P5, PS-4, PS-10 | **Open --- requirement recorded, deliberately undesigned; compatibility with P5's no-cascade checked and holds; semantics narrowed 2026-08-09 to *effort to overturn*, and bounded away from genre boundaries by AB-47** |
| **AB-48** | **Must the model be able to say *in* --- and about what?** **Split out of AB-33 on 2026-08-09**, on the AB-4/AB-32 precedent and for the same reason: AB-33 asks only what the representation *is*, and prices every option against what today's code would cost to change. **It is worse-formed than AB-4 was.** AB-4 at least conflated a requirements question with a representation one; AB-33 has no requirements twin at all, which is the likeliest reason it has sat *Not started* since it was opened. **This row is the twin.** **The question was already inside AB-33, filed as a consequence rather than as the question:** *"`HostRelationalEdgeKind` is `'On' \| 'Under' \| 'Against' \| 'Custom'`, so the model cannot say 'in' about anything, and AB-4 is being asked in a vocabulary with no word for its answer."* That is a requirements claim wearing a defect's clothing. **What it must collect,** in AB-32's manner: (i) must containment be **traversable** --- can a reasoning step be asked what is inside what, and get an answer by walking rather than by inspecting a field; (ii) must it be **nestable** to arbitrary depth (the *Abstraction Fractal* name asserts yes; **AB-32** already answered the composition half yes, and this asks the containment half); (iii) must it carry a **kind**, or is one unlabeled *in* sufficient --- note [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register) currently has `part-of` and nothing for plain containment, and whether those are one kind or two is this row's, not AB-4's; (iv) must a containment fact be **addressable as an edge endpoint**, which is P2's phantom-edge argument restated as a requirement. **Axis note, because AB-33 argued on none:** this is a **positioning** question in the axis frame's own terms --- *can this be recorded at all* --- and positioning is a **filter that fires once per relation kind introduced**, exactly as [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) fired it for extent and [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable) fired it for causality (*"eliminating 'no carrier anywhere'"*). **Containment is a kind being introduced and the axis has never been asked about it.** **Answer this row before AB-33**, on AB-32's stated grounds: the requirements half is answerable from the fiction with confidence and the representation half is not. **Resolved 2026-08-09, same day it was split out, and it was unasked rather than hard exactly as predicted.** **(i) Traversable: yes. (ii) Nestable: yes. (iii) Two kinds, not one --- `In` and `PartOf`, added to [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register) and to `HostRelationalEdgeKind`. (iv) Addressable as an edge endpoint: yes**, which is locked-frame clause 3's *usable as an endpoint* finally having something to be an endpoint of. **The discriminating case, and it fires the positioning axis in the frame's own terms:** a crate with a crowbar **on** it, a lid **part of** it, and treasure **in** it --- three relations between the same pair of graph positions that today collapse to one undifferentiated *is a member of the crate's graph*. That is *"eliminating 'no carrier anywhere'"*, the axis's one-firing-per-kind-introduced behaviour, arriving for containment as it did for extent ([C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)) and causality ([C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable)). **Why not `Custom`:** it requires a `relationLabel` and carries no semantics, and containment needs traversal, carry-closure and coarsening behaviour --- so this is new vocabulary, not a label. **Why two kinds and not one:** they differ on **separability** and on **intensional-versus-extensional** constitution (a crate implies a lid, not treasure); **they are explicitly *not* exclusive**, since a battery is both part of and in a flashlight, so `In` is not `PartOf`'s negation and an object may carry both. **What came with the resolution and is filed elsewhere:** the containment/spatial **family split** as a placement rule (register, second general finding), and the **licence prediction**, recorded as contested with C17 as its standing counter-case. **Two implementation obligations, both already booked rather than new:** `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer` is an **exhaustive switch** over the kind union, so each new kind must declare its transfer outcome (both are unconditional `carry`); and `EphemeraPositionRelationalEdgeData`'s `from`/`to` are both `EphemeraObjectId`, so root-incident edges need the **same** node-tag widening [locked-frame clause 3 already obligates](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) --- one widening, two motivations, do not double-book it. | AB-4, AB-32, AB-33, AB-12, relation-kind register | **Resolved 2026-08-09** --- *in* and *part of* are two non-exclusive containment kinds; family split and licence prediction spun out to the register; **AB-4's remaining fork flagged for re-examination**, since within-graph containment edges and P2 clause 3's cross-graph endpoint addressing may answer different questions rather than rival ones |

### Constraint matrix

The discipline this plan exists to enforce: **these do not close independently.** A row's answer is only provisionally valid until every row it constrains has been re-checked against it.

```text
   AB-12  (is there a privileged level at all?)  ---- may dissolve ---->  AB-4
      |                                                                  AB-2
      v
   AB-1   (root: one concept or several?)
      |
      +-----------------+------------------+
      |                 |                  |
 STRUCTURE          LIFECYCLE          CONSUMPTION
 AB-2  role         AB-5  minting      AB-9   level / projection
 AB-3  root         AB-6  decay        AB-11  operand vs step
 AB-4  level-edge   AB-10 authored     AB-7   state
 AB-8  extent       AB-14 minting      AB-15  default binding
 AB-13 DAG shape          modes        AB-19  describe vs mechanism
 AB-16 part kinds   AB-17 reabsorption AB-22  annotation
                    AB-18 recog. scope        vocab / timing
                    AB-20 failure/auth AB-23  situational
                    AB-21 identity            meaning
                    AB-24 context tier

   NEGATIVES  (added 2026-08-09, from C17)

   AB-44  (do all negatives carry causal force?)
      |
      |  if the description/mechanism split holds, AB-42 is answered
      |  per kind rather than globally --- so this closes first
      v
   AB-42  (negatives: ludic-graph nodes, or a collection beside the graph?)
      |
      +---- pressures ----> AB-12   (a negative may be the first thing
      |                              that genuinely cannot be an Object)
      |
      +---- parks lifecycle/decay inside AB-44, with PS-10

   AB-43  (what is a negative keyed by?)   NOT a dependency of AB-42:
                                           both horns need it equally,
                                           so it cannot discriminate
```

**The block above maps AB-1--AB-24 and the new negatives cluster only.** Rows AB-25--AB-41 have never been drawn into it and are carried by the coupling table and by each row's own dependency column --- stated so a reader does not read the diagram as complete.

**Three scheduling tiers (AB-14, AB-18, AB-24), each parameterizing the one above it:**

| Tier | Immediacy | Job |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **On-demand mint** | Blocking --- a pending operation cannot proceed | The referent must ground *now* |
| **Recognition** | Deferred, but promotable on demand | Did an arrangement compose into something? |
| **Context shift** | Low, ambient | Should we be watching for *different kinds* of meaning? |

**AB-14 is the lifecycle cluster's spine.** One mechanism --- improvisation --- invoked in two modes divided by **criticality**: on-demand (a pending operation is blocked) and recognition (nothing is blocked). AB-20 draws the two consequences of that axis (failure semantics, authorization), AB-21 the identity obligation it implies, AB-18 recognition's scope and the convergence of its two invocation paths, AB-17 the inverse operation, and AB-19 whether recognition is even a mint or merely AB-9 by another name.

Cross-cluster couplings that make a one-thread-at-a-time approach fail:

| Coupling | Why it bites |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-9 -> AB-5, AB-6** | If summary is a projection, minting and decay largely evaporate --- there is nothing stored to mint or to let rot. Answering AB-5 first would design machinery AB-9 may delete. |
| **AB-6 -> AB-3, AB-4** | A *derived* member set needs no root and no stored level-crossing edge; a *stored* one needs both, plus a drift-repair path. The structure cluster cannot close before this. |
| **AB-8 -> AB-4** | Framing (c) makes extent a composition case outright. Since (a) is also cheaper than first assumed, AB-8's cost is dominated by AB-4's answer rather than by any storage obstacle --- deciding AB-4 without testing (c) risks building host-spanning machinery for nothing. |
| **AB-2 -> AB-11** | If an abstraction is a node, it is already an operand and the executor may need no new vocabulary. If it is only a host or only a projection, "pull the rope" has nothing to bind to. |
| **AB-7 -> AB-1** | If state turns out to belong to a different lane entirely, example 3 leaves this initiative and AB-1's answer changes shape --- possibly from three axes to two. |
| **AB-10 -> AB-6** | An authored pattern definition is exactly what makes a *derived* member set cheap (match the pattern) instead of expensive (recognize ad hoc). |
| **AB-12 -> AB-1, AB-2, AB-4** | If no level is privileged, composition needs no new type and AB-4 becomes "what edge relates an Object to an Object at another level," not "what is a level-crossing relation." AB-1 then arbitrates only extent vs. state. Answering the structure cluster before AB-12 risks designing a type the reframe deletes. |
| **AB-14 -> AB-9** | Both minting operations are write-side, so AB-9 keeps its scope control **only if** its no-creation guardrail holds. AB-19 is where that boundary is actually contested: recognition-for-description may be indistinguishable from a projection. |
| **AB-19 -> AB-22 -> AB-18** | Superseded chain (2026-08-05). The authored-pattern-vocabulary shortcut is rejected, so AB-18's search is **not** bounded by a pattern library. It is bounded instead by AB-22's annotation vocabulary: if members carry structural-role affinities computed at mint time, an inert arrangement is cheap to skip and a causally loaded one is cheap to notice. **Design AB-22 before AB-18's cost policy** --- annotation timing determines whether the search is expensive at all. |
| **AB-14 -> AB-16, AB-17** | The minting modes cut *across* direction: on-demand minting usually runs downward and recognition upward, but neither is required to, and AB-16/AB-17 describe the same territory from the structural side. If direction and criticality turn out to be independent axes, these rows stay separate; if they co-vary, several collapse. **Narrowed 2026-08-06:** AB-16 is retired, so this interaction is now AB-14 -> AB-17 only --- and AB-16's minting-license fragment landed *on* AB-14/AB-17 rather than collapsing them. |
| **AB-20 -> AB-18, AB-19** | Authorization is *why* only recognition needs a confidence gate. Designing AB-18's trust story without that grounding invites re-deriving it as arbitrary policy --- or, worse, imposing a symmetric confidence requirement on on-demand minting, which the player's own reference already licenses. |
| **AB-21 -> AB-18** | The convergence requirement (one mint, two invocation paths) and the identity obligation (one wall, many references) are the same problem asked from two directions. A `stableKey`-shaped answer to one is probably the answer to both --- **and `Meta::Result`'s `nonCollidingPutItem` first-write-wins already supplies the mechanism**, see the `thinking` section. |
| **AB-23 -> AB-22, AB-19** | Situational meaning makes mint-time annotation a partial answer at best, and makes the member-affinity *filter* context-dependent rather than fixed. Settling AB-22's timing and vocabulary before AB-23 would design a static annotation scheme the situational answer immediately invalidates. |
| **AB-24 -> AB-18** | Tier 3 parameterizes tier 2: what recognition looks for is set by the current interpretive frame. AB-18's scope and trigger cannot be specified against an unspecified vocabulary. |
| ~~**AB-16 -> AB-8, AB-11**~~ | ~~Ordered extent and unordered parts need different carriers and different operations.~~ **Void 2026-08-06 on two counts.** The ordering premise was already wrong (**AB-26**: the structure is an adjacency graph, not a path), and AB-16 is now retired --- so neither "different carriers" nor "different operations" has a difference left to rest on. `pull` and `clear the place setting` differ as *operations*, which is the separability question now sitting on the manipulation ladder. |
| **AB-17 -> AB-5** | A minting design with no reabsorption criterion is a ratchet. The two must be designed together or the second will be retrofitted onto a shape that cannot express it. |
| **AB-13 -> AB-3** | Multiple parents mean a node sits in several closures at once, which is the doubly-reachable case `rootId`-is-recorded-not-derived already exists to handle. Whether that guard generalizes or merely rhymes is a real question. |
| **AB-44 -> AB-42** | If AB-19's description/mechanism split holds for negatives, they are **not one kind**, and the representation question gets a per-kind answer rather than a global one --- a mechanism-negative earns a node because a cascade depends on it, a description-negative may not. Deciding AB-42 globally first would either give every inert *there is no X* a node (paying AB-42's accumulation cost across the largest possible set) or deny one to a negative something genuinely propagates from. **AB-44 also bounds the cost that is AB-42's main argument against nodes**, so closing them in the wrong order prices the fork wrong. |
| **AB-42 -> AB-12** | A negative has no `shortName`, no body and no placement, so nodes-for-negatives needs a third node tag beside `'Character' \| 'Object'`. **That pressures AB-12's everything-is-an-Object reframe rather than being absorbed by it** --- possibly the first thing this initiative has found that genuinely cannot be an Object. Note the direction: AB-12 has until now only ever *dissolved* rows (AB-4, AB-2, composition); this is the first row that pushes **back** on it. |
| **AB-48 -> AB-33** | The requirements/representation split, on the AB-32 -> AB-4 precedent. Whether the model must be able to say *in* --- traversably, nestably, as an edge endpoint --- decides whether a labeled containment edge is worth building; AB-33 can then choose the representation. **Answering AB-33 first means pricing a change against current code with no statement of what the change is for**, which is the state that row sat in from 2026-08-06 to 2026-08-09. |
| **AB-48 -> relation-kind register** | Sub-question (iii) --- one containment kind or two --- adds or merges a register row, and the register's own rule is that soundness of a mechanism is a property of the kind. A plain *in* and `part-of` may coarsen differently, which is the register's question and not AB-4's. |
| **AB-33 -> AB-12** | Post-split, AB-33's residue is *do Room/Character hosts get a root node too, or do objects and rooms run two mechanisms* --- which is AB-12's Room/Feature bet, the one AB-12 says must not ride in on the Object bet. **PQ-13's one-mechanism entailment pressures this and does not settle it**; treating it as settled is the ride-in AB-12 warns against. |
| **AB-42 -> P2, P4, H3 clause 5** | Not AB rows, so recorded here rather than in the diagram. All three supply the same argument from different directions: a collection holding facts that cannot be edge endpoints is [P2's phantom](#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge) one scale down; P4's dataflow cascade propagates **between nodes**, so a non-node negative forces a second structure onto the hot path; and H3 clause 5's ports need an interior end to bind, which a collection entry does not have. **If AB-42 resolves toward the collection, all three need re-checking rather than assuming they tolerate it.** |
| **AB-43 -\|- AB-42** | **Recorded as an explicit *non*-coupling**, because the natural assumption runs the other way. Keying a negative whose referent does not exist is hard, and it is **equally** hard for both horns --- a collection entry needs a key exactly as much as a node needs an identity. **A cost shared by both options cannot discriminate between them**, and letting AB-43's difficulty attach to only one horn would make the collection look cheaper than it is. Close them independently. |

**A pattern worth promoting rather than treating as three coincidences.** AB-15 has now surfaced three times in the same shape --- **authoring supplies a default, play revises it**: the default *binding level* for unqualified reference (AB-15), the default *pattern vocabulary* for what can be recognized (AB-19), and the default *interpretive frame* a context-shift task detects deviation from (AB-24). If that generalizes, it is a single principle rather than three separate authoring questions, and it makes each of the three cheaper: the expensive open-ended inference becomes deviation-detection against an authored baseline.
---

Independent of AB-8's eventual framing: decide whether "at most one room per `OBJECT#`" was a character rule over-generalized to objects, and correct [`AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) / [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md) accordingly. Decide at the same time what `repairObjectPlacementDrift` is *for* if multi-room presence becomes legitimate --- an unwired repairer for a now-legal state is dead code with a misleading name. **Fourth site, added 2026-08-05 from [C2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c2-the-rope-in-two-rooms-refused-at-the-gate):** `evaluateComplexityPreGates`' terminal `multiPresent` error. It is the most **player-visible** of the four --- docs and an unwired repairer mislead readers, but this one refuses a command --- and reconciliation must also settle the *inconsistency* between it and the relational route's silent hostless treatment, which is a defect on its own terms whichever way multi-host resolves.
- [ ] **Phase 2. Answer every remaining row provisionally, in one pass**, in cluster order (structure, lifecycle, consumption). Provisional means: written down with its reasoning, explicitly not closed. Resist the pull to fully settle any single row here.
  - [ ] **Answer inside the [locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06), and do not re-argue it.** Its two shape clauses are fixed, so rows may assume a whole is a graph with a root node and that boundary crossings are interior-owned bindings. **They may not assume the calibration** --- that section's exclusion list names what is still open, and PQ-1's record split in particular has already reversed once. A row that quietly settles a PQ by leaning on the lock is the momentum failure the lock was written to prevent, not an economy.
  - [ ] **Tag as you go, applying the [test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) per row:** is this answer *derived from* [H1](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node), or merely *compatible* with it? Only the first earns `H1-dependent`. Tagging retroactively does not work --- the reasoning that shows which one it is exists while the answer is being written and is gone afterwards.
- [ ] **Phase 3. Consistency pass.** Walk the constraint matrix and re-check each coupling. Every row that a later answer invalidated goes back to Open with a note on what invalidated it. **Expect at least one round trip** --- if there is none, the provisional answers were probably not specific enough to conflict.
  - [ ] **The parts-and-ports back-sweep.** Re-read every open row against **locked-frame clauses 1 and 2** --- *a whole has its own graph with a root node*, and *boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns* ([Locked frame: parts and ports](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06), 2026-08-06) --- and record what falls out. **This is the retroactive half of [the dependent-set discipline](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09):** locked-frame clause 3's dependents were enumerated at lock time and locked-frame clauses 1 and 2's cannot be, because *"the reasoning that shows which one it is exists while the answer is being written and is gone afterwards."* **So this is a re-read, not a reconstruction**, and it should not produce a list claiming to be the set that was never captured. **Expect it to be productive rather than tidy-up:** the one session that swept rows against the whole/part dissolution found **six already-decided-but-unrecognized out of roughly eight examined**, three or four of them invalidated by a locked clause on the day it landed. **Named on the lock's section rather than its clause numbers** because `clause 1` is ambiguous across at least three constructs in this plan.
  - [ ] **Count the `H1-dependent` set and record the number**, per [Working hypotheses](#working-hypotheses). This is the point at which the set is finally known, and the count is the measurement the tagging exists to produce: **small** means H1 was cheap and most of the design stands without it; **large** means it is load-bearing and deserves more scrutiny than a hypothesis has had. Either result is informative; an untagged or over-tagged set produces neither.
- [ ] **Phase 4. Close the rows that the corpus can actually settle**, and explicitly park the rest with a named trigger ("revisit when X exists"), the way BD-36's character-relation widening is parked. A parked row with a trigger is a finished outcome; a parked row without one is debt.
- [ ] **Phase 5. Choose the thinnest vertical slice that exercises at least two clusters.** Not the easiest slice --- a slice touching only one cluster proves nothing about the couplings, which are the part most likely to be wrong. Two candidates, **neither a commitment**:
  - **(a) Read-side composition summary for the place setting**, exercising structure (AB-2/3/4) and consumption (AB-9) while leaving state and extent alone.
  - **(b) Convert carry closure to ephemeral-abstraction construction (AB-41), added 2026-08-07.** Retype `computeCarryClosure`'s BFS-and-bundle as construction of an H3-shaped whole (root, parts, ports), never persisted, per [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up). **The stronger first choice, for a reason distinct from (a)'s:** its acceptance bar is existing behavior, not a new design decision --- today's carry-closure test coverage must still pass unchanged, so the slice pressure-tests whole/port construction (H3 clause 1) and roles-not-kinds under a real mutation (H3 clause 2, already traced rather than newly risked) without deciding anything about decomposition triggers, apprehension, or stored-vs-derived. Optionally folds in Finding 5's port-preservation for `defer`-classified edges (H3 clause 5), still unimplemented anywhere. **Gated on AB-3's root concept landing** and on enough of P3's PQ series (chiefly the port record's shape) having a concrete-enough answer to build against --- which this slice may help settle empirically rather than needing settled first, the same relationship [P4-iteration-1](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) has to its own open questions. Directly discharges [the carry-closure retirement candidate](#shipped-code-this-initiative-would-retire).
  - [ ] Score each candidate against [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) --- **which does it unpark, and is that one ready?** Unparking is expected here rather than avoided; a slice that unparks nothing is more likely mis-aimed than cheap. Check each parked item's own prerequisites, and note that the backtrack channel is a **sibling initiative** to schedule against, not to absorb.
- [ ] **Phase 6. Build the slice, then graduate.** Falsifiable rules to [`positions/AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md); paths to [`AGENT.implementation.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.implementation.md); new vocabulary or a graduated target mental model to [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md) per its own Graduation rule. **Two obligations booked here 2026-08-07:** graduate [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register) once the kinds stop moving --- it is the artifact an implementer most needs and least likely to re-derive correctly --- and resolve [the `positionGraph` naming consequence](#the-naming-consequence-positiongraph-will-be-lying), which touches a type, a lane name and already-graduated vocabulary. **Partly pre-paid 2026-08-07:** [Wholes, parts, and ports](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) is already there as **Target**, so this phase's concepts work is to move it Target -> Shipped and add its contract clauses --- not to write it from scratch. Check the entry against what actually shipped before promoting it; a Target entry that graduates unexamined is how a durable doc acquires a claim nobody tested. If AB-3 resolved yes, this is also when `CarryClosureFragment`'s pinned note gets discharged --- in both of its locations.
- [ ] **Phase 7. Decide whether this becomes a standing ladder** (like the object-manipulation index) or stays one plan. Do not decide up front; decide once the corpus has shown how many independent initiatives are actually in here.

## Relationship to the object-manipulation ladder

Separate initiatives, real interactions. Recorded so neither plan silently assumes the other's shape.

### The dependency is asymmetric: representation unblocks reasoning

**Framing, 2026-08-05.** The reasoning layer has for some time been parking work that could not be addressed *because the right data did not exist*. A representation initiative therefore unparks some of that as a matter of course --- not coincidence, but the shape of the dependency. The ladder's rungs consume the world model; when a rung was parked for want of model, this is the initiative that supplies it.

**The inversion is the more useful half: those parks are a *specification*, not just a beneficiary list.** A rung parked for want of Feature nodes is a requirement for AB-12, written from the reasoning side before this initiative existed. **Phase 0's corpus should mine them** rather than inventing every case from scratch --- they have the advantage of being grounded in situations someone actually hit.

**But it holds for several parks, not all**, and the split matters so nobody expects the whole backlog to fall out:

| Data-blocked --- this initiative plausibly unblocks | Blocked on something else --- will not move |
| --- | --- |
| Feature look-matcher dispatch; character-inclusive catalog population (CPG-5 remainder) --- **AB-12** | **BD-24** / iteration 6, location-disambiguating modifiers --- *demand*-blocked, "no concrete case currently demands it" |
| **BD-16 (3)**'s `multiPresent` all-Error treatment --- explicitly waiting on a `Custom`-relation defer resolving into genuine multi-host co-location | **C3** / `MultipleCommands` policy --- same posture; no confirmed real-world example remains |
| Coyote's genre-bounded reasoning context --- **AB-19**; bounded by genre because there is no assembly-level structure to bound it with | **BD-18** / backtrack channel --- *architecture*-blocked: addressable resume points, bounded retry, cost policy |
| Situational meaning and the unpopulated mark-state axis --- **AB-23** | Iteration 2's remaining fallback steps --- prompt and calibration work |

| Touchpoint | Direction | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Referent resolution** | Ladder -> here | If abstractions are referrable ("clear the place setting"), Identify/Grounding must produce them as candidates. The already-tracked [character-inclusive catalog population](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) gap is the same shape of problem, and the node-model guidance for that scan (additive union getter, **not** a collapsed typed surface) applies here too. |
| **Synthesize instruction language** | Both | AB-11. The executor's ordered step sequence is where an abstraction-aware operation would live. BD-27/29/30's phase-stratified worklist and shared expansion environment are the machinery a level-crossing expansion would extend. |
| **Carry closure** | Here -> ladder | AB-3. If the graph gains a root, `CarryClosureFragment` collapses into it, which changes types the ladder's Expansion work owns. |
| **Backtrack (iteration 4)** | Ladder -> here | If an LLM can propose an abstraction (AB-5), a wrong proposal needs the same structured-critique channel BD-18 designs, not a silent guess. |
| **Reposition narration** | Adjacent | [`AGENT.relationalNarration.planning.md`](../../AGENT.relationalNarration.planning.md)'s Phase 1 asks what a reposition op *is*. A composition change ("the place setting came apart") is plausibly a fourth narration family. Do not pre-empt it; note the overlap when Phase 5 picks a slice. |
| **Separability and merger** | **Here -> ladder (handed over 2026-08-06)** | The first thing this initiative has *given* the ladder rather than asked of it. Cutting a rope makes two ropes; pulling a battery does not make two flashlights --- a difference in what a *divide* operation yields, so it is manipulation vocabulary, not representation. Left over from the retired **AB-16** and recorded on the ladder's [Deferred, not rung-sized](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) list. **Gated on this initiative:** the fractal has to settle before it is clear *how* separation and merger can happen, and only then is there a planning process worth designing. **AB-31** (fission) is the row on this side that will produce the answer. |
| **Features** | Constraint | [`positionGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/AGENT.md#known-limitation-deferred) records that Features are deliberately **narrower** participants than Objects --- never a relation subject, never membership-moved, more like a room's walls than its contents. An abstraction design must not assume a Feature node would inherit Object capabilities. |

---

## Discussion record

**Moved 2026-08-07 to [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md), unchanged.** It is provenance --- *why* the rows are shaped the way they are --- and is not needed to know what to do next, which is exactly the profile that should not sit in the middle of a working plan. **It was moved rather than trimmed:** this plan records supersessions instead of erasing them, and compaction would violate that discipline.

New entries go in that file, newest last, in the same style.

## Verification

No code slice exists yet. Until Phase 6, verification is that the baseline still passes and that no durable doc has acquired normative text for unbuilt behavior:

```bash
cd lambda/ephemera && npm run test -- --watchAll=false \
  dataSource/positions/ \
  dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/
```

Documentation checks, from the repo root. **This plan is now three files**, so anchor checking must run across all of them and resolve cross-file targets:

```bash
git diff --stat -- lambda/ packages/          # expect only AGENT.concepts.md
git diff --name-only -- '*/AGENT.contract.md' # expect EMPTY until Phase 6
```

- Every `](#anchor)` resolves **within its own file**, and every `](Other.md#anchor)` resolves in that file. The three files cross-link heavily and a rename breaks links silently.
- `AGENT.contract.md` acquires nothing before Phase 6. The concepts entry is **Target**-marked and carries its exclusion list; a concepts edit that drops either is the anti-pattern arriving by the side door.

Phase 6 adds its own commands here.

| **AB-0 split and its naming half locked: *Abstraction Fractal*** --- settled not by achieving scale-invariance but because the commitment became **corpus-grounded** (C6: not a tree; C11: no privileged level) and because a provisional label on a load-bearing term is the "hold 1a loosely" failure waiting to happen; locked **with its negative statement** (no `AbstractionFractal` type --- the name reads like a noun while denying there is anything to type) and **three departures** (DAG not tree; finite depth, base case = PQ-13; aspirational above the room, with the visibility hazard restated); graduated to `AGENT.concepts.md` with the departures **in full**; **AB-38 opened** for the Improvisation mechanism/trigger collision rather than burying it in a resolved row | Done (2026-08-07) |
| **Reasoning axis corrected to *no silent omission*** --- "lossless" overstated within hours of being written; the apprehension/reasoning conflict narrowed (they **agree** on scale and conflict only on member omission) and a candidate resolution recorded; **coarsening found to name two operations** with opposite silence requirements; **ports gain a developed argument on a third axis** (the summarization seam) where both prior arguments were positioning and mutation | Done (2026-08-07) |
| **C7 continued as the corpus's first two-axis case** --- the nesting question (flat spans vs a nested `thread-span` whole) decided on **reasoning alone**, positioning having gone quiet; the snag-ports-through-the-span mechanism identified as structural and the curated summary as question-relative and softer; competitor rescuable only via AB-36, i.e. an epicycle; **AB-37 opened** for the summary's upkeep | Done (2026-08-07) |
